# api decision log

> Why api is built the way it is. Each entry: what was decided, why, what was rejected, and in its tail "— *Consequence:*" the files, by their paths, that a change of it edits.
>
> **Rule:** do not propose again what was rejected without new facts. If a decision has to change, ask the author and rewrite its entry in its place, and every entry the change touches: an entry is its text in force — what was decided, why, what was rejected and what a change of it edits —, git keeps the old text, and the commit says why. An entry's number stands in its heading alone: an entry cites no other entry and no section, says in words what it rests on, and carries no date of who decided it and no amendment; a claim about the outside world cites its one record of [sources.md](sources.md) or [measurements.md](measurements.md).
>
> The repository was set up 2026-10-19 from the template of `spec` v0.1.0. How it comes to differ from the template — tools in the devcontainer, rules in RUN.md — is recorded here as a decision. Next number — R102.
>
> Open questions that concern several repositories — О-, П-, Т- — are in the registers of `spec`, in `spec/docs/product.md`, `spec/docs/privacy.md` and `spec/docs/repositories.md`. What is still to decide in the component itself is in [architecture.md](architecture.md).

## The `spec` tag

**R01. `api` takes `spec` as the git submodule `spec/` at an exact tag, which a move to a newer tag raises in a commit of its own. What came from the template is as in the template of that tag, a link into `spec` names the tag, and the documents are checked by `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` of the submodule.**
- The documents check is a required check of every pull request: the shared workflow [`documents.yml`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/.github/blob/ff2a359/.github/workflows/documents.yml) of `.github` builds the stage `toolchain` from the caller's `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` and runs `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` in it. The check reads the links, this log, the tables В and Д and the citations of `spec` by the submodule at its tag.
- The devcontainer is the one the recipe of `spec` at the tag builds for a repository of the kind `go`; the name of the container and of the network, `postStartCommand` and the Go extension are its own.
- An example of a citation is written in inline code, so that the check does not take it for a citation.

— *Why:* the check that holds every merge runs the recipe of the submodule's `spec`, so the documents are read by the same `spec` as the one they rely on: a link to a part of `spec` that is not at the tag, or a gap in the log, does not pass unnoticed. A tag is immutable, and a move to a newer one is a change seen in review.

— *Rejected:*
- carrying over the changes of the devcontainer at a move selectively: the container of `api` is the container of `spec`, and a difference would have to be recorded as a decision with a reason of its own, and there is none;
- examples of citations in guillemets: the check reads a number in guillemets as a citation and checks it against the log.

— *Consequence:* `spec/`; `.gitmodules`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `.devcontainer/`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

**R02. Go code takes `spec` as the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` and the code of the API contracts as its nested module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts`, both at the tag of the submodule `spec/`. One move to a tag raises the submodule and both modules together, and Dependabot raises neither module.**
- The nested module has its own tag, `contracts/vX.Y.Z`, of the same number as the tag of `spec`.
- `api` reads modules and packages through `module-kit`, and the open view of a package by the types of `spec`, `types/formatv0/open`, not by copies of its own. While the module format is v0, the platform reads only its current version, so `api` and `app` move to a new version of the format together.

— *Why:* a copy of the types, or a module at another tag than the submodule, would let the code read a format and a contract other than the documents at the submodule's tag describe. Dependabot would raise a module apart from the submodule.

— *Rejected:*
- copies of the types of the format and of the contracts in `api`: two homes of one format, which drift apart;
- the modules `spec` and `contracts` under Dependabot `gomod`, as the other Go modules are: it would raise them apart from the submodule.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `spec/`.

## Sign-in and permissions

**R03. The sign-in service is Firebase Auth, with sign-in through Google alone. `api` checks the ID token with the Admin SDK for Go behind a narrow port, on every request with revocation. Without a token, only the files of the package's device part and the four open views of the package: these are the content of modules, not a learner's data. A fresh sign-in is an `auth_time` no older than 5 minutes.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the sign-in service is Firebase Auth. The dev project of `api` is on the free Spark plan, without Identity Platform: that is "an optional upgrade", its capabilities — SAML and OpenID Connect, blocking functions, user activity and audit logs, multi-tenancy, an SLA — are not needed by the first slice, and on Spark it sets a limit of 3,000 daily active users (sources 2.1). The platform's production project is on Firebase Authentication with Identity Platform, on Blaze, where that limit does not hold, and `infra` holds its sign-in settings by code: the authorized domains `<project>.firebaseapp.com` and `<project>.web.app`, sign-in only by the Google popup, email, phone and anonymous sign-in off, the activity log off, and only `api` deletes a user;
  - the sign-in method is Google alone. Sign-in through Apple is the open question Т-41 of `spec`, which reads the membership's price and terms from Apple when it is decided, and `api` takes no provider of sign-in that `spec` has not decided: Apple comes by a change of this entry, with what the port and [build.md](build.md) need of a new provider — Д3. There is no email and password, no email links and no phone: the sign-in service would hold a password, an email address or a number, while the adult's contacts are only those without which sign-in does not work, and `api` sends no emails;
  - the token check is the Admin SDK for Go, `firebase.google.com/go/v4`, as in canvas-api. `VerifyIDToken` checks only the signature, by Google's keys from the cache, and `VerifyIDTokenAndCheckRevoked` also the revocation, by a network call on every check (sources 2.1). `api` checks every request with a token with revocation: after revocation an ID token lives for up to an hour more, and an old token from another device must neither create the account again, nor read a learner's data, nor spend the quota;
  - the port: `api` calls the sign-in service through three operations — check a token, which gives the UID of the sign-in service and `auth_time`, or on a refusal its class: "expired", "revoked", "no such user", "disabled" or "failure"; remove a user, which revokes the tokens and deletes the user; list the users. `VerifyIDTokenAndCheckRevoked` gives no token on any refusal, so `api` reads the UID of a revoked token and of a deleted user's token by `VerifyIDToken`, by Google's keys from the cache, without `GetUser`, and does not wrap the errors of the SDK before its helpers `Is…`, which compare the type (sources 11.1). A second parsing of the signature happens only on these two paths, and the comparison of revocation stays the code of the SDK, not a copy of it in `api`. The UID lies only in the account's record, and the subject of permissions is the account ID of `api`: another sign-in service, for example at self-hosting, changes the adapter, not the permissions and the data. The first slice has no shared OIDC adapter — Д4;
  - without a token `api` gives the files of the package's device part — the media files, `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` — and the open views of the package by its digest: the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps. These are the content of modules, not a learner's data, and the official modules lie open in the releases of `modules` anyway. `app` takes a picture by `fetch`, as a `Blob` for the canvas, and `api` on its origin answers with CORS on all paths and all responses, without a token too. A withdrawn version `api` does not serve all the same. A lesson, a task and everything about a profile — only with a token. With the modules of third-party authors this rule is reviewed together with their licenses (О-10);
  - a fresh sign-in is the `auth_time` of a token checked with revocation, no older than 5 minutes, for deleting a profile, deleting an account and changing a forgotten PIN; export does not wait for it. A user of the sign-in service is removed after the database transaction and the tuples of OpenFGA, and what did not go through the operator's job completes;
  - the first app signs in through a dev project of Firebase on the free Spark plan, through Google: the token's path is the same as at a deployment, with a signature, and the devcontainer does not change. The adapter is tested with the real SDK against a stand-in Google over TLS. The key of the service account is the secret `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS` outside the working tree, only of the type `service_account` and only outside a deployment; in Cloud Run — the service's credentials, without a key;
  - the service account of `api` gets the role `signInUsers` — `firebaseauth.users.get`, `.update` and `.delete` — exactly for the four calls of Identity Toolkit that the Admin SDK makes: `accounts:lookup` — the check of an ID token's revocation, `accounts:batchGet` — listing the users, `accounts:update` — revoking the tokens, and `accounts:delete` — deletion (sources 2.5). [build.md](build.md) names them and what `api` expects of the sign-in settings, and a new call of `api` comes with a row «в реестр spec» before the release that makes it: without the role's permission it will be refused in production.
- **What the sign-in service holds:** the account owner's sign-in method, for Google their Google account. Firebase Auth processes data only in the USA, the platform's data region, keeps IP addresses from its logs for a few weeks, and removes the rest about a user from its production and backup systems within 180 days after deletion (sources 2.1). `api` neither reads nor stores the email from the token, as in canvas-api.

— *Why:* Firebase Auth already worked in canvas-api with the same Admin SDK for Go. Firebase's price table marks sign-in without Identity Platform — "Other Authentication services" — at the free Spark plan too, and the limit of 50 thousand monthly active users stands at the upgrade (sources 2.1). Deleting a user, revoking tokens and `auth_time` are what deletion by a fresh sign-in needs. canvas-api checked with revocation on all requests but its hot path, the issuing of Centrifugo tokens; `api` has no real-time channel (Т-25), and its most frequent requests, for the package's files, carry no token.

— *Rejected:*
- comparing sign-in services without a preference — Identity Platform, Auth0, Clerk, Zitadel Cloud: Firebase Auth was taken for having worked in canvas-api;
- sign-in through Apple now: whether and when the membership of the Apple Developer Program is paid is decided by Т-41, not by `api`;
- email and password, email links, phone — superfluous contacts of the adult;
- Identity Platform on the dev project: the limit of the Spark plan, and its capabilities are not needed by the first slice;
- a check without revocation on all requests: the token of a deleted account would live for up to an hour more;
- the files and the open views of the package by token: they protect no learner's data, and a request with `Authorization` is no simple request and goes with a preflight, whose cache is by the exact address, so on its origin every file would get a preflight of its own; a `fetch` of a file and a Connect GET of an open view without headers go without a preflight and through the HTTP cache (sources 10.2);
- the Firebase Auth emulator, for the first app or for the tests: it needs `firebase-tools` and Java 21 or newer, a difference of the devcontainer from the container of `spec`, and it issues tokens without a signature, so the path of the check would differ from the deployment (sources 2.1); in its mode `VerifyIDToken` checks revocation too, so the UID of a revoked token cannot be read through it (sources 2.5).

— *Consequence:* `internal/authn/`; `internal/server/`; `internal/config/`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R04. Permissions are OpenFGA, embedded as a library in the process of `api`. The subject of permissions is the account: the token always belongs to the owner, and a learner's profile is a parameter of the request. The server distinguishes kinds of actions, not people. The PIN is a barrier of the interface in `app`, and `api` holds its hash. Access to the closed slice is by one-time invite codes.**
- **The OpenFGA model:**
  - the types are `user` (an account of `api` by its ID), `learner` (a learner's profile) and `classroom`. One account can hold the owner's own profile as a learner and the profiles of the owner's children, and the relation `owner` to a learner's profile is held for every profile of the account. The rights are `can_manage`, `can_learn` and `can_view`;
  - the model describes teachers and the class in advance, and the first slice writes no tuples for them, as in canvas-api; a teacher bound to a class will get `can_view` at once;
  - attempts, lessons and everything else about a profile are not objects of OpenFGA. Their rows in the database carry the profile ID: `api` checks the right to the profile and reads the row with this ID. There is one tuple per profile, not one per attempt, as there was with the canvas in canvas-api.
- **How OpenFGA works:**
  - as a library in the process of `api`, not as a sidecar as in canvas-api: the OpenFGA project documents embedding (sources 2.3), a separate process is only where it is needed, and the first app works in the devcontainer without Docker;
  - the storage is Postgres: the database `openfga` in the same Neon project as the database of `api`, with the same region and history window. It is a second database of `api`, and export, deletion and backups cover it too;
  - the command `api migrate` runs the storage's migrations, the files of OpenFGA through goose under a lock, and applies the model, under its own lock, together with the store of `api`. At start `serve` only finds the store and among its models the embedded one, and does not start if it is not there;
  - a tuple is written in the database transaction before `COMMIT`, and "dangling" tuples of profiles that are not in the database are cleaned up by the operator's daily job. A dangling tuple gives no rights: there is no row of the profile, and a request by it gets "no such profile". OpenFGA does not clean up its `changelog` itself (sources 2.3), and the same job deletes its records older than a day, as in canvas-api: a record lives no longer than two days;
  - the version of OpenFGA is exact, in `go.mod`, the license is Apache-2.0.
- **The account** holds the UID of the sign-in service, the owner's statement that they are an adult, with its date, the binding of an invite code, the PIN's hash and the counter of its attempts; the OpenFGA store holds the tuples "account — profile".
- **The actions of an adult and a learner.** On a shared device there is one token, and the server does not know who holds the device. So it distinguishes kinds of actions:
  - learning requests — an attempt, a submission, a hint, a lesson — carry the profile ID and require `can_learn`;
  - an adult's actions on a profile — the profile, consent, settings — require `can_manage`; the owner profile is deleted the same way as a child's profile, and the account does not delete its last profile;
  - actions on the account — the PIN, an invite code, a child consent while there is no profile yet — concern the account from the token;
  - irreversible actions — deleting a profile, deleting an account and changing a forgotten PIN — also require a fresh sign-in; export does not.
- **The PIN** is a barrier of the interface, not a security boundary, as in canvas-api. `app` keeps the section for adults behind it, and when `app` asks for the PIN is decided by `app`. The PIN's hash is argon2id, the PIN is 4–6 digits, no more than 5 failed attempts in 15 minutes; `api` keeps the hash, the counter and the lock time in the account. So the PIN is one for all the account's devices, and changing the device does not reset the lock. A forgotten PIN is changed by a fresh sign-in.
- **The owner profile on a shared device:** on an account that has a profile of another category, `app` opens the owner profile by the same PIN as the section for adults, at every opening. An own key lies on the device only if the key's owner decided so on this device, and otherwise in the memory of the tab while its profile is open; on the server, only in the request's memory. The key and its provider come with every request of the owner profile that calls AI, and `api` accepts them only with a profile of the category `adult` (О-4): this the server checks itself.
- **The checks before a learner's data**, in the order [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists. First the token's class and the mark "being deleted": for a marked account any request, both creating an account and redeeming a code, gets "account gone". On every request — the token, access to the closed slice and the owner's statement that they are an adult. A request with a profile ID first checks the right in OpenFGA and only then reads anything by this ID: another account's profile and a profile that does not exist get one and the same refusal. Then — consent, the AI route by the age category and the quota, and a fresh sign-in last, after the right. Creating a profile checks the country against the list until the DPIA, and the consent. Every write transaction under an account takes its row `FOR KEY SHARE` and checks the mark, and the operator's command that confirms a consent refuses a marked account.
- **Access to the closed slice** — one-time invite codes, as in canvas-api. The operator creates them by a command of `api`, the database holds only the SHA-256 of a code, at the first sign-in the code is bound to the account, and `api` does not read the email. Before access `api` creates neither profiles nor learners' data. The first app takes the same path: the author creates a code for themselves by the operator's command.

— *Why:* OpenFGA, as in canvas-api, where it was kept for readiness for B2B: teachers need permissions in the form "who got access to what and in what role", and the OpenFGA model expresses them by relations. An embedded library, not a sidecar, by facts that canvas-api did not have: the OpenFGA project itself gives a path of embedding, a separate process is only where it is needed, and the first app runs without Docker. The storage's migrations go through goose, not `RunMigrations`, whose package brings into the binary a MySQL driver under MPL-2.0 (sources 2.4). The subject is the account, as in canvas-api: a child gets no credentials of their own. The PIN's hash is in `api`, so that the PIN is common to the account's devices. Invite codes, as in canvas-api: with them no email is needed, Apple's "Hide My Email" does not get in their way, and codes are not personal data.

— *Rejected:*
- permissions by code over the database — a table "subject — relation — object" with one relation "the account owns the profile": it had for it a separate process only where it is needed, the first app without Docker and without a deployment, and teachers only at stage 5, but a model ready for teachers matters more than a second store. Do not propose it without new facts;
- OpenFGA as a sidecar: it is a second process, and locally an OpenFGA binary or Docker in the devcontainer;
- `RunMigrations` for the storage's migrations: the MySQL driver under MPL-2.0 in the binary;
- an OpenFGA object per attempt, like the canvas in canvas-api: a double write at every start of an attempt, though the rights to an attempt do not differ from the rights to the profile;
- `api` checks the PIN and issues a short confirmation for an adult's actions: the owner's token lies on the device all the same, and this will not become a security boundary, while there will be more code and state;
- the owner profile and an adult's actions only by a fresh sign-in: on a shared tablet every switch would become a new sign-in through Google;
- the PIN only on the device: each device would have its own PIN, and changing the device would reset the lock;
- access by an account ID that the person passes to the operator: the ID leaves the platform, and the person has more steps; a list of emails or of their hashes, which canvas-api rejected too.

— *Consequence:* `internal/authz/`; `internal/pin/`; `internal/argon/`; `internal/invite/`; `internal/server/`; `internal/store/`; `cmd/api/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Database and learner model

**R05. The database is Neon Postgres: one project, holding the database of `api` and the OpenFGA store, in `aws-us-east-1`, beside Cloud Run in `us-east4`, one place for all learners. The deployment is on the Launch plan with a history window of a day, and the first app on a dev project on the Free plan. A profile without activity and an account without requests are deleted after 12 months, and the words of hints live until the end of the attempt. The honest period of the backups is Neon's history window.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the database is Neon Postgres, as in canvas-api. One project holds the database of `api` and the database `openfga`, the OpenFGA store; export, deletion and backups cover both;
  - the region is the USA, one place for all learners: Cloud Run and everything `infra` keeps in a region is in `us-east4`, and Neon in `aws-us-east-1`. Neon works only in AWS and no longer creates projects in Azure (sources 3.1), so the region of the database is a Neon region near the region of Cloud Run, the pair that canvas-api had too. The dev project of `api` is in `aws-us-east-1` as well;
  - connections are only over TLS (sources 3.1). The migrations of both databases are the operator's job;
  - the deployment is on the Launch plan with the history window of a day, as Launch has by default; Neon estimates the typical spending on Launch at $15 a month (sources 3.1). The first app is a dev project of Neon on the Free plan. Its connection string lies outside the repository, not in the folder `/workspaces/api`, and the devcontainer does not change;
  - the backups are Neon's history window: within it the database can be restored to any moment, and what was deleted lives in the history until it leaves the window. On Free the window is 6 hours, on Launch from a day to 7 (sources 3.1). `api` takes no snapshots of the database: a snapshot would hold what was deleted even after the window. The honest period within which what was deleted leaves Neon's history is a day;
  - the tests of the code with a database run `embedded-postgres` v2, license MIT: a dependency of the tests only, which does not go into the build, at an exact version in `go.mod`. The library downloads Postgres from the releases of `theseus-rs/postgresql-binaries` and checks it against the SHA-256 sums of its manifest, which `go.sum` holds; a downloaded supervisor it checks against `checksums.txt` of the same release at run time, a sum `go.sum` does not hold (sources 3.4), so `api` builds the supervisor from the module's source code and passes it through `Config.Supervisor` or `EP_SUPERVISOR`;
  - what lies in the database, where, for how long and what goes into the export is the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md). The export carries everything about the learner and the account owner except secrets and service fields: the PIN's hash, the counter and the lock, the UID of the sign-in service, the binding of the invite code and the attempt's seed. The date of the last request with the account's token goes into the export: it is data about the owner, not a service field;
  - the connected modules are the table `connected_modules`: the profile ID, the module ID and the time of connection, without a version, and the key is the pair of profile and module. They, the attempts, the lesson runs, the evidence log, the state cache and the engine choices go with the profile by a foreign key, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a profile and an account and `api sweep` need nothing of their own for them;
  - the periods: attempts, observations, the evidence log and the engine choices live while the profile lives, and a profile without activity for 12 months is deleted. Beyond that:
    - the words of hints live until the end of the attempt and are deleted when the attempt is closed, in the same transaction: only the ladder of the same task needs them. After that, what remains of a hint is the rung's number and the source — the model or a fallback text;
    - an essay — an attempt of a task with a rubric — keeps neither what was read nor the words of feedback, and has no observation row. What was read of another attempt lives while the profile lives, because the verdict is repeated by it, and a rubric has no verdict to repeat; what was read of an essay is its whole text, and storing it would keep the essay on the server without the owner's choice, while artifacts reach the server only by that choice. The words of feedback do not live even until the end of the attempt: a rubric has no ladder, which is what needs the words of hints;
    - an account that nobody signed in to for 12 months is deleted with its profiles and with the user at the sign-in service. "Nobody signed in" `api` reads as "there were no requests with the account's token": `auth_time` changes only at a new sign-in, and the sign-in service refreshes the token itself, so by `auth_time` the account of an owner who learns every day but has not signed in again for a year would be deleted. Of the consents, a child consent leaves a minimal record without the account and profile IDs, and an adult's consent is deleted whole;
    - the `changelog` of OpenFGA lives no longer than two days: the daily job deletes the records older than a day.

    The deletion is done by the operator's daily retention job, by the same path as deletion on request, only without a fresh sign-in, and it checks the period under the row's lock: otherwise an owner who came back the same day would lose the account. It also completes the deletions of accounts whose steps after the transaction did not go through, and deletes the users of the sign-in service without an account of `api` who for 30 days have not signed in and not refreshed their token. `api` sends no emails, so the period is named by the text of the consent, as in canvas-api.
- **What Neon holds:** the processor is Neon, a part of Databricks. Its processor agreement is Neon's addendum to the Databricks DPA, and AWS is a subprocessor; the addendum says nothing about children's data (sources 3.1). Whether it is enough before the first child is the lawyer's question, together with the parental consent and the agreements with AI providers.

— *Why:* Neon already worked in canvas-api, and it scales to zero, as the platform asks of its services: compute goes to sleep after 5 minutes idle and wakes up in a few hundred milliseconds (sources 3.1). The OpenFGA store in the same project, as in canvas-api: one database for backups, deletion and the region. A dev project for the first app, for the same reason as the dev project of Firebase: the path is the same as at a deployment, and the devcontainer does not change. A history window of a day: what was deleted leaves the history within a day. `embedded-postgres` runs a real Postgres in the tests, locally and in CI alike, with neither Docker nor a secret. An adult does not see the words of hints — they see progress and the repeating traps —, and the platform collects a minimum of data. An account without requests would keep the UID, the PIN's hash and the owner's statement to no use at all.

— *Rejected:*
- Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL — in one cloud with Cloud Run and with the regions of Google Cloud, but an instance is billed every second while it runs, and it can be stopped only by hand, by the activation policy NEVER, so there is no scaling to zero on idle; free is only a trial instance for 30 days (sources 3.2);
- the Free plan for the deployment: when its 100 CU-hours a month run out, compute stops until the next month, and with it `api` (sources 3.1); a window of 7 days: what was deleted would live in the history for a week;
- Postgres in the devcontainer for the first app: a difference from the container of `spec` that would have to be recorded as a decision, and the path would differ from the deployment; the container has no Docker and `docker compose`, which canvas-api had;
- for the tests, a service container of Postgres in CI — without Docker in the devcontainer the tests with a database would not run locally —, and a Neon branch: it needs the network and a secret in CI, and a pull request from a fork gets no secrets;
- snapshots of the database beyond the history window: what was deleted would live in them longer than the honest period says;
- the words of hints while the profile lives: nobody reads them after the attempt;
- keeping what was read for a shorter time than the profile lives: a verdict is repeated by the observation without a time limit, and a short period would need a change of `spec`;
- an account until its owner deletes it: without sign-ins it would hold the owner's data indefinitely.

— *Consequence:* `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/deletion/`; `internal/export/`; `internal/config/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/build.md`.

**R06. The learner model lives in the database of `api` as an append-only log of evidence, and the state of the skills is a cache marked with the version of `learner-model`, the pinned release of `modules` and the window of format versions. An engine choice stores everything a repeat of it by the log needs.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the learner model is in the database of `api`: several devices and several learners of the account work with it, the subscription too, and the engine computes on the server (Т-10). The export is in an open format: the log, the current state and the choices with their reason codes;
  - evidence — one per finished attempt, with two exceptions: an attempt of a task with a rubric gives none (О-17), and neither does an attempt that the learner's leaving or the period without actions closed with no wrong answers. Such an attempt stays as a row with its close reason, and one left or abandoned with wrong answers has the outcome "left unfinished". What is in evidence and how much it weighs is decided by `learner-model`, and `api` stores its type as it is, with columns for itself: the profile ID, the attempt ID, the package digest, the module, the time and the sequence number. Evidence holds no content;
  - the log is only appended to, and the database holds it so: a trigger refuses any `UPDATE` of an `evidence` row, its `DELETE` on its own, outside the cascade of a profile's deletion, and `TRUNCATE`, so the rows go only with the profile, whole — on request or by period. The evidence's number in the profile's log is the primary key `(profile_id, seq)`, and evidence is one per attempt: `attempt_id` is unique. Beside the evidence of attempts, the log holds the adult's corrections of a module's level, rows of no attempt under an ID of their own; a correction is folded whatever its package;
  - evidence on a version that the index marked `wrong_key` is cut off by a filter at recomputation, which cuts off the evidence of attempts alone; the transfer by `retired_skills` also goes at recomputation, and the rows stay;
  - the cache is a row `skill_states` per profile: the state, its mark — the version of `learner-model`, the pinned release of `modules` and the window of format versions — and the number of the last evidence taken into account. The current versions of the modules, by whose graphs the transfer by `retired_skills` goes, depend on both the release and the window, so the window is in the mark too: were it to change without a change of the release, at a move of `api` to a new line of `spec`, the cache would go stale unnoticed. If the mark does not match the one on which `api` works now, the state is recomputed from the log. New evidence is appended to the cache without recomputing the whole log, and `learner-model` must give by appending the same state as by recomputation: the evidence of an attempt begun on a previous version of a module and ended after a move names the skills of the previous version, and appending transfers it the same way as recomputation;
  - the cache row is created with the profile, in the profile's transaction, empty, with the number 0, so that the first evidence of a profile also has something to lock; while a deployment is going on, the previous image creates profiles without it, so the code that locks it first creates it if it is not there (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). The evidence and the cache are written in one transaction, under the lock of the cache row, and the evidence's number is the next after the last in the profile's log, not after the number in the cache. If the mark matches and the log has evidence after the one taken into account, `api` first appends this tail to the cache in order, with the transfer, and then gives the state or appends the new evidence — at reading and at appending alike, or the new evidence would jump over the tail. While a deployment is going on, the previous image takes the same lock and does not overwrite a mark newer than its own, by the release or the window: it computes the state for the answer without writing it;
  - evidence is written by the catch-up of owed evidence, the package `internal/model` over `learner-model`: under the lock of the cache row one function appends the evidence of all the closed attempts of the profile that owe it and whose package the image holds, in the order of closing. The closing in a request calls it in the same transaction, the skill map and progress before reading, and the step `evidence` of the job `api sweep`, so the attempts that the job, the trigger or the previous image closed get evidence too. The evidence of a version withdrawn `wrong_key` is written to the log but not appended to the cache, so the appended matches the recomputed. An attempt whose package left the image gets no evidence — Д27;
  - an engine choice is a row of `engine_choices`: the reason code, from the closed list `ReasonCode`; the skills it gave and the package digest on which it was made; the skill chosen on the skill map, which holds until the learner asks for something else, so the record carries it into the next one; the profile's age category and grade at the time, the grade being what the starting point of modules without evidence is computed by; whether the pool was limited to tasks checked without AI; the versions of `engine` and `learner-model`, the release of `modules` and the window of format versions; the number of the last evidence; the digests of all the graphs and, of them, of the engine's modules — connected and suiting the learner; and its own time. There is no seed field: the engine takes no randomness, which the version of `engine` in the record shows;
  - the time of the choice restores the rest of its input: lesson runs — their start, reason and end, once recorded, do not change, and which segments had ended by then the close times of the attempts tell —, and the closed attempts of tasks with a rubric that the engine gets, whose close reason and close time do not change either: they give no evidence, and without them the engine could not keep the author's order for skills with a rubric. The summary of issues the engine gets beside the model `api` assembles from the records of choices and attempts before the time of the choice rather than stores, and a repeat assembles it again; the time a skill was given is by the skills a record holds, so the summary needs no package that left the image.

— *Why:* the database, not the device: several devices and learners of an account share the model, and the engine computes on the server. The log: a change of the algorithm recomputes the state from the log rather than carrying the old one over, an engine choice is repeated by the log, and by it too the model is recomputed without the evidence `wrong_key`. The cache, so that an engine choice after each attempt and the skill map do not recompute the log from the start, and its mark says when it is stale. The number after the last in the log and the tail before new evidence: otherwise during a deployment two writers would take one number, and evidence that the previous image wrote would not get into the cache under the new mark until the next full recomputation. Without the fields of a choice, its repeat by the log would not know which prefix of the log to take, which withdrawal marks were in force, from which pool the engine chose and with what input; they add no learner's data, since `api` has the category, the grade and the connections already. A left attempt with wrong answers is named "left unfinished", so that "not decided" does not mean two different things.

— *Rejected:*
- a family file in Google Drive, as in mathtrail-standalone: there the file was the truth, but writes were unconditional — "Whoever uploads last wins, silently" —, and an emptied Drive bin erased a profile without a copy (sources 3.3); also `api` would need access to the owner's Drive at every engine choice;
- the device: each device would have its own model, and the engine on the server would not see it; computing on the device is needed no earlier than stage 5 (Т-10);
- only the state, like the summary by topics and the window of the last 20 answers in mathtrail-standalone: a choice could not be repeated by the log, the model could not be recomputed without the evidence `wrong_key`, and the transfer by `retired_skills` would go over what was already computed;
- only the log, without a cache: every engine choice and every showing of the skill map would recompute the log from the start;
- one table of attempts instead of the log, like progress by the rows of attempts in canvas-api: the model would then read attempts with their content, and evidence is needed without it;
- the previous image does not end the attempts of a profile whose cache is newer than its mark: in the middle of a deployment the learner would get a refusal.

— *Consequence:* `internal/model/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Module versions

**R07. A new attempt gets the latest published version of a module — the newest by semver version of the pinned index that is not withdrawn, in the format window of `api`, one for all learners, — and a task of a lesson gets the package on which the lesson began. The image holds all the versions of the pinned index in the format window, withdrawn ones too. The configuration is the file `modules.json` with the tag of a release of `modules` and the digest of its index: the index's signature is checked by CI of each pull request, and the release goes only forward. Attempts on a withdrawn version and outside the window are closed "unchecked" by a job after the deployment.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the current version of a module is the newest by semver entry of the pinned index that the index has not marked `withdrawn` and whose format version is in the window of `api`: while the format is v0, only the current format version, the one of the tag of the `spec` submodule. A mark with any reason, an unknown one too, `api` counts as a withdrawal. This is the same rule by which the module format chooses the version of a module that teaches an anchor skill. The newest version is chosen by the package `moduleindex` of the `module-kit` library, not by the own code of `api`, and versions are compared by the format: in 0.y.z, y plays the major, and while the format is v0, a module's move to a new format version is a major change. The current version is one for all learners. It changes with a pull request that moves the configuration to another release of `modules`, or with a change of the window — the move of `api` to a new line of `spec`, — and takes effect with the build of the image;
  - a new attempt outside a lesson is on the current version: `BeginAttempt` takes it by the same rule as the catalog, `packages.Set.CurrentFor`, and the attempt stores the package digest and the versions of the module, the format and `module-kit`. A task of a lesson is an attempt on the package on which the lesson began: the lesson run holds its digest. On a withdrawn package or a package outside the window the lesson begins no new attempt and ends "unchecked", as an attempt does;
  - the deployment's list of modules, when the deployment sets one, bounds the new work: a module the list leaves out keeps its current version, but no new attempt or lesson run is begun on it — for the learner it is a module without a current version —, and an attempt or a run already open on it goes on to its end on its package;
  - whether a module suits a learner, `api` judges by the current version: if it does not suit the learner, while the previous one did, for them the module is like a module without a current version, and `api` does not give them the previous version. An open lesson run is otherwise: the module is still judged by the current version, and the lesson and each of its tasks by the run's package, the one the learner sees;
  - a module's connection is the module ID and the date of connection, without a version. The engine gets the graphs of the current versions of the connected modules that suit the learner. `learner-model` gets the graphs of the current versions of all the modules whose evidence is in the profile's log, connected or not, and whether or not the current version suits the learner: so the set of graphs is given by the log, the release and the window, which the cache mark carries. A connected module about which there is no evidence yet does not enter the cache: `learner-model` gets its graph at reading, together with the engine. What depends on the connections — the starting point of skills without evidence and the mastery of an anchor skill by the skills "in the learner's modules" — `learner-model` computes at reading and does not write to the cache. A module without a current version stays connected, its evidence is in the log and waits, it gives no graph, and there are no new attempts and lessons on it;
  - the image holds all the versions of the pinned index whose format version is in the window of `api`, withdrawn ones too — only for repeating a verdict. A file of the device part lies in the image once, by its own digest. A package leaves the image when its format version leaves the window. Which versions to hold is said by the index, and the build needs no database. The image is built by the deployment of `infra` around the signed binary of the release of `api`, and the packages are put into it by the code of `api`: the build stage of the `Dockerfile` calls a command of the binary that reads the embedded `modules.json`, the same command by which the recipe of the first app puts the packages into a folder; the deployment does not repeat the logic of packages;
  - the status of a digest in the image's index: "gives" — the package is in the window, and the entry is not withdrawn; "no longer gives" — the index knows the digest, and the entry is withdrawn or outside the window; "does not know" — the digest is not in the index. A digest that two entries name "gives" if at least one of them gives it. Open views, the check of the packages that `app` holds and the path of files answer by this status;
  - attempts and lesson runs on packages that the image's index knows but no longer gives are closed "unchecked" by the command `api after`, which the deployment of `infra` calls when the new revision has taken all the traffic, one at a time, writing their evidence; the daily `api sweep` takes the same steps before closing abandoned ones, as the safety net; and before the job, and wherever it has not reached the attempt, the check on request closes them: it is mandatory, since a package outside the window is no longer in the image. The verdict is "unchecked" with a neutral phrase, since this is not the learner's error, the words of hints are deleted in the same transaction, and the evidence is the outcome "unchecked";
  - a package digest that the image's index does not know — the previous image during a deployment, or a package that the new release brought — gets a temporary refusal without a record: for a method `unavailable` with the cause `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN`, for a file `503` with `Retry-After`; a repeat of the request reaches the new image. The index is cumulative, so the new image knows the digests of all the previous ones. A rollback to an image older than the release on which attempts were begun is not allowed: `api` is rolled back only by rolling forward, and until then such attempts and runs get the temporary refusal and wait;
  - the filter `wrong_key` at the recomputation of the model reads `withdrawn` on each entry of the index — and on the entries outside the window too: they are skipped only by the choice of the current version;
  - the files of the device part and the open views of a withdrawn version `api` does not serve: for an open view the cause "version withdrawn", for a file the answer `410`, so that a withdrawal is visible in the middle of work too. `app` learns of a withdrawal by a request of its own — "which of these digests does the image no longer give" — and does not infer it from a refusal: it takes open views from the cache and does not reach `api` with them;
  - the configuration is the file `modules.json` at the root of the repository: `release` is the tag of a release of `modules`, `index` is the digest `sha256:` of the bytes of its `module-index.json`. It lists no modules and holds no package digests. `api` embeds it in the binary by a Go package at the root of the module — `//go:embed` does not accept `..` (sources 4.3) —, and the image build reads the same file, without a copy;
  - a CI job of its own, on each pull request and push to `main`, takes the index and its bundle from the release, checks the signature by the recipe `release-verify`, checks the SHA-256 against `index`, rejects a release older than the one pinned on `main` and a pull request after which a module would lose its current version otherwise than by a withdrawal, and prints the current versions of the modules. The image build checks the index only by SHA-256, and the packages are checked by `module-kit` by the chain from it;
  - a withdrawal takes effect only with a pull request that pins a release with the mark, and its delay is not silent: the scheduled job "Withdrawals" once a day takes the newest release of `modules`, checks the signature of its index by `release-verify` and fails while the newest index withdraws a version that the pinned release gives;
  - the table of what lies where has the row "connected modules", and the row of an attempt carries the module ID and its version too, so that the export can be read without the index.

— *Why:*
- **The latest published.** A fix of a key reaches the learner with the next attempt, and a withdrawal goes the same way for everyone — by the signed index. The engine and `learner-model` have one graph per module. A learner's place in a module is their skill model, not a position in a course: what comes next is chosen by the engine by the model, so a new version does not upset the place, and `retired_skills` carry progress over. The state cache is recomputed with a change of release anyway.
- **The graphs of `learner-model` by the log.** Were the set of graphs to depend on the connections and on the profile's age category, which the cache mark does not carry, then after a module is disconnected or the category changes the appended state would silently diverge from the recomputed one.
- **A lesson on its own package.** A lesson is short, its segments go in the author's order, and the engine does not reorder them. A new version could remove or reorder a segment in the middle of a lesson. `spec` says nothing about versions within a lesson, and the rule is a decision of `api`.
- **All the versions in the window.** The build does not go to the database, so CI and the image do not touch learners' data, and any begun attempt in the window stays checkable. In v0 the window is one format version, and modules move to a new one all at once: the set of versions in the image is reset with each format version, and unchanged media files of new versions take no space.
- **A temporary refusal for an unknown digest.** Otherwise the previous image would close the attempts and lesson runs of the new one "unchecked".
- **The release and the digest of the index.** `spec` asks for the configuration "a release of `modules` and its index", and the chain of digests begins from the index: index → inventory → parts → files. The digest pins the bytes whose signature CI checked, and from then on it holds the file.
- **Only forward.** A withdrawal is marked only by the index of the next releases: the index of an old release does not have it, and the withdrawn version would go into new attempts again.
- **Each pull request.** A required check whose workflow is skipped by a path filter stays pending and blocks the merge (sources 4.2), and a new tag of `spec` may raise the minimum version of the release check.

— *Rejected:*
- a version pinned to a module's connection, where minor ones come by themselves and a major one by an action of an adult or a learner: the image would hold each major on which anyone has stayed, without a limit, graphs and cache marks would be separate for each profile, and a fix in an old major would require a patch line from `modules`. A withdrawal moves the learner to another version anyway;
- a pinned version that changes only by an action: a fix of a key would not reach the learner until the version is withdrawn;
- a task of a lesson on the current version: a lesson would lose segments in the middle of a run;
- an image only with the versions for new attempts and those that the previous configuration gave: an attempt opened two pull requests back would lose its check, and a withdrawn version would leave the image, while it has to stay for repeating a verdict;
- an image by the unfinished attempts from the database: CI or the build would go to the database with learners' data;
- a list of modules in `modules.json`: while all modules are official, the gate is the release of `modules`, and which of them a deployment offers is the deployment's own list;
- package digests in the configuration: the index names them, and they would repeat the chain from it;
- a hash of the set of the graphs' digests in the cache mark: each reconnection would recompute the whole log, and a disconnected module would have no state until it is connected again;
- `app` inferring a withdrawal from a refusal: it takes open views from its cache without reaching `api`;
- closing only on request: open attempts on packages that are not in the image would stay in the database for long, until someone returns to them or the job for abandoned ones closes them.

— *Consequence:* `modules.json`; `modules.go`; `internal/packages/`; `cmd/api/packages.go`; `cmd/api/after.go`; `cmd/api/sweep.go`; `cmd/modules-check/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/model/`; `internal/catalog/`; `internal/rpc/`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/workflows/withdrawals.yml`; `Dockerfile`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/build.md`.

## Carrying progress over

**R08. Carrying progress over after a major version of a module is not a separate step but part of each computation of the state from the log: at a recomputation and at an append, `learner-model` routes each piece of evidence by `retired_skills` of the graph of the current version, and the log does not change. Evidence about a skill without a pair goes to no skill, and about a skill that the graph of the current version does not know, it waits in the log until the next version names it: so also after a withdrawal of the newest major.**
- **What was decided:**
  - when: carrying over happens each time `learner-model` computes the state from the log. This is the first read of a profile's model after the deployment of an image that changed the cache mark, and each append of evidence — that of an attempt begun on the previous version of the module included. There is no separate job or migration, and the log does not change;
  - over what: over the profile's log and the graphs of the current versions of the log's modules. Only the table `retired_skills` of the current version is needed: by the module format, the record of a retired skill stays in all following versions, and `replaced_by` names the skills of this version and, when a named skill is later retired itself, its replacements instead. So evidence of any age is carried over without the old graphs, and the packages that have left the image are not needed for carrying over, as long as the evidence carries everything the recomputation reads;
  - who does what. `api` cuts off the `wrong_key` evidence by its column — the package digest —, gives `learner-model` the log and the graphs in its types, with `retired_skills`, writes the result into the cache with the mark, and gives the engine the graphs of the connected modules that suit the learner. The graph of a connected module about which there is no evidence yet `learner-model` gets at a read too, but it does not go into the cache. Carrying over is done by `learner-model`: each module skill ID in a piece of evidence it looks up in the graph of the current version of the module that this ID names by its full ID:
    - the skill is in `skills` — the evidence goes to it: by the format, the meaning of a skill under the same ID does not change;
    - the skill is in `retired_skills` — it goes to each skill from `replaced_by`, and with what weight is decided by `learner-model`. `[]` — to none;
    - in neither — it goes to no skill, without an error, and the row stays in the log;
    - an anchor skill `core.<name>` goes as it is if the vocabulary of some format version in the window of `api` knows it, and otherwise to no skill: it can be a supporting skill of a task (the schema `task.schema.json`, `supporting`), it has no module, and `retired_skills` does not concern it. There is no table for anchor skills, and a new meaning comes under a new ID. While the format is v0, the window is one version;
    - the skills of a trap, if the evidence carries them, by the same rule. By the format the trap ID itself is not reused, and it needs no table: a trap that is not in the catalog of the current version is not among the repeating traps — there is nothing to show it with;
  - a skill without a pair, `replaced_by: []`: by the format, the progress in it goes nowhere. It is not on the skill map — the map draws the graph of the current version, and `retired_skills` is closed and carries no title of the skill. Its evidence stays in the log and goes into the export under the earlier full ID. There is no notice to the learner or the adult;
  - a withdrawal of the newest major, when a version of the previous major becomes current, goes by the same rule. The evidence about skills that are in the previous major under the same ID goes to them as usual, and about skills that it does not know, it waits: it stays in the log, goes nowhere and is not visible on the map. When the next version names such a skill in `skills` or `retired_skills`, its release changes the cache mark, and the recomputation counts it. With `wrong_key` the filter cuts it off anyway. The waiting evidence comes back because the comparison of versions counts a withdrawn version as published: the next version stands above it and accounts for its skills. The evidence of a module without a current version waits the same way: it gives no graph;
  - repeatability: the same prefix of the log up to number N, the same `wrong_key` marks of the pinned index, the same graphs of the current versions and the same version of `learner-model` give the same carrying over, and the cache mark and the number of the last evidence counted hold them. The engine choice stores the release of `modules`, the number of the last evidence and the package digests — the digests of all the graphs that `learner-model` and the engine got —, so carrying over repeats in a repeat of the choice too. In the cache is only what the log, the release, the window and the version of `learner-model` define: the state of the skills of the log's modules. What depends on connections and the profile — the starting point of skills without evidence, including in a connected module about which there is no evidence yet, and the mastery of an anchor skill by the skills "in the learner's modules" —, `learner-model` computes at a read, by the graphs that `api` gives at that time, and does not write into the cache. Time is not carrying over either: forgetting is a rule of `learner-model`. Were any of this to go into the cache, the cache mark would have to carry it. A snapshot of the state, if `learner-model` introduces one, holds only under the same mark, and carrying over does not apply to it: the log is carried over, not what was computed;
  - on the map after carrying over — the graph of the current version and the state computed from the log. By the format, a renamed skill gets the evidence of the earlier one, a split one — each of its parts, a merged one — the evidence of all, and with what weight and whether "mastered" carries over is decided by `learner-model`. A skill without a pair and a waiting skill are not on the map, and a new one is without evidence. The data of the skill map go by the IDs of the current version;
  - a lesson begun on an earlier package names the skills of the earlier version: they go to the engine translated by the same rule;
  - its own part of the export names, for each module of the log, the version by whose graph the state is computed, and for a module without a current version, that there is no graph and its evidence waits. The log carries the earlier IDs, and the table of an official module a reader will find in the open release of that version. How this goes for third-party modules, of stage 4, is decided together with their licenses (О-10).

— *Why:* carrying over at each computation follows from the learner model as an append-only log: the log is the truth, and the state is a cache, which is recomputed when the graph changes. A separate step of carrying over would keep a second place where progress can diverge from the log. The rule "in neither — to no skill" is one for a skill without a pair, for a withdrawn major, for a module without a current version and for any gap in the table. Nothing is lost by this: the log does not change, and a recomputation with a fixed version brings back what waits. A skill without a pair gets nothing, as the format requires, and there is nothing to show it on the map with: there is no title either in `retired_skills` or in the image after a change of the window. `spec` asks for no notice.

— *Rejected:*
- carrying over as a separate step — by a job after the deployment or a migration of the state: the state is recomputed lazily from the log, and carrying over on top of an already computed state would be the second place where progress diverges;
- rewriting the earlier skill IDs in the log with the new ones: the log is append-only, and the export would lose which skill the evidence was about;
- a skill without a pair on the map with the mark "retired" and the earlier progress: there is no title in `retired_skills`, and the package of the earlier version leaves the image with a change of the window;
- notifying the adult which skills went away: `api` would store per profile which version the learner saw, and `spec` asks for no notice;
- reverse carrying over after a withdrawal of the newest major — routing the evidence of its new skills to the skills of the previous major by `retired_skills` of the withdrawn version: the format has no such rule, a merge cannot be unambiguously split back, and `learner-model` would get two graphs of one module, while it has one graph per module;
- rejecting in CI a pull request after which the previous major becomes current: the harmful version would stay current;
- a check of its own of the coverage of `retired_skills` in CI of `api`: it would repeat the version comparison of `module-kit`, and a gap in the table only delays evidence, and the fixed version brings it back.

— *Consequence:* `internal/model/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Showing the solution and hints

**R09. Showing the solution on request: to a learner of the category `adult` — from the start of the attempt, to a child — from the fourth wrong answer, when the hint ladder has been passed. The setting "ask before showing" in a child's profile is on by default, and then showing is an adult action, and a learning request to show `api` rejects. Showing closes the attempt with the outcome "solution shown".**
- **What was decided:**
  - the ladder. A wrong answer is a submission in which at least one answer slot got "not yet"; "unreadable" and "unchecked" do not raise the rung, as in canvas-api (sources 6.1). The k-th wrong answer gives rung k while k is not more than three, and the attempt stores the number of wrong answers as a whole. From the fourth wrong answer there is no new rung and AI is not called for new words, as `offer_help` in canvas-api: to a child `app` offers to call an adult or to show the solution, to an adult learner — to show the solution. "Explain differently" of rung 3, if it was not asked for yet, remains. This is exactly what the platform calls "after the third rung";
  - a child is a profile of any age category but `adult`. Showing to them `api` gives only from the fourth wrong answer, by whatever kind of action the request came. A learner of the category `adult` shows the solution from the start of the attempt, by a learning action;
  - the setting "ask before showing" is only in a child's profile, and by default it is on. It is changed by an adult by an action on the profile, `can_manage`. On — `app` asks for the PIN of the section for adults and sends the showing as an adult action, `can_manage`, and a learning request to show `api` rejects with the cause "an adult is needed". Off — showing is a learning action, `can_learn`. The request carries no proof that the PIN was asked for: on a shared device the token is one, and the server tells kinds of actions apart, not people. The refusal is not a security boundary but one place where the setting is enforced for all devices of the account;
  - in code an adult action is checked for the right `can_manage` by the handler: the checks before it hold `can_learn`, as the table of methods does, — without the right, `permission_denied` without `Refusal`. The order of refusals: a task with a rubric — "no showing"; a child before the fourth wrong answer — "ladder not passed", by any kind of action; a learning action with "ask before showing" on — "an adult is needed";
  - "call an adult" is an interface of `app`: `api` notifies nobody;
  - showing closes the attempt. The outcome is "solution shown", that is, the evidence "not solved". Showing adds no trap of its own. The traps that the program found in the wrong answers before showing, and the items correct before showing, with their verdict, the evidence carries. The task's solution — a closed field — goes to the device when the attempt is closed, the words of hints are deleted in the same transaction, the answer is a solved task, as with "correct", and the segment of the lesson ends;
  - a correct answer — all items of the task are correct — closes the attempt too, and the solution comes together with the verdict. The tutor's words after a correct answer — praise and a question about which technique helped — `app` asks for by a second request, like the words of a rung, and `api` gives them on a closed attempt too: once — the attempt marks that they were given, — and while the profile has not begun a new attempt. The words themselves `api` does not store, and this call does not get the previous questions: they were deleted with the words of hints;
  - a task with a rubric has neither a ladder nor showing the solution: an item with a rubric has no verdict, and by the format such a task may have no solution;
  - the ladder is per task, not per item, by the rule of `spec`. So on a workbook page with a dozen items four submissions with an error offer to show the solution of the whole page, and `api` does not change it.

— *Why:* showing after the ladder and with an adult's setting is what `spec` decided for showing the solution. The threshold is the fourth wrong answer, as in canvas-api, where the platform's ladder came from: there the analysis went on only while «неверных ответов на холсте не больше трёх», and from the fourth `offer_help` came (sources 6.1). So a child first tries the simpler case of the third rung, and there is no AI call beyond three rungs. The setting is on by default: in MathTrail a child did not see the solution before a correct answer at all (sources 6.1), and this way an adult sees it first, and the same adult can turn the setting off. Showing closes the attempt, because the key does not go to the device until the task is solved: an answer after a shown solution would check nothing. "Solution shown" means "not solved" — as "I don't know" in standalone: "a child who has seen the solution has not solved the task; counting it as wrong keeps the rating an honest estimate" (sources 6.1).

— *Rejected:*
- never showing the solution to a child, as canvas-api did: `spec` puts showing after the ladder instead;
- offering showing already from the third rung: a child would skip the simpler case;
- a setting off by default, like the switch "Ask before “Show solution”" of the prototype: it is off, but the prototype's code reads its value nowhere (sources 6.1);
- showing without closing the attempt: the solution carries the key, and it would be on the device while the attempt is open;
- the adult looks at the solution in their own section, and the child's attempt goes on: the section is on the same device, and the key would be there too;
- a check of the PIN on the server with a short confirmation: the server tells kinds of actions apart, not people, and the PIN stays a check of the device;
- showing to a child before the fourth wrong answer by an adult action: the setting would then mean "showing at any moment", while showing to a child comes after the ladder;
- "skipped, the rating does not change", as "Show solution" of the prototype: "solution shown" is the evidence "not solved".

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R10. A hint on request: before the first wrong answer — the task's technique and its leading question, and `app` shows "givens and unknown" from the open view; after it — the current rung once more, without AI; "explain differently" — the same rung in other words, one AI call per rung, without the work. The model's question and the fallback text speak of the first wrong answer slot in order. A profile has one open attempt of a task of a module, and beside it the open attempts of one page from paper: a new one closes the previous one of its kind, and without actions an attempt is closed after 7 days.**
- **What was decided:**
  - a request "Hint" before the first wrong answer — and after submissions where there were no wrong ones, only "unreadable" or "unchecked", — gives, without AI and without reading the work, the IDs of the task's techniques, empty if there are none, and the leading question of the attempt's instance if the task has one: `GetHintResponse.leading_question`, and the open attempt holds the one given in `OpenAttempt.requested_leading_question`. Beside the words of a rung the question is always empty. At a step whose `gives` gives techniques, `app` names the attempt and the step's place and gets the same IDs. Before the first wrong answer there is no rung yet. The request is an action on the attempt: it moves the time of the last action;
  - "givens and unknown" are open: `app` shows them from the task's open view, at a step of the mode "by steps" and on request alike, and `api` neither records that the learner saw them nor puts them into the evidence;
  - after a wrong answer the request gives once more the words of the current rung that the attempt stores, — without AI and without the work: `app` sends the request without the work. If there are no words — the second request for them did not arrive, — the learner gets the fallback text of the rung, also without AI, as in canvas-api (sources 6.1). The rung grows only with wrong answers;
  - "explain differently" is the same rung in other words: one AI call per rung, within the platform AI quota. The right is spent as soon as the call has gone, however it ended; with an exhausted quota or without an AI route there is no call, and the right stays. A second time on the same rung is the refusal "already asked" — there is no call —, and `app` keeps the earlier words. The model gets what the words of a rung get, but for the work: the key and the solution of the task, the trap of the rung's answer slot, the rung, the presentation style, the explanation language, the age category and the AI route, — and the task's previous questions, which the platform's rules of privacy let go to AI from what is stored. The trap — the program's finding by the verdict — the words of a rung get too, in the second request, when the verdict is already recorded. The model does not get what was read, just as the words of a rung do not get it, and does not get the work because the request does not send it;
  - the answer to "differently" passes the leak check and the form check. If it did not come or did not pass, the learner gets the fallback text of the rung, and if they are reading exactly that text, the outcome "no other words" in a successful answer, without a repeat: the call was made, and the right is spent. In canvas-api the trap text that matched an already shown hint was also replaced by a template (sources 6.1). Without AI — a tapped choice in the mode without AI (О-5) — "explain differently" is not offered;
  - each call of an attempt's words — both of a rung and of "differently" — gets the previous questions of the same task, so as not to repeat them, as the model in canvas-api got «ступень подсказки и прошлые вопросы этого холста» (sources 6.1);
  - an AI failure, an exhausted quota and words that did not pass the leak check or the form check give the fallback text of the rung: the verdict is already given, and the learner always gets an answer. A neutral phrase is only for the verdict "unchecked". `tutor` left this choice to `api`;
  - the rung's answer slot is the first answer slot "not yet" in order in the submission that gave the rung: items by place, answer slots by order in the item. The program chooses it. Its trap the words of a rung and of "differently" get, and the fallback text is taken by the chain of the format: on rungs 1–2, the text of its trap value, without it the trap advice of its trap, without a trap the rung template, and on the third, the simpler case of its item. From the fourth wrong answer there is no rung, and the answer slot stays the one that rung 3 speaks about: "Hint", the fallback text and "differently" speak about one place. `api` does not store the answer slot: it is derived from the verdicts of the submission;
  - the evidence. The outcome is by the wrong answers, "after hint number k": the rung they reached, `min(wrong, 3)`; the outcome "with a sketch hint" `api` does not write — it has no such hint. A request and "explain differently" do not change the outcome. The evidence carries the task's technique at a step and on request, by `step_techniques_at` and `requested_techniques_at`; the leading question given on request, only by its own field of `learner-model`, `leading_question_on_request`, and not by the field of the technique on request; how many times the learner asked for "differently", by the rungs where they asked; and whether the solution of this task went to the profile's device earlier — by the earlier attempts of the same task closed "correct" or "solution shown" before the start of this one: the learner sees the solution after a correct answer too. A template has no such fact: a new attempt gives new numbers. The weights are decided by `learner-model`;
  - the end of an attempt is a correct answer or showing the solution, "unchecked" on a withdrawn version or outside the window, the end of its lesson run "not suitable", "done" of a task with a rubric, and also leaving and the time limit:
    - a profile has one open attempt of a task of a module. A new one — by a lesson, by the engine choice or the same task again — closes the open one in the same transaction that begins it: "left", and an overdue one "unchecked" or "abandoned", as the check on request would close it. The unique partial index `attempts (profile_id) WHERE closed_at IS NULL AND kind = 'module'`, which the migration `0022` recreates, does not let a second open one in, so two devices of one profile do not open two attempts at once. So a child who has nobody to call leaves the task, and `app` expects exactly that: for it the attempt ended also when the learner has begun the same task anew (sources 6.2). An action on an attempt is its start, a submission, the start of a step and "Hint" before the first wrong answer; reading the open attempt is not one and does not move the last action: opening an attempt on another device is not yet work on the task;
    - the attempts of a task from the learner's paper go beside it: `BeginPaperAttempts` begins one for each task picked on a page, from 1 to 20, and closes the open attempts of another page "left" in the same transaction — the page is `page_id`, the ID `app` gives it, kept on each attempt while it is open, migration `0026`, so that a Start again after "Add" closes none of its own; it leaves the open attempt of a module as it is, and a start of a task of a module closes no attempt of paper. An attempt of paper is of its own kind: it holds no module, package, task or seed, and begins by no choice, run or earlier attempt. The unique index holds the attempts of modules alone, the open attempts of a page have the index `attempts_paper_open`, and the list of profiles names the attempt of the module. It gives no evidence — its task is of no module and has no skill —, so its close settles none, and the time limit closes it as any. `FindTasks` gives the frames with `turn_degrees`, the turn by which `tutor` turned the photo upright before it found them; `api` keeps neither the photo of the page nor the frames and turn the finding gave: the record of the key of `FindTasks` holds its outcome alone, and a repeat is "repeated", without frames or turn;
    - with platform AI closed to the learner, the path of a page stops at its finding: `FindTasks` makes no call without the admission and the reserve of the quota, and refuses "not admitted", "quota" or "platform limit"; `BeginPaperAttempts` refuses as the start of a task of handwriting does. A task from paper has no check without AI, so the path does not go on by hand;
    - an attempt without actions for 7 days is abandoned. A request on it closes it "abandoned", the closing stays, and the request gets "attempt closed" with the reason "abandoned". The safety net, for the attempts on a withdrawn version too, is the step `abandoned_attempts` of the operator's daily job `api sweep`: `UPDATE` in batches of 1000, past the rows that a request holds, with the time limit checked once more on the row itself, and without the profile's activity date; the evidence it owes the job's step `evidence` appends;
    - "not suitable": the lesson run ended "not suitable" while its attempt was open. Open attempts outside a lesson are not rechecked: a task is checked at the start of an attempt, and such an attempt brings no new tasks. "Done": the learner finished an attempt of a task with a rubric, accepted after at least one submission of the attempt;
    - the outcome of a left, an abandoned and a "not suitable" attempt: there were wrong answers — "left unfinished", and the evidence carries the traps that the program found; there were none — there is no evidence, and the attempt stays a row with a close reason and is visible in progress. An attempt with a rubric, "done", left or abandoned, gives no evidence (О-17);
  - what an attempt stores beyond the table of what lies where: the number of wrong answers and the time the task's technique was first given at a step, columns of the migration `0006`; the times the techniques and the leading question were first given on request, `requested_techniques_at` and `leading_question_at`, each only when the task has it; whether the words after a correct answer were given; the time the solution was shown; the close reason — "correct", "solution shown", "left", "abandoned", "unchecked", "not suitable", "done" — and its time, set together, and the time of the last action. All this goes into the export. By which kind of action the solution was shown `api` does not store: the outcome does not need it, and the data is a minimum;
  - the words of hints are the table `attempt_rungs` of the migration `0008`, a row per rung of the attempt: the source and the words, and after "explain differently", its time, source and the words that the learner reads from then on. They live until the end of the attempt: a database trigger deletes the words in the transaction of the closing, by any reason, the job's too, and the rung number and the source stay. An abandoned attempt ends no later than 8 days after the last action: 7 days and a day until the job.

— *Why:* a request by rung and "differently" of the same rung are what `spec` decided for a hint on request: if a request raised the rung, the learner would get to the simpler case and to the solution without trying themselves, and the rung is chosen by the program by the wrong answers. "Differently" is once per rung: so beyond the words of the rungs there are no more than three calls per attempt. Without the work — because the request sends no work, and the model gets the same as the words of a rung, and the previous questions. "No other words" is an outcome, not a refusal: the call was made. The first wrong answer slot in order is a rule without AI, one for the model and for the fallback text: both speak about one place. One open attempt — as in MathTrail: in standalone a task without an answer becomes a skip when the next one is asked for, and in canvas-api a canvas is abandoned only by a new canvas of the same task (sources 6.1).
