A time limit is needed on top of that: the words of hints live until the end of the attempt, and an abandoned attempt a new one may never close. 7 days — so that the learner can come back to the task after a weekend too. The attempts of a page go beside the one of a module because the learner solves the tasks of a page in any order and comes back to one, and a task of a module between them should not take the page away; a new page closes the one before, as a new task does, so that the attempts of paper are bounded by one page. Leaving without wrong answers does not move the model, as the skip in standalone, where punishing a skip would mean "punish a stray tap" (sources 6.1), and the wrong answers before leaving are evidence, and leaving does not hide them; a run that stopped suiting the profile is, for evidence, like leaving.

— *Rejected:*
- each request raises the rung, as in the author's analysis and in the prototype's code: the learner would get to the solution without trying themselves;
- `api` records that the learner saw "givens and unknown": in the mode "by steps" `app` shows them at a step without a request to `api`, so the record would mean something other than what it says;
- "explain differently" without a limit, within one quota: a child would spend the account's quota by presses;
- no "explain differently": other words of the same rung are what a learner who did not understand the question lacks;
- "differently" with the work of the last submission: `app` sends the request without the work, and the work would have to be kept on the device and sent once more;
- "differently" with what was read from the database instead of the work: of what is stored, the platform's rules of privacy let only the previous questions go to AI, and what was read the words of a rung do not get either;
- "no other words" as a refusal: the call was made, and the right is spent;
- the leading question on request in the field of the technique on request: the log is append-only, and a recomputation would no longer separate mixed facts;
- the outcome of a left attempt "not solved": that is what the platform calls "solution shown", and the term would mean two different things;
- the reason "left" for "not suitable" or "done", and "correct" for "done": "left" is the profile's leaving for another attempt, and an item with a rubric has no verdict; one term means one thing;
- an answer slot with a trap text ahead of the first wrong one: the hint would jump around the page;
- the answer slot where the analysis found the first error: the choice would depend on AI, and without an analysis the rule would be different;
- a neutral phrase on an AI failure on words: the verdict is already given, and the fallback text of the rung helps more;
- an abandoned attempt only by a time limit, of 24 hours: a child who came back the next evening would lose the attempt, and leaving the task earlier would be impossible;
- the outcome "unchecked" for a left, an abandoned and a "not suitable" attempt: if `learner-model` gives it zero weight, the wrong answers before leaving will be lost to the model;
- leaving without wrong answers as a wrong answer: standalone rejected the same (sources 6.1);
- one open attempt for a task from paper too, each closing the one before: the tasks of a page would close each other, and the photo of homework would take away the task of a module the learner was in;
- one attempt for a whole page: the rung, the wrong answers and the close are of a task;
- the attempts of every page open until each is solved: an attempt would wait 7 days on the device of a page the learner has put away.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/model/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Platform AI quota

**R11. The platform AI quota is dollars by the estimate of `tutor`, per family day, with two caps: the profile's — $0.50, the account's — $1.50, as the first values of the tariff "closed slice". The account gets the tariff with the invite code. An exhausted quota closes platform AI until the next day: a check of handwriting is "unchecked", words are the fallback text, and the attempt waits.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the unit is dollars by the estimate from the spending message of `tutor`: the model, the tokens and the price by its price table with a date. So the card of `api` in `spec` asks for: "platform AI quotas — by the spending that `tutor` reports". The quota is only on platform AI: the spending of an own key the platform does not accumulate, and the limit is set by the key's owner. There is no automatic move from an own key to platform AI: it is another AI route and another processor;
  - each model call on platform AI is counted: both readers of handwriting, the words of a rung, "explain differently", the words after a correct answer, the reading and the assessment of an essay;
  - there are two caps, per day, as in canvas-api (sources 7.1). The profile's cap is so that the children of one account do not share the quota "first come, first served", the account's cap is a cap on spending. The first values are the profile $0.50, the account $1.50: in canvas-api the cap of the worst day was ≈ $0.52 per child and ≈ $1.55 per parent (sources 7.1). These are values of the tariff's configuration, the table of tariffs of `internal/quota`, and a pull request of it changes them, not a change of this entry. The owner profile spends the same cap of the account, and an account with one profile runs into the profile's cap;
  - the day is the family's, as in standalone (sources 7.1). `app` sends the offset of its clock from UTC with requests to platform AI, and with an own key it does not; the account holds the offset and the end of the current day, and a new offset takes effect from the next day. A request that does not call AI — `BeginAttempt`, "something else" — carries no offset: the day its refusal "quota" names is the one `api` holds with the account. A day runs from the family's midnight to midnight, and the first day by a new offset ends at the first midnight by it that comes no earlier than 20 hours after the end of the previous day: otherwise a change of the offset from +14 hours to −12 would give a day of 2 hours and a second quota. So after a trip east by more than 4 hours, the first day by the new offset is longer — up to 44 hours. An offset outside −12…+14 hours or not a multiple of a quarter of an hour `api` rejects. Until a device has reported an offset, the day is UTC. Summer time and a trip are an ordinary change of the offset;
  - the tariff is a field of the account, as the platform's privacy document names it in the account. The operator creates an invite code with a tariff — by default "closed slice", — and at the redemption of the code the tariff passes to the account. The tariff is changed by an operator's command that finds the account by its code: the account ID does not leave the platform. The caps of each tariff are in the configuration of `api`. If the account has no tariff or the tariff has no caps, platform AI is closed: "closed by default", as with the admission function of `tutor`. The tariff `dev_check` is the dev projects' alone: `serve` deployed reads it as a tariff with no caps, so platform AI is closed to an account that got it all the same, "not admitted", and `GetQuota` gives no share; off a deployment it has the caps of the table of tariffs;
  - the quota is the step of the checks of a request that comes after the right and the admission, only for requests that call AI. It changes what the learner gets, and does not reject the request;
  - exhausted — the profile's or the account's cap does not cover the request's reserve. Then there is no call:
    - a check of handwriting and of a choice written by hand — "unchecked" and a neutral phrase, and the rung does not grow;
    - the words of a rung — the fallback text, and "differently" is not called, and the right to it stays;
    - there are no words after a correct answer;
    - an essay is not read: "unchecked" and a neutral phrase, and the attempt waits, like any;
    - a tapped choice is checked as always.

    For a submission and for words this is no refusal but an outcome in a successful answer: the verdict "unchecked" or the fallback text with the cause "quota" and the time when the next day begins. A refusal — an error of the protocol — is for the quota at the start of an attempt and of a lesson's segment, and for "explain differently", which without a reserve does not call AI. The text is in `app`, and it names no numbers, as in standalone (sources 7.1), but it may promise "tomorrow": the day is the family's;
  - in the middle of a task the attempt waits. It is open until a new attempt or the limit of 7 days closes it, and `app` says that checking will come back tomorrow. A new task with handwriting `api` begins only if the remainder covers a typical task — two checks, words and praise: this is a value of the configuration. A task with a rubric has its own typical task, a value of its own — two readings of the essay with an assessment, the first submission and one rewrite: ≈ $0.06 by the price of an essay in `tutor`, $0.030 with Opus 5.5 · `high` (sources 7.1). While the attempt waits, `api` does not offer a new task by itself: the engine is called only when the learner asks for another task. If they asked, the waiting attempt is closed with the reason "left", as always, the wrong answers before leaving give "left unfinished", and `app` warns that there is no coming back to it. A lesson's segment waits the same way;
  - AI closed for a new task — one rule for a quota that does not cover a typical task, the admission refusal "not admitted" and the provider's refusal by a limit: `api` gives the engine only tasks that are checked without AI, as `tutor` requires for admission. In the first slice there are almost no such tasks, and then the learner sees that there is no checking until tomorrow, and on the provider's refusal, that there is none for now;
  - the provider's refusal by a limit on the platform's key is the outcome "limit reached" of `tutor`. The provider's limit is monthly, and such a refusal does not promise "tomorrow": its cause is "platform limit". `api` closes platform AI for everyone until an operator's command opens it again, and writes the event code into the log, without an ID: this is a signal to the operator. The spending limit of the workspace at the provider is set by the author;
  - the adult sees in their section the share of today's quota of the profile and the account and when the next day begins, not dollars, and the learner the share of their own profile alone, where they begin work; the share of the account a child never sees. The view is `app`'s, and the request is `AccountService.GetQuota` of the contract, in `internal/account`:
    - **the right.** `ACTION_UNSPECIFIED` is `invalid_argument`. A learning action goes by `rpc.Learner`, with its refusals of consent and of age. An adult's asks `can_view` in the handler, as the skill map does, and without it gets `permission_denied` with no `Refusal`, as the contract has it for a right that is missing; it asks for no consent of the learner;
    - **the reading.** `store.QuotaSettled` reads in one snapshot and writes nothing: the family day the account keeps at the time of the request, by `store.Today` — the request carries no offset —, the caps of the tariff, and what the counters of the profile and of the account of that day have spent, without their reserve. A day with no counter has spent nothing, and a profile not of the account is "no profile";
    - **the share.** `quota.Share` — whole percent rounded down, 100 at the cap and past it. `cap_reached` is the settled spending of the profile or of the account at its cap, beside a learning action too, which gets no share of the account. A tariff without caps, or no tariff, gives neither share and `cap_reached` false. `next_family_day` is the end of that day;
  - stage 3 adds tariffs and payment (О-14), and the rule does not change: the caps are set by the account's tariff. The caps of stage 2 are not a model for stage 3 — Д10;
  - in the first app the author's calls go by the platform AI route, with a key from an environment variable on their machine, and the quota counts them by the tariff of their account.

— *Why:* dollars — because a reading call and an analysis call cost differently, and so do the tokens of different models. By this argument `tutor` chose an estimate in dollars for the spending message: «С оценкой в долларах `api` считает квоты и юнит-экономику (О-14), не зная цен моделей» (sources 7.1). Two caps — as in canvas-api, where the parent's limits moreover survived the deletion of a child (sources 12.1). The family day is a lesson of standalone: a UTC day ended for the author "at seven in the evening" and broke off a session (sources 7.1). The least length of a day is needed because standalone defers a new offset until the next day but does not bound its length. The tariff from the code — because the code is the only way into the closed slice anyway, and the platform's privacy document holds the tariff in the account anyway. The attempt waits — because a left one with wrong answers gives "left unfinished" anyway, and a new reason or "unchecked" would hide the wrong answers from the learner model. The share in `GetQuota` is of the settled spending: the contract has it grow only within a day, and a reserve returned would lower it. The estimate of spending, a guide for the caps and for the tariffs, prices the words of a rung by the measured call of `tutor`, $0.0152 with Opus 5.5 · `high` (sources 7.1): the solving mode "self" — 20 tasks, 30% with one error, 26 checks and 26 words calls — costs ≈ 26 × ($0.0086 + $0.0152) ≈ $0.62, "by steps" the same, and the profile's cap of $0.50 covers about 16 tasks, not 20; the caps do not change by it.

— *Rejected:*
- calls by kind, as the counters of readings and analyses of canvas-api: two readers and a change of model change the price of a call, and the card asks for counting by spending;
- "checks" as the unit: a check of handwriting and a check of an essay cost differently;
- only the account's cap: the children of one account would share the quota, first come, first served;
- a UTC day, as in canvas-api (sources 7.1): for a family in the USA the quota would renew at 19–20 hours, and the phrase could not promise "tomorrow";
- a sliding day: "tomorrow" would become the hour when the day began;
- a time zone that the adult chooses: an extra step, and until it is made, the day is UTC;
- one tariff for everyone and a tariff only from stage 3: the author and the testers need their own cap, and the rule of the quota would have to be tied to the tariff again;
- an operator's command by the account ID: the ID would leave the platform;
- closing the attempt with the reason "quota" without evidence or with the outcome "unchecked": the wrong answers before it would be lost to the learner model;
- an automatic move from an own key to platform AI: another processor and an unnoticed spending of the quota;
- the typical task of handwriting for an essay: two checks, words and praise do not fit it;
- pricing the words of a rung in the estimate by the answer of a judge, $0.0098: that answer carries the key and the analysis, and the estimate would be wrong, not rough;
- in `GetQuota`, counting the reserves in, as `store.QuotaFits` does: the share would fall when a reserve is returned; writing the day of a new offset, as `store.FamilyDay`: the request carries none, and a read that writes would hold the account's row; the refusal "no profile" for an adult without `can_view`, as the export and the storage of works give it for `can_manage`: the contract and the skill map give `permission_denied`.

— *Consequence:* `internal/quota/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `cmd/api/tariff.go`; `cmd/api/invite.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R12. The quota is counted by a reserve before a call and a settlement by the spending message in the day of the reserve; a broken-off call is counted as the reserve, not as the worst case. The counters of the profile and the account live no longer than 2 days after the end of their own day; deleting a profile takes its counters away, and the account's counters stay.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the reserve is taken before each request that calls AI, on the counters of the profile and the account in one transaction. The request goes if on each of them what is spent, what is in reserve and the new reserve together are no more than the cap. So two devices of one account do not slip past the cap at once, and a reserve at the start of a check was in canvas-api too (sources 7.1);
  - the size of the reserve is the expected price of the kind of call, not the worst case. The worst case of one answer in `tutor` is 4,000 tokens of input and 16,000 of output, for Opus 5.5 about $0.34 (sources 7.1), and a reserve by it would close checks long before the cap. The expected price of a kind by its price table and the model of the kind is given by `tutor`, and without it, a value by kind in the configuration of `api`. A check of handwriting reserves both readers together, an essay the reading together with the assessment, a workbook page by the number of crops: the quota does not run out in the middle of a check;
  - the settlement is in the day when the reserve was taken:
    - an exact spending record is counted at its own price;
    - a record by the worst case — for an answer cut off before the tokens were counted — is counted as the reserve: a failure of the platform does not eat a child's day. The worst case goes into the spending log;
    - there was no call — the reserve is returned;
    - the provider refused one reader by a limit — only the record of the one that answered is counted;
    - there is no spending message at all — the reserve stays;
    - the process crashed — the reserve stays as the spending of its day, as in canvas-api a call that was made was counted even on a failure;
  - overspending beyond the cap is no more than the difference between the exact price and the reserve for each running request. It is held by the limited `MaxOutputTokens` of each kind of product call of `tutor`;
  - what is stored: the profile's counter and the account's counter — the start of the day, spent and in reserve; in the account — the tariff, the offset and the end of the current day; in the invite code — the tariff; the flag "platform AI closed" — one per deployment. The counters are keyed by the start of the day, not by a date: with a change of the offset the family's date may repeat. The tariff of an invite code and the flag carry no data of the learner or the owner, and they are not in the table of what lies where: codes are not personal data, as in canvas-api (sources 12.1), and the flag is one per deployment;
  - retention periods: a counter is needed for its own day and for one more day after its end — for settling requests across midnight, — and then the operator's daily retention job deletes it. So a counter lives no longer than 2 days after the end of its own day, and leaves the history of Neon a day later still. A counter is created with the first reserve of the day, so a day without requests has none;
  - the export is the tariff, the offset, the end of the current day and all stored counters: everything goes into the export but secrets and service fields;
  - deleting a profile takes its counters away, and the account's counters stay: otherwise a deletion and a new profile would lift the cap on spending, as canvas-api found (sources 12.1). Deleting the account takes everything away, and a new account needs a new one-time code, so the cap is not lifted then either. With the open sign-in of stage 3 this stops holding — Д10;
  - logs — without IDs. In them are the spending message of `tutor`, which has neither content nor identifiers, and the event codes "quota exhausted" and "platform limit". The offset is in no line of the log, as in standalone (sources 7.1). Unit economics reads sums by kind of call and model and the number of exhaustions per day, and `api` writes no summaries by account;
  - against the platform's privacy document: the counters and the offset are pseudonymous data about when and how much a family learns, and they do not go to AI. The offset names a time zone, not a street or a city, so it is data of the account, not the geolocation that the platform promises not to collect.

— *Why:* the reserve and a call that was made being counted — as in canvas-api: «Сделанный вызов засчитывается, даже если он не удался или вышел по тайм-ауту: он может быть оплачен, а дневной лимит должен оставаться потолком трат» (sources 7.1). The provider does indeed charge for a broken-off request: "If your client disconnects or times out in the middle of a request that was on track to succeed, that request is still charged" (sources 7.1). But the price of such a request `tutor` knows only by the worst case, and the quota counts the reserve so that a failure does not punish the learner. The account's counters survive the deletion of a profile, as in canvas-api. The period is shorter than the 30 days of canvas-api: the quota needs only its own day and one more day after it — for settling requests across midnight, — and the data must be as little as possible. There are no summaries by account: with a handful of participants of the closed slice they are personal data.

— *Rejected:*
- a reserve by the worst case: with Opus about $0.34 per call, and the profile's cap would run out in one and a half reserves;
- counting a broken-off call at its worst case: a failure of the platform would eat a child's day;
- returning the reserve of a crashed request: its calls may have been paid for;
- counters for 30 days, as in canvas-api: the quota does not need them;
- summaries by account for unit economics: with a handful of participants this is personal data, and sums without IDs are enough;
- deleting the account's counters together with the profile: a deletion and a new profile would lift the cap.

— *Consequence:* `internal/quota/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/deletion/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Lesson

**R13. `api` keeps a lesson by a lesson run — a row of the database per profile and lesson, on the package the lesson began on, one for all devices. A run is begun by `app`'s request for the lesson that the engine chose, and the learner's leaving for another task does not end it. It ends "passed" — by "lesson passed" from `app`, when all task segments have ended —, and also "unchecked", "not suitable" and "abandoned" after 30 days without actions. The place among the explanations and examples and the state of the demo are held by `app`, and `api` does not know them.**
- **What was decided:**
  - a lesson run is a row of `api`'s database: the run ID, the profile ID, the lesson's full ID, the module ID and its version, the package digest, the engine choice that gave the lesson, the time of the beginning and of the last action, the end reason and time. The beginning, the end reason and the end time, once recorded, do not change. The term is the same as `app`'s (sources 8.1). The attempt of a lesson's task carries the run ID and the segment place, from 1. An open run is one per profile and lesson, held by the unique partial index `lesson_runs (profile_id, lesson_id) WHERE ended_at IS NULL`, and a second simultaneous request rereads the row and gives the open one; one choice opens one run — `choice_id` is unique. There may be several open runs of different lessons;
  - the beginning. A run is begun by `app`'s request for the open view of the lesson that the engine choice named, and the choice itself begins nothing. If the lesson has an open run, `api` gives it. A new one begins on the package of the current version, if the module is connected and it has a current version that suits the profile, the lesson is in it, and the lesson and each of its tasks suit the profile's age category. There is no other way to a new run: the engine chooses a lesson or a task, and `app` has no list of lessons (sources 8.1);
  - the answer is the run ID, the lesson's open view whole, the package digest, the places of the task segments that have ended, and the segment's open attempt, if there is one. `api` gives an open run again by its ID — after a restart and on another device —, and `app` gets the profile's open runs and its open attempt with the profile: so a device without the state of the lesson finds them too. A request for a lesson without an open run does not begin a new one: the refusal names how the last one ended;
  - the check is full, every time `api` gives a lesson and begins a segment's attempt: the module — by the current version, and the lesson and each of its tasks — by the run's package, the one the learner sees. If it fails, the run ends "not suitable", and its open attempt is closed with the reason "not suitable". A lesson that does not suit a new run, or that the current version does not have, is a refusal without the open view, and the engine does not get such lessons at all. "Lesson passed" does not serve as a check: it brings nothing to the device, and the run records what was;
  - the task segment. A segment's attempt begins only if the run is open, every task segment before it has ended, and the segment itself has not; an open attempt of the segment is given back the same. It is an attempt on the run's package, with its own seed, the same as outside a lesson: a new one closes another open one with the reason "left", and a new attempt of the same segment is a new seed, and for a template perhaps another instance. The quota and closed AI concern only a task that needs platform AI: if what is left is not enough for a typical task, the refusal "quota" with the time of the next day; if AI is closed, a refusal with a cause, and the run stays open. A segment of a task that the code will not begin — on a workbook, with handwriting without admission to AI, or with a rubric where the admission gate does not admit both calls of an essay — is not checked by the lesson when it opens: the attempt of such a segment is "not admitted", and the run stays open (Д26);
  - a segment has ended when its attempt is closed "correct" or "solution shown", and the segment of a task with a rubric when its attempt is closed "done". "Left" and "abandoned" do not end it — on return the segment begins a new attempt —, and neither does the submission verdict "unchecked": the attempt waits. The close reason "unchecked" — the version is withdrawn or outside the window — ends the run "unchecked" in the same transaction, by a database trigger, by whatever path the attempt was closed. At the end of a segment `api` answers what next: the place of the next segment of any kind — an explanation, an example or a task —, or "no more segments", and the answer of "Done" names it as the answer of a correct one does; `app` keeps the places among the explanations itself;
  - the run end reasons:
    - "passed" — `app` sent "lesson passed" with the run ID, and all task segments have ended. Before that, a refusal with the places of the segments that have ended. If the run has already ended, the answer names the reason, and for "passed" this is not an error: "lesson passed" may come twice too. The package check on request goes first, so on a withdrawn version the run ends "unchecked";
    - "unchecked" — a lesson's attempt was closed with this reason, or the job after a deployment and the check on request found a run on a package that the image no longer gives;
    - "not suitable" — the full check failed;
    - "abandoned" — 30 days without actions. An action is any request about the run and its attempts: the lesson's open view, a segment's attempt and its requests, "lesson passed"; a run's last action is the later of its own and its attempt's, and reading a segment's attempt does not move it; closing by a job does not count as an action. The check on request and the step `abandoned_runs` of the operator's daily job `api sweep` close it, as `abandoned_attempts` closes attempts, and the closing does not move the profile's activity date;
    - "early" is only a place for an early end of a lesson by a new version of the engine: the contract has no such reason, and `api` does not end a lesson early;
  - the engine. When the request itself ended a run — "lesson passed" or a check that found the end —, `api` calls the engine with the skill that the last choice holds, whatever code that choice came with, and the choice goes into `RunEnd`: of the answer "lesson passed" or of the data of the refusal "run ended". A repeat of "lesson passed" gives the same choice, and a run that ended before the request carries no choice: `app` asks for "something else". If a run ended by a job, the engine is called when the learner asks. At the end of a segment the engine is not called: segments go in the author's order, and the engine of the first slice has neither consolidation within a lesson nor an early end of one, and the contract has no such end reason. At the engine's input are the profile's runs — the lesson, its skills, the end reason, empty for an open one, and the end time —, while the rule does not read the places of ended segments, and `api` does not pass them: a call holds as much data as the rule reads. Lessons whose task segment the code will not begin do not go to the engine (Д26);
  - the module version: an open run goes on its own package, and the lesson's skills go to the engine from this package, mapped by `retired_skills` of the current version. A lesson whose run has ended is, by a new choice, a new run on the current version;
  - if the quota ran out mid-lesson, the waiting is not recorded separately: the run is open, and the segment's attempt waits or has not begun. A request for another task closes only the attempt — "left" —, and the next day the lesson continues. Beginning a run needs no AI: explanations and examples are read without it;
  - if the module was disconnected with an open run, the run ends by the general rules, and the engine does not give it;
  - `api` does not know the place among the explanations and examples and the state of the demo: `app` holds them and after a break returns the learner to where they were (sources 8.1). A worked example is not evidence, and data are kept to a minimum;
  - a run has no evidence of its own: a segment's attempt gives evidence, as outside a lesson;
  - in the table of what lies where the row "lesson runs": the term — while the profile lives, into the export — all the fields, deletion — with the profile. An attempt also carries the run ID and the segment place. Neither the ID nor the lesson is written into the logs, and nothing of a run goes to AI: a lesson adds no call of `tutor`.

— *Why:*
- **A run outlives leaving.** `app` keeps the place and the state of the demo by the key "lesson ID, package digest" and, when `api` gives the lesson again on the same package, opens it at that place (sources 8.1). A run that ended by leaving would take from the learner the lesson's tasks they have already solved — because of one task on the side or a quota that ran out in the evening. One run for all devices — because the lesson's attempts are at `api` anyway, and another device opens the lesson after the last segment that has ended.
- **"Lesson passed" from `app`.** By the end of the last task, a lesson after which an explanation comes, as `gaps-and-posts.yaml` and `first-sentences.yaml`, would end unread, and a lesson without tasks, as `reading-model-essays.yaml`, would never end (`spec/format/examples/lesson/valid/`). `app` decided so too, and `api` matches "lesson passed" against its attempts: by the format only the server ends a task segment.
- **30 days.** That long `app` keeps the state of a lesson without changes (sources 8.1). A lesson is short — the lesson on its own package rests on that —, and a run without a term would go on a version months old, while the engine and the adult would see "in progress" lessons that will not be returned to. An attempt's term is 7 days, because the words of hints live with it, while a run holds no content, and a week's break does not cut a lesson off.
- **"Not suitable".** A lesson goes only to a learner whom both the lesson and each of its tasks suit: otherwise a task only for adults would reach a child in a lesson where nothing is skipped. The category is confirmed once a year, and when the country changes, the age is given again, so a lesson's task may stop suiting mid-lesson. Check every time a lesson is given and a segment begins — because the lesson's tasks lie ahead.
- **Beginning by request and the run ID.** The engine chooses after each attempt, and a choice that the learner did not open must not leave an open run. A new run on the same digest — after "passed" on one device, "abandoned" or "not suitable" — `app`, by the single key "lesson ID, package digest", would open at the place of the former one, past task segments that have not ended in the new one, and it does not open a task segment again: the learner would be stuck. The run ID tells them apart.
- **No call of the engine at a segment.** The engine does not reorder segments, and in the first slice a call at each segment would decide nothing (sources 8.1).

— *Rejected:*
- one open run per profile, which leaving for another task or lesson ends "left": it diverges from how `app` keeps a lesson, and the learner would lose the lesson's tasks they have already solved;
- a run without a term: open runs would pile up, a lesson would go on a version months old, and the format window changes rarely;
- a term of 7 days, as for an attempt: it is shorter than the term of the state of a lesson in `app`, and a week's break would cut a lesson off;
- "passed" that `api` infers from the end of the last task, and the place in the lesson at `api`: `app` rejected both — the arguments are above, and the place at `api` would also be a request and a row of the learner's data on each "Continue";
- checking whether a lesson suits only at its beginning: a task only for adults would reach a child;
- closing the open attempt of a lesson that stopped suiting with the reason "left": that is the name of the profile's leaving for another attempt;
- beginning a run by the engine choice itself: each choice that was not opened would stay an open run;
- a run without an ID, by `app`'s key: a new run on the same digest would open at someone else's place;
- calling the engine after each attempt of a lesson so that it answers "next segment": a call without a decision;
- the places of ended segments at the engine's input: its rule does not read them;
- asking the format that a published lesson ID name no other lesson, as for a trap: the format promises this for skills, traps and anchor skills, while a task's key changes under the same ID with a major version, and a run holds the digest of its package anyway.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/model/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Task with a rubric

**R14. The attempt of a task with a rubric is open for rewriting and ends by the learner's "Done" — a new close reason "done", which also ends the lesson's segment. A submission with a rubric ends with feedback, "unreadable" or "unchecked", not with a verdict. Feedback that did not pass the feedback rule or the leak check is replaced by the phrase of `app` "no feedback this time", without repeating the call.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the path is two calls of `tutor` in one request: reading the essay by one reader, then assessing the read text by the criteria of the rubric, without the picture. Before reading, `api` takes a reserve for reading together with the assessment: one charge kept as two parts, and an essay not read gives the assessment's part back;
  - a submission with a rubric ends with one of three, and this is not a verdict — an item with a rubric has none:
    - feedback — the assessment returned at least one criterion with an assessment. A criterion's mark is "met", "not met" or "not assessed", the platform's words, and not "not yet": that is the name of a value of a verdict. Not assessed is a criterion that is absent from the model's answer or that is named twice, and `api` does not repeat the call. `AssessEssay` of `tutor` resolves such a criterion itself: it gives one mark for each criterion in the rubric's order, with none for such a one, and `api` reads it, or one past the end of what came, as "not assessed";
    - "unreadable" — reading said that the sheet as a whole cannot be read. The assessment is not called, and its reserve is returned, and the learner sees a request to rewrite the sheet — the same text of `app` as for the verdict "unreadable";
    - "unchecked" — an AI failure, an exhausted quota, the admission refusal "not admitted", a provider's refusal by a limit, or an assessment that returned no criterion with an assessment. The learner sees a neutral phrase, and for the quota also the time of the next day. The attempt waits, as for any task.

    "Unreadable" and "unchecked" mean here the same as the verdicts of the same name — it is not the learner's error —, but they stand at a submission, not at an answer slot and an item. The three ends of a submission have no common name: "result" in the platform's vocabulary already means both feedback and the end of an attempt;
  - feedback passes the leak check — for a rubric it searches only for the task's leak phrases — and the feedback rule, by the code of `tutor`. If it passed, it goes to the device. If it did not, the learner sees the phrase of `app` "no feedback this time", and the call is not repeated. This is not the neutral phrase of "unchecked": that one says there is no check, and here there was an assessment, and only the words did not pass. The marks are recorded then too;
  - what goes to the device is the read text, if there was reading, and the words of the feedback or a note that there are none. The justifications, the marks and the criteria do not go while the attempt is open: the learner sees feedback, not an assessment, and the criteria are closed until the task is solved;
  - the attempt is open for rewriting: the learner corrects the same sheet and sends it again, and each submission is a new reserve and two calls again, and the quota holds the spending. The assessment gets neither the earlier feedback nor the earlier text: they are not on the server. A new submission after a lost answer is a new submission like any other. But a repeat of the request with the same idempotency key does not call AI again: `api` answers with what it recorded — how the submission ended —, without what was read and the words of the feedback, which it does not store, so a network failure does not spend the quota twice;
  - closing. "Done" is a learning action, `can_learn`. `api` accepts it after at least one submission of the attempt, however that ended, and before that refuses it with the cause "no submission". The attempt is closed with the reason "done" in one transaction, without evidence (О-17), and the answer is the task solved — the criteria of the rubric in order, without the marks, and the solution, if there is one — with what comes next: in a lesson the next segment, which "done" ends, and outside a lesson the engine's choice, as after any attempt. A repeat on an attempt closed "done" gets the same answer by the state of the attempt; one closed otherwise is "attempt closed". "Done" after "unreadable" or "unchecked" is the way out for a learner whose essay cannot be read and for one whose quota has run out. "Left", "abandoned", "unchecked" on a withdrawn version or outside the window, and "not suitable" close an attempt with a rubric like any other, open neither the criteria nor the solution, and none of them gives evidence;
  - two devices. `api` refuses a submission to a closed attempt before calling AI. If the attempt was closed while its submission was going, how the submission ended is not recorded: the answer carries the feedback from the request's memory and says that the attempt is closed, and the quota is counted as for any call;
  - the lesson. The segment of a task with a rubric ends when its attempt is closed "done". "Left" and "abandoned" do not end it, as for any segment;
  - there is no ladder, no showing the solution and no "explain differently". "Hint" gets the same as a request before the first wrong answer: the IDs of the task's techniques, empty if there are none, and `app` shows the "givens and unknown" from the task's open view. The empty list is the answer that there is nothing to hint;
  - the quota: `api` begins a new task with a rubric only if what is left is enough for a typical task with a rubric — two readings with an assessment, ≈ $0.06 —, and this is a configuration value of its own. An own key does not spend the quota;
  - logs — without IDs and without content: the request's line names the check that turned the feedback down by a closed set — `empty`, `leak` or `form` —, as for the words of the hints. By it one sees how often the phrase "no feedback" replaces feedback.

— *Why:*
- **"Done", not the first feedback.** Feedback says what worked and what to work on, by the criteria, and rewriting is part of writing: in the prototype the essay goes "Plan, Write, Improve" (sources 9.1), and the example graph of the module of essays has the skill `improving-an-essay` (`spec/format/examples/skill-graph/valid/short-essays.yaml`). If the first feedback closed the attempt, in a lesson it would be impossible to rewrite after it: a segment's attempt begins only while the segment has not ended. And feedback lost in the network or that did not pass the rule could not be returned: the server does not store its words. "Done" — after any submission, so that a learner whose essay cannot be read, or whose quota has run out, is not stuck on the segment: a task with a rubric has no showing the solution, by which the segment of a task with a key ends.
- **The phrase "no feedback".** The format gives a rubric no fallback text: fallback texts belong to the rungs of the ladder, after a wrong value, and a template of the platform would have to be translated into the language of each module — the platform moved away from that, giving the templates of rungs to the module. A repeat is one more call of the assessment beyond the reserve and one more wait, while an essay at Opus 5.5 · `high` takes ≈ 12 s as it is. Its feedback broke the feedback rule in 2 essays of 45 (sources 9.1), so the learner will see the phrase "no feedback" rarely, and the attempt is open, and they can send the essay again themselves.
- **A criterion without an assessment — without a repeat**, at the same price of a repeat. The mark "not assessed" is more honest than a mark made at random.
- **"Unreadable" — only the sheet as a whole.** A single illegible word must not drive the whole essay to rewriting — the same argument by which a sheet under a rubric is read by one reader.
- **"Done", not "assessed".** The reason names the learner's action, not the assessment, which the learner must not see.

— *Rejected:*
- the first feedback closes the attempt, and rewriting is a new attempt of the same task: in a lesson it is impossible to rewrite after feedback, feedback lost or that did not pass the rule cannot be returned, and for a template a new attempt is a new seed and perhaps another topic;
- "Done" only after feedback or two "unreadable" in a row: the rule is more complex, and with an exhausted quota the segment would wait until tomorrow;
- "Done" before the first submission: the learner would skip the essay without sending a single sheet. After a submission they can end the attempt even with an empty sheet that gave "unreadable" — but an adult sees this in the attempt's submissions, and one whose essay cannot be read has no other way out;
- one reason for any attempt with feedback, however it was closed — by "Done", by leaving or by the term: the reason would have two meanings. A learner who left sends the essay again on return — it is on the device (sources 9.1) —, and for a template writes a new one: a new attempt is a new seed and perhaps another topic, as a task with a key is solved again on return;
- one repeat of the assessment when feedback did not pass the rule: the arguments are above;
- a fallback text of the module — a blank of feedback or an advice at a criterion: format v0 has none, and this is a change of the format of `spec`;
- showing the criteria with marks instead of feedback: the learner would see an assessment, not feedback, and the criteria are closed;
- "unreadable" as an item's verdict: an item with a rubric has no verdict;
- a refusal "nothing to hint": the empty list of techniques says it, as before the first wrong answer of any task.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/quota/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/logging/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R15. Of an attempt of a task with a rubric the server stores how each submission ended, and for a submission with feedback, the mark of each criterion and the source of the words. It stores neither the essay's text, nor the justifications, nor the words of the feedback: they live in the request's memory and on the device, and the essay itself is on the server only as a stored work, while its storage is on for the profile. The marks are seen by an adult in progress when the attempt is closed, and the learner sees the feedback.**
- **What was decided:**
  - an attempt with a rubric is the same row as any attempt: the package digest, the module and its version, the task, the close reason and the times, and for a lesson's segment, the run ID and the place. It has no fields of the ladder: a rubric has no wrong answers, rungs or showing the solution;
  - each submission has its time and how it ended: with feedback, "unreadable" or "unchecked". Feedback has the mark of each criterion by its number in the rubric — "met", "not met" or "not assessed" —, and the source of the words: the model or the phrase "no feedback", as the source of the words of hints. A criterion has no ID, its number in order names it, and the number is read by the attempt's package digest. In code, by migration `0020`, a submission of an essay is a row of `submissions` with `essay_end`, `marks` by the numbers of the criteria and `words_source`, and no observation, by checks of the table; its `answer` and the key's record hold the answer without the text read and the feedback;
  - not stored — neither in the database, nor in the repeat queue, nor in the cache of answers or of idempotency, nor in the logs — are the crop of the sheet, the read text of the essay, the justifications and the words of the feedback. They live in the request's memory, and what was read and the feedback also on the device, with the essay (sources 9.1). An essay has no observation row, and the words of the feedback, unlike the words of hints, do not live even until the end of the attempt;
  - the essay itself is kept on the server only at the account owner's choice: as a stored work — its strokes and its picture, as the device holds them, of the kind "essay" by the task's rubric — while whoever consents for the learner keeps the storage of the profile's works on, with deletion in one step. What was read and the words of the feedback stay off the server then too;
  - the term is while the profile lives. Everything listed goes into the export — the marks and the source as `RubricSubmission`, a criterion by the task ID, the module version and the number —, and the export is read by the package of an official module, which is open. It is deleted with the profile, with its attempts;
  - who reads the marks. The learner — no: they see feedback, not an assessment, and the list of criteria with marks does not go to them. An adult — in progress, when the attempt is closed: for one closed "done", the essay is finished, and for one closed otherwise, the rewriting was abandoned. The open attempt gives the time and the end of each submission, and progress the same with the marks once the attempt is closed. A task with a rubric is solved when its attempt is closed "done": then its criteria and its solution, if it has one, are open, and the adult reads what stands behind a number; "left", "abandoned", "unchecked" and "not suitable" do not open them, and the adult sees the marks by the numbers of the criteria, while the feedback that `app` keeps with the essay on the device says what to work on, by the criteria. What to develop the platform derives from the learner model, and rubric feedback does not go into it (О-17): the marks in progress stand beside it;
  - against the platform's privacy document:
    - the marks are pseudonymous data, an opinion of AI on the learner's writing. They hold no content of the work;
    - the promise "What the learner writes by hand stays on the device" holds: neither the text nor the crop is on the server but by the owner's choice of storage. Feedback may quote the essay, so it is not stored either;
    - what goes to AI is what the document names: for reading, the crop of the sheet; for the assessment, the read text without the picture, and the criteria of the rubric. Nothing of what is stored goes: the assessment does not get the earlier feedback;
    - into the logs — neither the text, nor the feedback, nor the justifications, nor marks with IDs;
    - since the server does not store the text, nobody on the platform will see a child's disclosure, in an essay, of harm to themselves or from others: the platform does not look for signs of harm, and a learner under 18 is always shown where to turn. Keeping real names out of the work is what `app` reminds of;
  - the repeat of the engine choice: the close reason and time of an attempt, once recorded, do not change, so the repeat restores the closed attempts of tasks with a rubric by the time of the choice.

— *Why:* progress needs the marks — what to develop —, and does not need the text and the words. There is no need to repeat feedback from the observation: a verdict is repeated, and feedback has none, — so what was read, which the database keeps with the profile for the sake of repeating a verdict, an essay does not need. Otherwise the server would keep an essay without the owner's choice, while the learner's artifacts reach the server only by that choice. The marks go to an adult after "done", because the learner sees feedback, not an assessment, and to an adult they say what to work on. The essay is stored by the owner's choice because otherwise the only copy of a learner's work depends on the browser's rules.

— *Rejected:*
- everything, as for any attempt — the read text, the words of the feedback and the assessment: the server would keep an essay without the owner's choice;
- only the fact of the attempt: progress would have nothing to show;
- the words of the feedback until the end of the attempt, as the words of hints: nothing reads them, and the quotes of the essay in them are its text;
- the marks only in the export: they would not serve progress, for whose sake they are stored;
- the marks to the learner too: a list of marks is closer to an assessment than to feedback.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/export/`; `internal/deletion/`; `internal/rpc/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.6.6

**R16. A move to a tag of `spec` closes the rows of table 3 that the tag answers and carries the answers into the documents in words; an addition to a row that `spec` did not see goes as a new row. What a tag asks of learners' data the move does not decide: a task of its own decides it.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the answers of `spec` to the rows of table 3 go into `docs/architecture.md` and this log, said in words, and the rows they answer leave table 3 with the move;
  - `spec` records a row by its commit, and its inbox matches rows by number: an addition that a task made to the row after that commit `spec` did not see. Such an addition goes as a new row of its own, unless the tag already carries it;
  - what a tag asks of learners' data — such as storing essays by the account owner's choice — is a decision about learners' data, read against the platform's privacy document, not a step of the move: the move records it as a row of debt, and a task of its own decides it.

— *Why:* the documents of `api` say what is in force, and a row answered by `spec` is no longer open work. An addition that `spec` did not see would vanish from the register if its row closed whole. A move to a tag is a review of what the tag changed, and a decision about learners' data needs its own reading and its own review.

— *Rejected:*
- closing an answered row whole, with the additions `spec` did not see: they would vanish from the register;
- deciding what a tag asks of learners' data in the move itself: it is a decision about learners' data, not a move to a tag.

— *Consequence:* `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/decisions.md`.

## Protocol `app` ↔ `api`

**R17. The protocol `app` ↔ `api` is the contract of `spec`, the package `novatrail.api.v1` at the submodule's tag: it carries the messages, the services, the enumeration of causes and the refusals of each method, while the order of checks, the idempotency keys, CORS, the codecs, the interceptors and the serving of files are the code of `api`. A refusal is a Connect error with a standard code and a typed cause, an outcome is a field of a successful answer, and the causes are one enumeration. An action that a repeat must not do twice carries an idempotency key as a field of the message.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the requests are those of the first slice, which [architecture.md](architecture.md) lists at the level of meaning, without the names of messages and fields: what comes in, what goes out and what the learner sees. Beyond the learning flows they carry the profile's fields "ask before showing", the mode and voicing; the return to an open attempt on another device; three variants of beginning an attempt — an engine choice, a lesson's segment and the same task again; the module catalog apart from the connected modules; the request "something else", which calls the engine; an adult's progress. Where the contract decided otherwise than that list, the contract is right, and [architecture.md](architecture.md) names beside the list how they differ;
  - the boundary. In the contract — the messages and the services, the enumeration of causes, the refusals of each method — which causes it returns and with what code —, the idempotency level of the methods and the form of the path of a file of the device part with its answers. In the code of `api` — the order of checks, the rights of OpenFGA, the idempotency keys and their records, CORS, the codecs, the interceptors, the serving of files and the HTTP cache;
  - a learning request and an adult action on a profile carry the profile ID, also when the matter is an attempt, a run or a choice: `api` first checks the right to the profile, and reads them by the pair "profile ID, their ID", so another's and nonexistent ones get one refusal, "not found". An account is created by one request — the invite code and the owner's statement that they are an adult —, and before it any request of the account gets "no access";
  - refusal and outcome. If the action did not happen — a Connect error with a standard code and a cause with data: the places of ended segments, the time of the next day, the close reason of the attempt. If the action happened and it changes what the learner sees — a field of a successful answer. An exhausted quota does not refuse a submission — the verdict "unchecked" with the cause "quota" —, but it does not begin a new attempt or segment, and that is a refusal. "No other words" is an outcome: there was a call, and the right is spent; "already asked" is a refusal: there is no call;
  - the causes are one enumeration for refusals and outcomes: "quota" is a refusal at the beginning of an attempt or a segment and an outcome on a submission. Among them are "ladder not passed" — showing the solution to a child before the fourth wrong answer, "no showing" — at a task with a rubric, "no rung" — "explain differently" without a rung, "no more words" — the correct answer words a second time or after a new attempt, "not found", "already exists", "PIN locked", "PIN already set" — "set PIN" when there is one, "PIN not set", "answer not current", "segment unavailable" — a segment's attempt out of order, "key not for this profile", "key already used". The list and the Connect codes are the contract's;
  - what `app` does by the code: `unauthenticated` — a re-sign-in, by the cause; `unimplemented` — only an offer to reload; `unavailable` — a temporary refusal, and `app` repeats it with a growing pause, but not for long, and then shows a failure; the rest `app` shows in its own words by the cause. A cause that its build does not know it reads by the code: in binary protobuf it arrives as a number;
  - the re-sign-in. "Fresh sign-in needed" is `unauthenticated` with the allowed age of the sign-in in seconds, "account gone" is `unauthenticated` without data. The re-sign-in is a sign-in of the same user, without signing out: signing out erases everything of the account on the device (sources 11.5), and a build that signed in again through signing out would erase the device. `app` reads the cause before any refresh of the token: without a cause, it refreshes the token and repeats the request, and opens the sign-in window only if the refresh failed; on "fresh sign-in needed" it signs in again by the window, and on "account gone" it erases everything of the account and signs out, without refreshing the token: for a deleted user the refresh would end with the SDK's sign-out (sources 11.1);
  - the idempotency key is a field of the message, not a header: CORS allows only `Authorization` and the headers of Connect. It is needed by the beginning of an attempt, a submission, the words of a rung and the correct answer words, "explain differently", showing the solution, the request "something else", and the requests of the PIN and of consent. `api` writes it at the beginning of the request, in the same transaction as the reserve of the quota or the action itself, with the method and the target — an attempt, a run or, for "something else", the profile; the same key with another method or target is the refusal "key already used". A repeat while the first is going is the refusal "still going"; after it, an answer by what is recorded, and the repeat does not call AI and does not spend the quota a second time. "Lesson passed", "Done", the beginning of a step, "Hint" and the deletion of a profile and of the account repeat by the state, without a key: a repeat of the deletion of a profile gets "no profile", of the account "account gone", and `app` reads both as "deleted already";
  - what a repeat gets. The record of the beginning of an attempt holds the answer: it has nothing of what the learner sent. The record of a submission holds a reference to the submission, and a repeat gets its recorded answer, with the chosen options; for a submission with a rubric, how it ended, without content. The record of the words of a rung and of "explain differently" holds the answer without the words — the rung and the outcome —, and a repeat adds to it the words that the attempt stores while it is open, so the words live only with the attempt and go with its closing; a repeat when the wrong answer is no longer the last is "answer not current", and on a closed attempt "attempt closed". The correct answer words are stored nowhere: a repeat gets the answer without words, with the flag "repeat", and a lost answer is lost words;
  - the key's record — the key, the method, the profile ID, the target, a reference to what is recorded — a submission or a choice —, the state, the time and the request's fingerprint, SHA-256, without content. In code the common helper `rpc.Once` leads the keys of every service. A submission that calls AI goes by `rpc.Across`: its first transaction writes the record "going" with the checks of the attempt and commits it before the call, the second transaction after the call turns it into "done" with the submission, and a refusal after the call deletes it, so the repeat is checked again; no transaction is open for the time of the call. "Going" lives no longer than the request's time limit: a request that outlived it has crashed, its reserve stays spending, and the repeat goes again. The operator's daily retention job deletes records older than a day, so a record lives no longer than 2 days; the records are deleted with the profile and do not go into the export: this is a service field;
  - the engine choice. The answer by which an attempt outside a lesson ended — "correct", showing the solution or "Done" —, and the answer by which a lesson run ended, carries the next engine choice: by the result of an attempt, evidence, then the engine. In a lesson the answer to the end of a segment carries the place of the next segment or "no more segments". The request "something else" calls the engine itself — the learner asks for another task, chose a skill on the skill map or did not get an answer with a choice —, and closes the open attempt with the reason "left". The choice begins nothing: `app` begins an attempt or a run by it. Its reason code goes only into progress;
  - what goes to the device. With a task and in the answers of an attempt — never traps, neither the catalog nor the matched trap: the words of a rung in `api` get it. The key and the task's solution — only with an attempt closed by a correct answer or by showing the solution, or a task with a rubric solved by "Done". The slot checks, the skills, the band, the difficulty, the techniques — except the IDs of those given —, the simpler case, the leak phrases and the attempt seed — never, and the criteria of the rubric and the marks — not to the learner. `api` does not give an own key back. The marks and the repeating traps go to an adult as progress: it names a trap by `description` and `trap_advice`, as the module format requires, only by closed attempts;
  - for a connected module without a current version — for all or for this learner — `app` gets the mark "no current version", without a digest: the module stays in the list, it can be disconnected, and there are no attempts and lessons on it;
  - the data of the skill map are by profile and module: the state of the skills of the current version, as `learner-model` gives it, and `app` takes the graph and the tiers by the digest of the current version. A skill without a pair and a waiting skill are not on the map;
  - the rights: the module catalog, connecting and disconnecting a module — `can_learn`; the skill map to the learner — `can_learn`, progress to an adult — `can_view`; voicing is written by the learner too, `can_learn`, and only it; the own key check and the list of providers — `can_manage` of the owner profile of the category `adult`; the request of withdrawn digests — the account from the token.

— *Why:*
- **The boundary.** In the contract is what both sides know: `app` builds the request and reads the answer, and the stubs of the services are born in `spec`. `app` does not see the order of checks, the keys and CORS, and their place is the code of `api`. The refusals of a method are in the contract, because by them `app` decides what to show.
- **Refusal and outcome.** The Connect code says what `app` does with any refusal: signs in again, offers to reload, repeats or shows. The cause says what to show. An outcome is in the answer, because the learner sees it as an ordinary screen: "unchecked" is not a failure of the request.
- **The key as a field.** A header that is not among those CORS allows the browser would refuse already at the preflight (sources 10.2). A key recorded at the beginning of the request does not let a repeat, while the first waits for AI, spend the quota twice. The target in the record — so that a key repeated by mistake with another attempt does not get its answer. The correct answer words are not stored, and storing them for the sake of a repeat would mean keeping the words longer than they are needed.
- **The choice in the answer.** Evidence and the choice are one chain in `api`, and `learner-model` and `engine` are computations without input and output: the answer to the end of an attempt does not wait for AI, and the learner does not wait for one more request.
- **Traps.** The trap catalog is closed whole: by the module format, a trap tells what mistake a task expects, and the learner's app does not get it.
- **Rights.** Connecting is `can_learn`, because the learner connects the module, and on a shared device the server does not tell people apart. Progress is `can_view`, because at stage 5 a teacher reads it.

— *Rejected:*
- "result or refusal" as a field of each answer: it would duplicate the Connect errors; Connect codes without a cause: one code would cover a dozen different refusals;
- the idempotency key as a header: CORS will not let it through; a key on each request: "lesson passed", "Done", the beginning of a step and "Hint" repeat by the state anyway;
- storing the correct answer words for the sake of a repeat: they are stored nowhere;
- the matched trap in the answer to a submission: traps do not go to the device;
- the engine choice only by a separate request: an extra request after each attempt, while the engine is called by the result of an attempt;
- a re-sign-in through signing out: it would erase the device;
- deciding the protocol method by method: the common rules are decided once;
- the names of messages and fields in [architecture.md](architecture.md): there would be a second source of truth beside the contract.

— *Consequence:* `internal/rpc/`; `internal/server/`; `internal/idem/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R18. On the wire is binary protobuf, and the server accepts JSON too, with the proto names. The module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps go without a token, by Connect GET, and a file of the device part by HTTP GET by the package digest and the path, with answers of its own to a withdrawn version and to a deployment; what they give is cached for a day. `app` learns of a version withdrawal by a request of its own. CORS is on all paths and answers, and the interceptor's log is the method, the code, the cause and the time.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the wire is binary protobuf: `app` turns it on in the transport connect-web, which by default sends JSON. A new value of an enumeration — a cause, a close reason of an attempt — that a release of `spec` added to the package `v1`, binary protobuf carries to an earlier build of `app` as a number: a proto3 enumeration is open. In JSON the server writes the value's name, and connect-es skips an unknown name, and the field reads empty: a new close reason would look like "attempt open". The crops of the work are `bytes`, in JSON a base64 string, a third longer, and the crop of a handwriting check goes twice (sources 10.1);
  - the server accepts JSON too — for debugging. The standard JSON codec of connect-go writes the names in lowerCamelCase, so `api` sets its own — `protojson` with `UseProtoNames`, as the contracts of `spec` require, and with `DiscardUnknown`, as the standard one (sources 10.1);
  - without a token — the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps: methods with the idempotency level `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`, and `app` takes them by Connect GET without headers — and without `Connect-Timeout-Ms`, `app` sets no deadline for these calls. Such a GET goes without a preflight, is cached by HTTP and carries no profile ID. A change of the level is a breaking change for `buf breaking` `FILE` (sources 10.1);
  - the open view in the contract is the bytes of the format's JSON and the format version beside them: `api` and `app` read it by the format's types, and by the version `app` knows whether it will read the view;
  - a file of the device part is not a Connect method but an HTTP GET by the package digest and the file's path, without a token and headers: such a `fetch` goes without a preflight and through the HTTP cache (sources 10.2). The answers are `200` with the file; `404` — the part of this package has no such path; `410` — the image no longer gives the version: it is withdrawn or outside the window; `503` with `Retry-After: 30` — a digest that the image's index does not know, that is, a deployment is going on. The form of the path and these answers are the contract's;
  - the HTTP cache. The answer `200` of an open view and of a file carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400`, a day: by the digest they do not change, and a withdrawal reaches learners only with a pull request of `api` anyway. A file also carries an `ETag` — the digest of the file itself —, so a repeat with `If-None-Match` gets `304`. A refusal is `no-store`;
  - the version withdrawal is a request of its own, "which of these digests does the image no longer give", from the account from the token, without a profile ID. The answer for each digest is "gives", "no longer gives" or "does not know": a digest that the image's index does not know is a deployment, not a withdrawal. `app` sends the request at start — with the digests whose open views it keeps —, and after the refusal "version withdrawn" or the answer `410`, deletes the open views of "no longer gives", and asks about the unknown ones later;
  - CORS — when `api` is on its own origin: the exact origin of `app` from the configuration, on all paths and all answers, errors too; the methods `GET` and `POST`; the headers — `Authorization`, `Content-Type`, `Connect-Protocol-Version` and `Connect-Timeout-Ms`; `Access-Control-Max-Age` — 7200 s, the cap of Chromium (sources 10.2); `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` — `Retry-After`: it is not in the safelist of response headers. On the origin of `app` CORS is not needed;
  - logs: the Connect interceptor writes the method, the code, the cause of a refusal and the time. It writes neither the body, nor the headers, nor the address of the request: in the body are the work and the learner's data, in a header the token, and in a GET's address the message. A request of a file writes its own row, `package_file` — the status and the time, without the address: the Connect interceptor does not see it, and the request logs of Cloud Run are excluded.

— *Why:* the libraries' own behaviour decides the wire (sources 10.1): connect-web sends JSON by default, connect-es ignores unknown JSON fields and with them unknown names of an enumeration, in binary form protobuf-es keeps an open enumeration as a number, and adding a value to an enumeration is safe in JSON only when every client emits numbers; `bytes` in JSON is base64. The JSON codec of connect-go has no `UseProtoNames`, and its fields can be set before use. Connect GET needs no special headers and is made to be cached, and the server of connect-go serves GET only to methods `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`; the buf rule on the idempotency level is in the category `FILE`. `Authorization` is not in the safelist of headers, a request with it goes with a preflight, and the preflight cache is by the exact address; a `fetch` without a cache mode of its own goes through the HTTP cache (sources 10.2). A cache for a day keeps a withdrawn version in the browser no longer than a withdrawal takes to reach the image anyway.

— *Rejected:*
- JSON on the wire: the arguments are above. JSON with numbers instead of the names of enumerations: the names would not be readable in JSON either, and base64 would stay;
- open views by POST: when `api` is on its own origin, each method comes with its own preflight, and POST has no HTTP cache; open views by token: they protect no learner's data, and the token would bring a preflight on each address;
- the open view as `google.protobuf.Struct`: without types, and the numbers in it are `double`; as messages of its own: beside the format's schemas there would be a second schema in `.proto`;
- a withdrawal inferred from a refusal to give an open view: `app` takes open views from the cache and does not reach `api` with them; `404` for a withdrawn version — cannot be told from an error of the path; an answer without CORS — `fetch` would not tell it from a network failure;
- an HTTP cache for a year, as for an immutable file: a withdrawn version would live in the browser's cache longer than a withdrawal takes.

— *Consequence:* `internal/rpc/`; `internal/server/`; `internal/catalog/`; `internal/packages/`; `internal/logging/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R19. There is no real-time channel in the first slice (Т-25): the check of handwriting is two requests, the submission with the verdict and then the tutor's words, and an essay is one request. Come back to the channel together with voice, at stage 3 (Т-13).**
- **What was decided:**
  - the check of handwriting is two requests: the submission and the verdict, then the words of the rung, for which `app` sends the work once more. A tapped choice is one request, without AI. An essay is one request: the reading and the assessment, and the answer is the read text and the feedback;
  - how long the learner waits is measured on the first app (measurements 2.1, 2.2): the verdict, the submission as a whole, p50 9.3 s in the first run and 5.3 s in the second, of which the reading by the pair p50 6.7 s and 3.0 s; the words of a rung p50 10.6 s, "explain differently" p50 8.7 s; the second request without a call 1.2 s — the overhead that a channel would win. The time of the reading changes noticeably from run to run, with how long the models of the pair think. An essay is ≈ 12 s by one request (sources 10.3), and there is nothing to split it into: it has no verdict;
  - the overhead is a property of the local run: `app` and `api` on one machine, and the preflight is local, while the token check, the database and the AI provider are over the network, as in a deployment, and each request with the database goes from the author's machine to `aws-us-east-1`.

— *Why:* a channel would bring the verdict no earlier than the answer of the first request, and the words earlier only by the overhead of the second request, ≈ 1 s; one answer with both would make the verdict wait for the words too. The verdict and the words measured are two to three times the estimate by the measurements of `tutor` (sources 10.3), and the argument holds for the same reason: the verdict does not wait for the words. canvas-api rejected giving everything without a channel (sources 12.1), and here the facts are other:
- the tutor's words go out whole, after the leak check: there is no stream of words to `app`, and a channel would carry only events;
- the reading and the analysis are two calls, and the verdict is ready after the reading: the answer of the first request is the early verdict, and a channel would carry one event — the same one;
- the rules of the contracts do not allow streams, and the platform has no queues or event bus of its own;
- canvas-api's own argument — on Cloud Run background work after the answer gets almost no processor, so everything goes inside the request (sources 10.4) — holds here too: each of the two requests does its work inside itself.

That canvas-api went without Centrifugo in one of its epics is no argument: it keeps Centrifugo in the stack, and that epic lacked it only because it had no beta (sources 10.4).

— *Rejected:*
- an event channel, like Centrifugo in canvas-api: a service of its own and a subscription for the sake of one event, which comes anyway as the answer of the first request;
- one answer with the verdict and the words: the verdict would wait for the words;
- a stream in the contract: the rules of the contracts allow only unary methods.

— *Consequence:* `internal/rpc/`; `internal/learning/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Deletion and export

**R20. A fresh sign-in is an `auth_time` no older than 5 minutes: it is required by the deletion of a profile, the deletion of the account and the change of a forgotten PIN. The token check tells apart "expired", "invalid", "revoked", "no such user", "disabled" and "failure", and "fresh sign-in needed" and "account gone" are refusals with the code `unauthenticated` and a cause of their own.**
- **What was decided:**
  - a fresh sign-in is an `auth_time` of a token that passed the check no older than 300 s by the clock of `api`. An `auth_time` ahead of the clock of `api` by no more than 300 s is fresh too: this is the same tolerance that the Admin SDK gives the token's expiry (sources 11.1). A fresh sign-in is required by three actions — the deletion of a profile, the deletion of the account and the change of a forgotten PIN. The export and the other adult actions do not require it, and the PIN is not a confirmation;
  - the class of a token check refusal:
    - an expired token and the token of a disabled user — `unauthenticated` without a cause: the SDK will refresh the token or sign out by itself, and `api` does not disable users;
    - an invalid one — a token that the SDK did not accept: broken, empty, of another project or signed not by a Google key, `IsIDTokenInvalid` for a token that is not expired, not revoked and whose user is not disabled — `unauthenticated` without a cause, as an expired one;
    - a revoked one: `api` reads its UID by `VerifyIDToken`, by Google's keys from the cache, without `GetUser`: if the account is marked "being deleted", this is "account gone", otherwise `unauthenticated` without a cause;
    - no such user — "account gone", and if the UID has an account without the mark — the user was deleted bypassing `api`, for example in the project's console —, `api` deletes the account by the route of its deletion, without a fresh sign-in and in this same request: its first step, while the step at the sign-in service is done already. What did not pass the operator's job finishes;
    - a failure — only what kept the check from ending: Google's keys that cannot be got, the network, a 5xx answer of the sign-in service. Its answer is `unavailable`, and a forged token does not get it: `app` would repeat the request.

    The errors of the SDK are not wrapped before the helpers `Is…`;
  - the order of the checks: the class of the token, then the mark "being deleted" — on any request, the creation of the account and the redemption of a code too —, then the checks of every request, and the fresh sign-in last, after the right;
  - "fresh sign-in needed" is `unauthenticated`, and in the data the allowed age of the sign-in in seconds, 300, as `max_age` of RFC 9470 (sources 11.2). Freshness is checked only by the three actions above;
  - "account gone" is `unauthenticated`, without data, on any request with a token: when the account is marked "being deleted", when the sign-in service answers that there is no such user, and when the token is revoked and the mark is there.
