- **The table of methods** (`internal/rpc/methods.go`) says for each method of the contract: whether a token is needed — only the four open views of `PackageService` and `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` go without it; whether an account is needed — only `CreateAccount` goes without it; which right is checked on the message's profile — by the method's right; whether fresh sign-in is needed — `DeleteProfile`, `DeleteAccount` and `ResetPin`. A test holds the table equal to the server's methods, a right on those and only those methods whose request carries `profile_id`, and "no token" on those that are `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`. The server does not serve a method without a row. Showing the solution and the skill map have a double right — `can_learn` for the learner, `can_manage` or `can_view` for an adult: the table checks `can_learn`, which in the first slice the owner always has, and the handler checks the right that its request names.
- **`GetLegalTexts`** is cached for an hour at most, and no longer than until 00:00 UTC of the nearest day of a next version it gives.
- **The interceptor of the checks**, in this order:
  1. the token from `Authorization: Bearer`; if there is none, `unauthenticated` without a cause;
  2. `authn.Port.Verify` and the refusal class by the classes of refusal. A revoked token of a marked account and the token of a user that does not exist are "account gone"; a failure is `unavailable`; the rest is `unauthenticated` without a cause;
  3. the account by the UID: a mark is "account gone" on any method, `CreateAccount` too; a UID without an account is "no access", `permission_denied`, but for `CreateAccount`;
  4. the date of the last request;
  5. the right — on the message, when the wrapped `Receive` has received it, before the handler and before any reading by the profile ID: an ID that is not a UUID in the form the database writes it — lowercase, with hyphens —, OpenFGA's "no" for the right and a profile row that is not there under the account are one refusal, "no profile", `not_found`. The interceptor reads the profile row after the right — so that a dangling tuple gives no right to anything, and fresh sign-in stays last;
  6. fresh sign-in last: "fresh sign-in needed" with `max_auth_age_seconds` 300.

  Who passed the checks — the UID, the sign-in time and the account ID — the handler takes from `rpc.CallerFrom`. A refusal is `rpc.Refuse`: a Connect error with a code and the detail `Refusal`, and the text is the cause's name. A failure of a port or the database is, to the client, a code without a text, and to the log, the error's kind. Step 5, "consent given", is the helper `rpc.RequireConsent` for the handlers of the learning requests: if the profile has no confirmed consent, "no consent", `failed_precondition`.
- **"No user, and the account has no mark"**: the user was deleted bypassing `api`. The interceptor deletes the account as `DeleteAccount` does, its mark first, and answers "account gone"; if the mark was not put, `unavailable`, and a repeat puts it.
- **The request's deadline** is `API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 30 s by default, and that of `FindTasks`, `ReadStatement` and `SubmitWork` `API_PAPER_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 90 s, each less than `API_HTTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT`: the answer of a request cut off by the deadline still has time to go out. The client's deadline from `Connect-Timeout-Ms` is taken if it is shorter. The calls of the ports obey it — the Firebase SDK repeats a call up to 4 times and waits up to two minutes —, as do the record "going" of an idempotency key and the job's completion of the marks.
- **The date of the last request** is `store.TouchAccount` on a request that passed the class and the mark, when the date read together with the account is not today by UTC: the other requests of the day make no write, and the `UPDATE` itself, with `last_request_on IS DISTINCT FROM` today's date, writes the row no more than once a day even when two requests race. The `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` of this `UPDATE` does not hinder writes under the account, and it conflicts with the `FOR UPDATE` of the mark, as it should.
- **CORS** is a wrapper of the whole handler: on all paths and all answers, errors, `404` and the `500` of a panic too, the headers are set at the moment the answer begins, so the clearing of the answer's headers on a panic does not take them away. The origin is exact, `API_APP_ORIGIN`, in the form a browser writes it: the host lowercase, without the default port, without a path and `*`, and in a deployment only `https`; if it is not set, `api` stands on the origin of `app`, and CORS is not needed. Preflight is `204`, the methods `GET` and `POST`, the headers `Authorization`, `Content-Type`, `Connect-Protocol-Version` and `Connect-Timeout-Ms`, `Max-Age` 7200, and the answers have `Expose-Headers: Retry-After`.
- **The log** is the event `request` of the log's table: the method is the contract's procedure name, the Connect code, the cause is the name of the `Cause`, the token's refusal class, the time in milliseconds, and for a failure the error's kind. No body, no headers, no address, no UID, token or ID: the values of the method and the cause are forms of the contract's names, the code and the class are closed sets, and a test compares them with the contract, Connect and `authn`. A panic of a handler leaves a line too — with the code `internal`, next to the line `panic`. There is no line for a request that Connect rejects before the interceptors — a wrong `Content-Type`, a GET of a method with side effects, a broken GET: such a request has no method yet.
- **`serve`** reads the variables of `config.ServeFrom`: `API_DATABASE_URL` and `API_OPENFGA_DATABASE_URL` — the secrets of the pooler's strings with the check of the connection strings, `API_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS` — required outside a deployment, a refusal in a deployment, and `API_APP_ORIGIN`. Before the port it opens the pool of the database of `api` with `Ping`, `authz.Open` and the port `authn`; if one did not open, a start failure with the field `database`. It closes them in the share of the exit left for closing: a request that did not fit in the drain still holds a connection, and the pool's `Close` would wait for it longer than the platform's grace period.
- **The check of the image.** `serve` does not start without the databases and the sign-in service, so the job "Image" starts PostgreSQL on the runner — `postgres:17.11-bookworm` with a digest, the same version 17.11 as the tests have; Dependabot does not see the script, and it is raised by hand —, migrates both databases with the command `migrate` of the release image and starts each image with a service account key made for this run: the key belongs to no project, and `serve` sends it nowhere. Then the same `/health` and the stop on SIGTERM.
- **Cost.** The Admin SDK with its modules, Connect and the contracts module are in the binary, and `THIRD_PARTY` names each module, all under licenses of the list. The license names of `cloud.google.com/go` and `google.golang.org/api` would by the rule be `go` and `api` — as for the licenses of Go and of `api` itself in the release —, so they keep the host, and `release-licenses` refuses a repeated name and these two.

— *Why:*
- the checks in one interceptor keep their order in one place: a check forgotten in a handler would be a refusal nobody notices, and the table and its test do not let a method be forgotten;
- the right on the message, not in the handler: "before any reading by the profile ID" by itself;
- 30 s hold the AI calls — the p50 of an essay call is 11.9 s (sources 10.3) — and free the handler long before the two minutes of the SDK's repeats;
- once a day: the date is needed to the day, and a write on each request would load the account's row, which the writes under it hold;
- deletion on the path "no user" at once answers other devices "account gone" and closes the writes under the account, and a UID without a user does not hold the account's data indefinitely.

— *Rejected:*
- checks by helpers in each handler;
- writing the date on each request;
- on the path "no user", only the answer, without the mark;
- 60 s: a handler stuck on the SDK's repeats would hold on for a minute; 15 s: an essay call at the tail of the distribution would not fit;
- `rs/cors` and `connectrpc.com/cors`: a module and rules that ten lines of our own do not need, and they would not keep the headers on the answer of a panic.

— *Consequence:* `internal/rpc/`; `internal/config/`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `.github/image.sh`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Account and invite codes

**R34. An invite code is 16 characters of Crockford's base32, 80 bits from `crypto/rand`, in four groups of four; it is read as a person will type it, and the database holds the SHA-256 of the code in this reading. The code is created by the operator's command `api invite` with a tariff — by default `closed_slice`, "closed slice" —, and the command prints it once. `CreateAccount` is one transaction: "already exists" before the code, then the redemption of the code, its binding and the tariff into the account, and the owner's statement with its time. `GetAccount` reads the account in one snapshot.**
- **The code** (`internal/invite`) — Crockford's alphabet, digits and letters without I, L, O and U: `0123-4567-89AB-CDEF`. Reading a code, `api` ignores case, hyphens and whitespace characters — the end of a line from a terminal, a non-breaking space from an email —, and reads O as 0, I and L as 1, as Crockford prescribes. The hash is of the 16 characters of this reading, so every spelling of one code has the same hash. What is not 16 characters of the alphabet is "code not accepted", without a request to the database.
- **The command `api invite [-tariff closed_slice]`** — one code per run. Only the code goes to stdout, and the log to stderr: a failure with the stage `invite` and the field `database`. The connection string is `API_DATABASE_URL`, the pooler's, as for `serve`: writing one row holds no session. The tariffs are those of `internal/quota`, and the command rejects any other. `-tariff dev_check`, the dev projects' alone, is a usage refusal when `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is set; from the operator's own terminal the command cannot tell a deployment's database, and `serve` deployed reads that tariff as one with no caps. In a Cloud Run job stdout goes to the log too, so `api invite` is run from the operator's own terminal.
- **`CreateAccount`** (`internal/account`, `store.CreateAccount`):
  1. the account exists — `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS`, `already_exists`, before the statement and before the code, so a repeat after a lost answer gets it, not "code not accepted" (the contract, `CreateAccount`). A marked account is stopped already by the interceptor. A request not in form looks for the account once more before refusing: one created by a neighbouring request after the checks is "already exists" too, and a marked one is "account gone";
  2. the owner's statement is not `true` — `invalid_argument` without a `Refusal`;
  3. the code is not in form, not found or redeemed — `CAUSE_CODE_NOT_ACCEPTED`, `invalid_argument`;
  4. the transaction: the account's row with the UID, the time of the statement in `adult_stated_at` and today's request date — `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (auth_uid) DO NOTHING`; then one query — `UPDATE invite_codes … WHERE redeemed_at IS NULL RETURNING tariff` in a `WITH` and `UPDATE accounts` by it — redeems and binds the code and gives its tariff to the account. If there is no code, the transaction rolls back, and no account remains.

  Races are safe. Two requests of one UID: the second waits on the unique index of the UID and finds the first one's account — "already exists". Two accounts for one code: the second waits on the code's row and finds it redeemed — "code not accepted". If the first rolled back, the second goes on.
- **`GetAccount`** — the account ID, whether a PIN is set, the profiles — the owner's first, then by the time they were created — and the child consents without a profile, waiting and confirmed. The read is one transaction `REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY`: the device erases the databases of the profiles that are not in the list, and the profiles and consents in it are of one moment. An account marked or deleted after the checks is "account gone".

— *Why:*
- 80 bits cannot be brute-forced either through `CreateAccount` or from a stolen table of codes, so a hash without salt and without a secret is enough, and there is no code in plain form in the database;
- Crockford's alphabet and its reading forgive what a person confuses copying a code from a screen or from an email: case, O and 0, I, L and 1;
- one code in stdout: the operator passes it on as it is, and the log does not hold it;
- one transaction in the contract's order leaves neither an account without a code nor a code redeemed without an account.

— *Rejected:*
- shorter, 8 characters, 40 bits: `CreateAccount` has no request quota, and brute force would become a matter of time;
- HMAC with a secret instead of SHA-256: a second secret of the deployment, which 80 bits do not need;
- several codes per run: the first app needs one, and output on one line does not mix codes up;
- a direct connection, as for `migrate`: the command needs no session, and the operator would need a third connection string;
- the code in a log line: in a Cloud Run job the log is stored, and the code is a secret until it is redeemed.

— *Consequence:* `internal/invite/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/store/`; `cmd/api/invite.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## PIN and idempotency keys

**R35. The PIN hash is argon2id at OWASP's minimum: 19 MiB, two passes, one lane, as a PHC string. Failures are counted over the last 15 minutes, and the fifth closes input for 15 minutes from itself. An idempotency key lives in the space of its account, and the record recognizes another request by the request's fingerprint: the SHA-256 of the request without the key, and for a request with a PIN or an age, argon2id with the record's salt. A PIN request writes the action and the key's record in one transaction.**
- **The hash** (`internal/argon`):
  - argon2id from `golang.org/x/crypto/argon2`: m = 19456 KiB, t = 2, p = 1, a salt of 16 bytes, a hash of 32 bytes. This is OWASP's minimum: "m=19456 (19 MiB), t=2, p=1" (OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet, read 2026-11-09).
  - The hash is stored as the PHC string `$argon2id$v=19$m=19456,t=2,p=1$<salt>$<hash>`, and the check goes by the parameters the string names: they can be raised without a migration, and old hashes are checked by their own.
  - The comparison is in constant time.
- **The count of failures** (`internal/pin`):
  - the account holds the time of each failure of the last 15 minutes — the column `pin_failed_at` —, and the count is the failures younger than 15 minutes;
  - the fifth such failure closes input for 15 minutes from itself, so in any 15 minutes there are no more than five failures, at the junction of windows too;
  - a correct PIN resets the count. Nobody reads the columns `pin_failures` and `pin_failures_since` of migration 0001 any more: a migration with the mark "-- contract:" will remove them;
  - `SetPin`, `ChangePin` with the correct old PIN and `ResetPin` write the new hash and open input.

  The time is the clock of `api` to the microsecond, as in the database: a recorded answer and a refusal read later name the same instant.
- **A PIN request** (`internal/account`):
  1. the form — a PIN of 4–6 digits and a key of 1 to 128 bytes; otherwise `invalid_argument` without a `Refusal`;
  2. one transaction under the account: the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE` with the check of the mark, then `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. The second conflicts with itself, and not with `FOR KEY SHARE`, so the PIN requests of one account go one after another, and the other writes under the account do not wait for them;
  3. the key's record:
     - there is one, of the same method and request, "done" — the recorded answer, and nothing is counted;
     - of another method or request — `CAUSE_KEY_ALREADY_USED`;
     - "going" — `CAUSE_STILL_GOING`;
  4. the action and its refusals — "PIN already set", "PIN not set", "PIN locked" with the end of the lock;
  5. the new state of the PIN, the answer and the key's record "done" — in the same transaction.

  A refusal is not recorded: the transaction rolls back, and a repeat is checked again. A PIN failure is an answer, not a refusal: it is recorded, and a repeat does not count it. A repeat next to the first request waits for the account's row and finds the first one's record, not "still going".
- **The key's record** (migration 0002) — the account, the key, the method — the contract's procedure —, the profile ID, the fingerprint, the state "going" or "done", the answer — the contract's binary protobuf — and the time. The primary key is `(account_id, key)`. A request of the account itself has no profile; an attempt, a lesson run and a submission are targets of their own, as columns. The PIN answer is whether the PIN matched, how many tries are left and the end of the lock: there is no content in it. The two phases of the record — "going", then "done" — are for a request that waits for AI, and the daily job deletes records older than a day.
- **The fingerprint** (`internal/idem`) — of the request written by protobuf deterministically, without `idempotency_key`:
  - for most requests, SHA-256;
  - for a request with a PIN — the PIN requests — and with an age — `RequestChildConsent` — argon2id with the record's salt, with the same parameters.

  The deterministic protobuf encoding is stable within one build; a repeat across the deployment of a new version may get "key already used", and this is an accepted cost.
- **Up to three hashes.** A PIN request has up to three argon2id hashes: the fingerprint, the check of the PIN and the hash of the new one, for `ChangePin`. The fingerprint and the hash of the new PIN are computed before the transaction, so no more than one hash holds the account's row and the pool's connection — the check of the PIN or the fingerprint of a repeat.

— *Why:*
- against brute force of the PIN through `api`, the count of failures protects, not the hash: a PIN has 10⁶ values. The hash is needed against whoever reads the database. More memory per hash is shared among the requests of an instance, and adds almost nothing to the protection against brute force from the database;
- 15 minutes from the fifth failure in a sliding window hold "no more than 5 in 15 minutes" in any window;
- the key in the account's space: another account's key says nothing about someone else's action and does not hinder one's own;
- a fast hash of the request would give away the PIN — 10⁶ values — and the age — 18 values —, and the age is stored nowhere. A slow fingerprint protects them no worse than the PIN hash and needs no new secret;
- one transaction for a short request: the action and its record do not diverge, and "going" is needed only by a request that waits for AI.

— *Rejected:*
- the second recommended option of RFC 9106, t = 3, p = 4, 64 MiB: three times the memory per request. OWASP's option of 46 MiB with t = 1 is the same in the other direction;
- a lock until the end of a window from the first failure: at the junction of windows there would be up to ten failures in 15 minutes. A window from the first failure with a lock from the fifth too: at the junction up to eight got through;
- a shared space of keys: the same key of another account would be "key already used";
- an HMAC of the fingerprint with a secret of the deployment: a new secret for the deployment and for the first app;
- a fingerprint without the PIN and the age: the same key with another PIN would get the earlier answer, though the contract says to refuse "another request".

— *Consequence:* `internal/argon/`; `internal/pin/`; `internal/idem/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Profiles, the country before the DPIA and consent

**R36. The owner profile is created in the category `adult` by the owner's statement, with a consent — a row given by this statement — and the tuple `owner` before `COMMIT`. A child's profile comes by a consent, whose category is computed by the table of the ages of digital consent: below the country's age, a parent gives consent by a signed form, and the profile is created by the confirmed consent; from it, the learner gives consent themselves, in their own profile, which is created at once and waits. The countries are a list of the allowed ones in the database of `api`, empty at the start, which the command `api countries` keeps; it does not open the EU countries with their regions, the EEA and the United Kingdom before the DPIA. An account has no more than 10 consents waiting without a profile.**
- **The list of countries** (`internal/countries`, migration 0003, `api countries list|allow XX|remove XX`):
  - empty at the start: a country where the law requires more than a statement of age is closed until the operator opens it;
  - 42 codes are never opened before the DPIA:
    - the 27 EU countries — "EU countries" ([european-union.europa.eu](https://european-union.europa.eu/principles-countries-history/eu-countries_en), read 2026-11-09);
    - the EU's outermost regions with codes of their own — MQ, YT, GP, GF, RE and MF: "five French overseas departments – Martinique, Mayotte, Guadeloupe, French Guiana and Réunion; one French overseas community – Saint-Martin". Madeira, the Azores and the Canary Islands have no codes of their own ([European Parliament, "Outermost regions (ORs)"](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/factsheets/en/sheet/100/outermost-regions-ors-), read 2026-11-09);
    - "the three EEA EFTA States – Iceland, Liechtenstein and Norway" ([efta.int](https://www.efta.int/eea/eea-agreement), read 2026-11-09);
    - the United Kingdom — UK GDPR;
    - out of caution, until the DPIA: the Åland Islands — AX, a part of Finland with a code of its own — and the codes by which the closed countries are called otherwise: UK instead of GB, EL instead of GR, IC — the Canary Islands, EA — Ceuta and Melilla;
  - a removed country accepts no new profiles, and the ones created stay;
  - the country is checked by `CreateOwnerProfile` and `RequestChildConsent` — the requests that name it —, and it is one of the codes of the schema of the table of ages. `CreateChildProfile` takes the consent's country, and the contract has no refusal about the country for this method.
- **The owner profile** (`CreateOwnerProfile`):
  - the form;
  - then in a transaction under the account: "already exists", before the country, so a repeat gets it even if the country was removed since; then "country not accepted";
  - the rows of the profile and the consent and the tuple `owner` — from inside the open transaction, after the rows, before `COMMIT`. If the tuple was not written, the transaction rolls back.

  The adult's consent is a row of `consents`: confirmed now, the method `owner_statement` — the owner's statement "I am 18" with the account's date, the category `adult`, the profile's country; "who" is the account itself. The date the profile's age was stated is the date of the statement.
- **The child consent** (`RequestChildConsent`):
  - the age in years, from 0 to 17, required;
  - the category by the table of the ages of digital consent of the country, `internal/consentage`: below the country's age, a parent gives consent by a signed form, by the code of the consent request; from it, the learner themselves, in their own profile;
  - the age lives in the request's memory and is stored nowhere, and the key's fingerprint is argon2id;
  - the consent requests name the current versions of their texts.
- **The limit of waiting consents.** An account has no more than 10 consents "waiting" without a profile, by the constant `waitingConsentsMax` in code. A request beyond the limit is `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS` with the code `resource_exhausted`, and a repeat with the same key is the same consent even at the limit: the key's record answers before the count. The count goes under the account's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which every request with a key takes already, so requests side by side do not exceed the limit.
- **A child's profile** (`CreateChildProfile`):
  1. the consent by the canonical UUID, under the account, `FOR UPDATE`; another's and a nonexistent one are one refusal, `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`;
  2. the consent has a profile — "already exists", before the state;
  3. "waiting" — `CAUSE_NO_CONSENT`;
  4. the profile of the consent's category and country, the date the age was stated is the date of the request, and the tuple before `COMMIT`.

  A parent's consent by a form the operator confirms by `api consents confirm`. The form's scan is kept outside the database of `api`, and the consent method confirms the adult's age at a child's profile. Before the public launch a card transaction is added to the form (П-28).
- **The settings** (`UpdateProfile` by `can_manage`, `SetVoicing` by `can_learn`) — the fields that came:
  - the pseudonym — 1–30 code points after trimming spaces at the edges, without control characters: the database does not hold NUL, and an end of line is not part of a name;
  - the avatar — up to 64 printable ASCII characters;
  - the language — a valid BCP 47 tag: subtags of 1–8 letters and digits joined by hyphens, no longer than 64 characters, which `golang.org/x/text/language` parses. A subtag the library does not know is valid too, and an underscore instead of a hyphen, which the library forgives, is not;
  - the grade — 1–12, and 0 clears it;
  - "ask before showing" for `adult` — `invalid_argument`.

  The defaults of a new profile are by the contract: "by steps", voicing on for `under_13` and off for the others, normal speed, and for a child "ask before showing" on.
- **The profile's activity date** — a request with `profile_id` that passed all checks, fresh sign-in too, writes today's date by UTC, once a day, like the account's date. The dates of a new profile — of activity and of stating the age — are by UTC too, not by the session's time zone: by it the profile is deleted after 12 months without activity.
- **The idempotency key** — one helper: the account's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the key's record and an action over the transaction; the PIN and the consent are its actions. The fingerprint of both is argon2id; a request with neither a PIN nor an age has the fast one. The profile and the consent share the check of the canonical UUID, `store.CanonicalID`.

— *Why:*
- closed by default: a country whose law asks more than a statement of age, and which nobody named, the operator opens themselves, rather than finding it open;
- 42 codes in code, not in the database: the EU cannot be opened before the DPIA even by mistake, nor through a region with a code of its own;
- the adult's consent as a row: consent is kept for each learner, and the helper "consent given" reads one table for everyone;
- "already exists" before the country and the state: a repeat after a lost answer learns that the profile exists and reads the account;
- 10 waiting consents: a family needs a few, not hundreds, and the number does not depend on the tariff.

— *Rejected:*
- a list of forbidden countries: a country whose law asks more would turn out open;
- the list in the deployment configuration: the operator's command keeps it, and a change of the configuration is a deployment;
- the adult's consent without a row, only the account's statement;
- the limit of consents as a number in the configuration: it does not depend on the tariff, and an extra variable would fall on the deployment of `infra`.

— *Consequence:* `internal/countries/`; `internal/consentage/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `cmd/api/countries.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Deletion in code

**R37. Deleting a profile and an account is the package `internal/deletion`, in the order of deletion: the database, then the tuples, then the user at the sign-in service, and the account's row last. What failed after the database is "unfinished": the answer is "deleted" all the same, a log line names the failure, and the mark or a dangling tuple is left to the job. A repeated deletion of a profile that has a tuple and no row deletes the tuple. The path "no user" deletes the account whole by the same package.**
- **A profile** (`DeleteProfile` by `can_manage` and fresh sign-in):
  1. a transaction under the account: the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE` with the check of the mark, the profile's row `FOR UPDATE`;
  2. a child consent goes into the minimal record `child_consent_records`: the ID, when given, how checked, when deleted. An adult's consent goes whole;
  3. the profile's row, and with it, by the foreign keys, the consent and the records of idempotency keys;
  4. after `COMMIT`, the tuple. If it was not deleted, "unfinished": there is no row, and the tuple gives nothing.

  The account does not delete its last profile.
- **A dangling tuple.** `DeleteProfile` has a mark in the table of methods: when the right is there and the profile's row under the account is not, the interceptor deletes the tuple and answers "no profile" — only with fresh sign-in: without it the tuple stays, and the answer is the same. If it was not deleted, `unavailable`, and a repeat deletes it. The other methods do not touch a dangling tuple: the job removes it.
- **An account** (`DeleteAccount` by fresh sign-in):
  1. one transaction:
     - the account's row `FOR UPDATE` and the mark;
     - the minimal records of the confirmed child consents — and of those whose profile is not created yet;
     - the profiles, the consents, the records of keys;
     - the account's fields — the statement, the PIN, the tariff, the offset, the end of the day, the request date;
     - the code's binding — the code stays redeemed;
  2. the tuples by `user:<account ID>`, in batches;
  3. `RemoveUser` — revocation and deletion, "no user" is done. It runs even when step 2 failed;
  4. only when 2 and 3 went through — a transaction: the clearing once more and the account's row.

  If step 1 failed, `unavailable`, and nothing changed. Steps 2–4 failing is "unfinished". A repeat gets "account gone" from the interceptor.
- **"No user", an account without a mark**: the interceptor deletes the account with the same `Account`, without fresh sign-in, and answers "account gone". Step 3 then finds the user deleted. If step 1 failed, `unavailable`, and a repeat deletes it. An account that a neighbouring request of the same user deleted first is "account gone" too.
- **`Finish`** — steps 2–4 for a marked account. `Account` calls it, and the daily job calls it for the marks that requests left.
- **The log.** An "unfinished" deletion answers with the code `ok` — and on the path "no user", "account gone" —, and its line `request` is of the error level, with the failure's kind (`rpc.NoteFailure`): the operator sees that there is something to complete. One failure of steps 2–4 is on its own, so that its kind is visible; two are together.

— *Why:*
- one package for the four paths — `DeleteProfile`, `DeleteAccount`, "no user" and the daily job — keeps the order of deletion in one place;
- "unfinished" is a separate kind of error: the caller tells "deleted, and the rest will be completed" from "not deleted";
- the interceptor, not the handler, deletes a dangling tuple: it reads the profile's row itself, after the right, and a request without a row does not reach the handler.

— *Rejected:*
- deletion on the path "no user" by the mark alone: the learners' data would live until the job's pass;
- a refusal on an "unfinished" deletion: there is nothing about the learners in the database, and a repeat after the refusal would already get "account gone" — `app` would erase everything and sign out all the same;
- a dangling tuple only for the job: a repeated deletion of the profile must delete it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/deletion/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R38. The daily retention job is the command `api sweep`: one pass per run, its steps in order, with no state between passes. A pass is bounded by its own period, `-for`, 9 minutes by default; the 12-month period is checked under the lock of the row; a dangling tuple is one older than the request timeout and without a row of the profile at its account; a user of the sign-in service without an account is one 30 days after the latest of the sign-in, the token refresh and the creation. The log is counters by steps; a pass that did not reach its end, or with a refusal, exits with code 1.**
- **The steps** (`internal/sweep`, in the order of `logging.Steps`), each choosing its work again by a query:
  1. `marks` — marks "being deleted" older than the request timeout: `Finish`, the steps of an account's deletion after its first transaction;
  2. `idle_accounts` — accounts without requests for more than 12 months, by the same route as `DeleteAccount`, without a fresh sign-in (`deletion.IdleAccount`);
  3. `idle_profiles` — profiles without activity for more than 12 months, by the same route as `DeleteProfile` (`deletion.IdleProfile`);
  4. `gone_attempts` and `gone_runs` — the steps of the job after a deployment too: the attempts and lesson runs on packages that the image no longer gives are closed "unchecked", before `abandoned_attempts` would close them "abandoned";
  5. `abandoned_attempts` — an attempt without actions for 7 days is closed "abandoned";
  6. `evidence` — the evidence the closed attempts owe, those this pass closed among them;
  7. `abandoned_runs` — a lesson run without actions for 30 days ends "abandoned";
  8. `users` — users of the sign-in service without an account of `api`: `RemoveUser`;
  9. `tuples` — dangling tuples: `DeleteOwned`, by accounts;
  10. `consent_records` — the minimal records of child consents for children deleted more than 5 years ago by the calendar, in batches of 1000; a younger record stays. The period runs from the date of deletion in the record itself: there is no ID of the account or of the profile in it;
  11. `consent_forms` — the scans of forms after their period, through the storage port; `api sweep` reads the forms' storage, as `serve` does;
  12. `problem_reports` — the reports handled more than a year ago;
  13. `quota` — the counters of the quota whose day ended more than a day ago;
  14. `keys` — records of idempotency keys older than a day;
  15. `changelog` — OpenFGA's `changelog` older than a day.

  The deletions by period come before the closing of attempts and runs, the tuples and `changelog`, so that a deleted profile has nothing to close and the pass itself picks up what it left. A failure of an element is counted, and the row `failure` names its kind and step; the step goes on, and the following steps do in any case.
- **Batches and an interrupted pass.** Accounts, profiles and tuples go by 100. Accounts and profiles are chosen by the primary key, in ascending order of ID after the last one seen: a failed element is not chosen again in the same pass. Users go one at a time, and the account of each is checked right before the deletion: a list of the sign-in service of many pages is minutes out of date by its end, and a user who came back in those minutes and created an account keeps it. Keys and `changelog` are deleted by 1000 rows per `DELETE`; `changelog` has no index by time (sources 11.7), but the job keeps no more than two days of records in it. There is no state between passes: the next pass picks up an interrupted one by itself, because each step chooses its work again.
- **The pass's period** is the flag `-for`, 9 minutes by default: less than the 10 minutes that Cloud Run gives a job's task by default (sources 11.6). When it runs out, the pass stops between elements, the further steps do not begin, and their rows say `finished: false`. Time is not counted as a failure.
- **12 months** is strictly more: the date of the last request or activity is earlier than the same date a year ago, in UTC. The period is checked again under the lock: an account by `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` with a condition on `last_request_on`, a profile the same way on `active_on`, under `WriteUnderAccount`. Postgres waits for the request that writes today's date and reads the row again, so an owner who came back on the day of the pass loses no data. A profile of an account that was marked after the choice is "nothing done": it goes with the account. The owner's profile is deleted by period the same way as a child's profile: its date of activity is moved only by requests with its ID, not by the account's requests and not by requests on the children's profiles.
- **A dangling tuple** is a tuple `owner` of an account on a profile, older than the request timeout (the time of the write is given by OpenFGA), whose profile has no row, or a row of another account. The job does not read tuples of the model's other kinds. A young tuple is not touched: it is written before `COMMIT`, and the transaction of its profile may still be going.
- **A user without an account** is a UID that no account has, marked or not, and the latest of whose three times — the sign-in, the token refresh, the creation — is older than 30 days. The creation is `CreationTimestamp` of the Admin SDK, the field `authn.User.Created`: a user just created in the console is not deleted on the same day.
- **`changelog`** is deleted by SQL through the storage's pool: OpenFGA cannot do this, and `authz.Rights` holds the pool, which it opens itself.
- **The configuration** is `config.SweepFrom`: the databases `api` and `openfga` through the pooler, the Firebase project and key. The log level, the request timeout and the time to close are `config.LoadFrom`, as for `serve`: the deployment gives the job the same variables as the service, and their `API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` must match. The deployment is recognized by `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, which Cloud Run sets for a job's task (sources 11.6): with it a service account key is forbidden, and outside a deployment it is required. The job's schedule is given by the deployment of `infra`.
- **The log.** For each step, a row `swept`: the step, `done`, `failed`, `finished`. Neither ID nor UID: the list of the event's fields does not let them through. The exit is 0 only after a clean pass: a period that depends on the job is "until the next successful pass".

— *Why:*
- with no state between passes there is nothing to repair when a pass was interrupted: the next one chooses its work again;
- an own period of the pass gives the result row of each step on a break too; without it the platform would stop the pass in the middle of an element silently;
- the 12-month period under the lock of the row keeps the data of an owner who came back on the day of the pass, and it rests on the same `FOR UPDATE` as deletion on request;
- a young tuple without a row is usually a profile whose transaction has not yet ended;
- the creation time protects a user who has neither a sign-in nor a token refresh.

— *Rejected:*
- a pass without its own period: on a break by the platform the log would not name where it stopped;
- any tuple without a row being dangling: the job would delete the tuple of a profile being created right now, and that profile would be left without the right;
- only the sign-in and the token refresh: a user without both would be deleted by the very first pass, even one created an hour ago;
- a cursor of the pass in the database: the steps choose their work by a query anyway, and the cursor would have to be maintained and deleted by hand.

— *Consequence:* `internal/sweep/`; `internal/deletion/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/logging/`; `internal/config/`; `cmd/api/sweep.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R39. The run on the dev projects is `just dev`, `just dev-app` and `just dev-check`, with the configuration from a file in a Docker volume outside the repository. The ID token is a custom token of a placeholder user — `dev-author` for the check, `dev-app` for the local build of `app` —, signed by the service account key and exchanged at the sign-in service, with no new Go code. The country of the author's profile on the dev project is the placeholder `US`. `just dev` runs on the stub of `tutor`, and `just dev-check` walks the whole path, the platform AI quota on the database included, without a paid call.**
- **The configuration file** is lines `KEY=VALUE` in `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev/dev.env` or at the path `API_DEV_CONFIG`. The recipe refuses a file in a git tree — by the same rule as the binary for a secret file: `.git` in the directory or its parents —, takes only the names `API_*`, reads the lines as they are, without executing them, and prints no value. No secret goes into the process's command line: only through the environment, files and pipes. In it:
  - the pooler's strings and the direct strings of the databases `api` and `openfga`;
  - the Firebase project and the path to the service account key, `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_FILE`;
  - the Web API key, `API_DEV_WEB_API_KEY`, and, if needed, `API_DEV_UID` — only the recipe reads them, the binary does not know them;
  - `API_PACKAGES_DIR` — the folder into which `just packages` puts the packages of the pinned release —, `API_APP_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:5173` — the origin of `app` on loopback, whose server proxies the paths of `api` —, and `API_CONSENT_FORMS_DIR`.

  Beside the file, the volume keeps `<UID>.code` — `dev-author.code` —, the invite code of the check's account, mode 600.
- **The volume.** `.devcontainer` mounts the named volume `novatrail-api-dev` at `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev`, and the Dockerfile creates this directory, like the directories of the other volumes, with the mode `700`: the volume belongs to `vscode` and survives a rebuild of the container. It is one of the differences of the devcontainer of `api` from the container of `spec`, which a move to a new tag keeps.
- **The ID token** (`scripts/dev.sh`, sources 2.6):
  - `custom_token` makes a custom token for a UID with Firebase's claims — `alg` `RS256`, `iss` and `sub` the service account's email, `aud` Identity Toolkit, `iat`, `exp` in an hour, `uid` —, signed by `openssl` with the key from the file;
  - `exchange` swaps a custom token on stdin for an ID token by REST `accounts:signInWithCustomToken`, and `id_token` does both for a UID;
  - the key, the Web API key and the token go through files, pipes and the configuration of `curl` on stdin, not through the command line.
- **The dev projects** are `novatrail-api-dev` — Firebase on the Spark plan and Neon on the Free plan in `aws-us-east-1`, Postgres 17. The Neon strings are with `sslmode=verify-full`, and pgx understands `channel_binding=require` from Neon's string. The Web API key is «Browser key (auto created by Firebase)» in Google Cloud's Credentials. The Firebase project has the Web App `app` and `127.0.0.1` among the authorized domains, with the same key.
- **`just dev`** runs on the stub of `tutor`: `scripts/dev.sh run` unsets the dev file's key of platform AI, `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE`, and sets `API_TUTOR_STUB=1`, so a run calls no provider. The models are `just dev-ai`, `run-ai`, with the key of the dev file, for a task that checks the words of a model under the cap its text names. `just dev serve` listens on port 8080.
- **`just dev-app`** keeps `api` up for the local build of `app`, with a custom token of the account `dev-app`, on `closed_slice`, in the volume `novatrail-dev-token`. The container of `api` is `api` in the network `novatrail-api`, by `--network-alias=api` of its devcontainer, so `app` in its container, attached to that network, goes to `http://api:8080`. The steps of the legal texts, of the account and its owner's profile, of the migrations with the country, of the packages, of `api reopen` and of the start of the server are functions that `dev-check` and `dev-app` share.
- **`just dev-check`** is the whole path:
  1. the build, `migrate` of both databases, `countries allow US`, the packages;
  2. `api reopen` before the server: the flag "platform limit", if a run with `app` left it, would answer the reading with "limit" without a call;
  3. `serve` on a free port, with the platform AI key `sk-ant-dev-check-refused`, which Anthropic refuses, and `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE` of the dev file overridden by an empty value — empty means "not set" —, and the stub's switch unset: the real key does not get into the check;
  4. the ID token of `API_DEV_UID` or `dev-author`;
  5. the account: with `<UID>.code` kept, `check_account` gives it the tariff `dev_check` by `api tariff` at each run; without it, an account made before is deleted by `DeleteAccount` and made anew by `api invite` with that tariff and `CreateAccount`, and the code is written as soon as `CreateAccount` answers; with no owner profile, `CreateOwnerProfile`. `GetAccount` is checked by `jq`: the account and the owner profile of the category `adult`;
  6. the quota: `ExportAccount` before and after, and between them an attempt on a task that AI reads — the engine's choice of a task, or the first unfinished segment of the lesson it gave; a task by tap, or one not admitted, the check skips and asks the engine again, up to 8 times —, with a synthetic PNG of one white pixel. The reading reaches Anthropic, which answers 401 before the model, for free; the outcome is `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`, there is no record of the spending, and the reserve remains spending. The check: the profile and the account have spent more than before, and the reserve holds as much as before;
  7. the storage of works: after the reading, it switches the storage of the owner's works on to the current storage-consent, puts a synthetic work of the attempt — a history `[]` and the pixel the reading sends —, lists it, reads it back as an adult, finds it in the export, erases it, puts it again and switches the storage off, after which the list is empty;
  8. the log of `serve`: each line is JSON, and there is neither UID, nor email, nor connection strings, nor the Web API key, nor the token, nor the dev file's key, nor the check's; the row `ai_usage` of the submission has the outcome `key_refused`, and none has a model's answer;
  9. a second server, on the stub of `tutor` (`stubbed`), with no key of platform AI and a file of facts of the check's own in place of the dev file's, `child_facts`: Anthropic's `data_processing_agreement` and `minimal_retention` for an adult and a child under 13, and `minor_safeguards` for the child, so that the stub admits a child's workbook page — the pages of the pinned release are for a child alone. No call of that server reaches a provider, so its facts govern the stub's admission alone; `api check`, the first server and the dev file keep the facts the author declared. On it:
     - the engine's task of handwriting is solved by a crop of 2×1 pixels that the stub reads as the right value, and `GetCorrectAnswerWords` gives words with the question last;
     - a synthetic child of 9 gets a parent's consent by its form, which `api consents confirm` confirms, and a profile of the pseudonym `Dev child`; it connects `novatrail/grade-3-math`, a page of the engine's task with a wide crop in every box is solved, the export holds its evidence, and the profile is deleted by a fresh sign-in. A profile of that pseudonym an earlier run left is deleted first, and a consent with no profile is taken back — the consent step of the first server takes its own back, so any such is an earlier run's;
     - an essay of the owner's: `novatrail/english-essays` connected, the engine's choice on its skill `paragraphs`, a wide crop that ends in feedback, "Done" with the criteria of the rubric, and the module disconnected. The first server disconnects that module from the owner before it connects the module of the first app, so that a module an earlier run left connected gives none of its tasks to the steps that look for others;
     - the log of that server holds neither the words, the praise, the essay's text or feedback, nor the code of the request, nor the token of either sign-in, and a quota of the account's family day with no room for the page or the essay is named as such: the page keeps up to 224,000 of its 1,500,000 as spent, the essay 30,000;
  10. at its end the check prints what the account's counters of the export hold, so that two runs one after the other give what one spends (measurements 8).

  A repeat skips what was created. The recipe does not go into CI: it needs real projects. The file loader and the custom token are checked in CI by the test `scripts/dev_test.go`: it calls the script's functions in bash, and checks the signature by the public key.

— *Why:*
- a custom token gives a real ID token of the sign-in service — the same route of the check with revocation as `app` has —, without `app` and without code that would get into the binary;
- a placeholder UID separates the test accounts from the author's sign-in through Google, which comes with `app`;
- the volume keeps the key outside any git tree and does not vanish on a rebuild, and nothing is needed on the host;
- the stub for `just dev`: a run costs nothing and sends nothing to a provider;
- the key Anthropic refuses: the reserve and the settlement go the whole path on Neon — the first transaction, the call, and the settlement by a transaction of its own — without a route of its own for the check in the code and without money;
- the network alias: an address of the container changes with a rebuild.

— *Rejected:*
- MathTrail's working project `mathtrail-canvas-prod` instead of a new one: `api sweep` would delete its users without an account of `api`;
- a small web application for the sign-in by the window — that is the work of `app`;
- a Go program that issues the token: a second adapter of the sign-in service beside the binary, while `jq`, `openssl` and `curl` are in the container already;
- a host folder — it has to be created before the container starts, otherwise the container will not start;
- the home folder in the container — it vanishes on every rebuild;
- the author's real country — the data of the dev account are synthetic, as its UID is;
- quota steps without a call: the reserve and the settlement are not seen on the database;
- substituting the provider's address through `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`: the Anthropic SDK reads it, and the adapter of `tutor` sets no address, but this is a hole, not a route for the check;
- the address `hostname -i` of the container for `app`: a rebuild changes it.

— *Consequence:* `scripts/dev.sh`; `scripts/dev_test.go`; `justfile`; `.devcontainer/`; `docs/build.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.12.0

**R40. A move to a tag of `spec` goes to its latest release, and the Go libraries that `api` takes with the types of `spec` must build beside that tag: a build has one Go module `spec`, at the tag of `api`. Code that rests on a library that does not build beside the tag waits for the library's release on the tag's line; the rest of the move does not wait.**
- **One module `spec` per build.** MVS gives the Go module `spec` the highest version that the build names, and `api` names its tag, so a library that `api` takes is built with the types of `spec` at that tag, not at the one its own `go.mod` names. A library on an older line of `spec` may read the types the old way and not build.
- **The check** is `go build` of the packages of the library that `api` imports, in an empty module in a temporary folder, beside the tag. What does not build is named with the code that waits for it.

— *Why:* the latest release carries every answer of `spec` up to it, and a move to an older one would have to be repeated at once; a library built with another tag's types would not build, or would read them otherwise.

— *Rejected:*
- an older tag than the latest: without the answers that came after it, the move would be repeated right away;
- staying on an older line of `spec` on which every library builds: the format window and the answers of `spec` would stay old for all the code;
- an older format window on the new line until the libraries catch up: `spec` keeps one current version of format v0, and a library on the older line does not build on the tag anyway.

— *Consequence:* `spec/`; `go.mod`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`.

## Module packages in code

**R41. The module packages are put into a folder by the binary's command `api packages <folder>`: it takes the embedded `modules.json`, downloads the index of the pinned release of `modules` and checks it by digest, and each package in the window — withdrawn ones too — it downloads as an archive from the release of its record, strictly unpacks in memory, reads by `module-kit` along the chain from the digest, and lays out by digests: the index, the package's three texts under the package digest, a file of the device part once, under its own digest. `serve` at start reads the folder `API_PACKAGES_DIR`, `/packages` by default: the index by digest, the packages' texts by the same chain, and the files of the device parts by the size that the list names; a foreign index or a broken package is a start failure. The index's signature is checked by the CI job "Modules" — `release-verify`, and then `cmd/modules-check` by the rules of the window.**
- **What was decided:**
  - `modules.json` in the root names the release of `modules` and the digest of its index, and it is embedded by the package `github.com/altnovatrail/api` in the module's root, `modules.go`. The parsing is strict: two members, a tag `vX.Y.Z` and a digest in lower case (`internal/packages`, `ParseConfig`);
  - `module-kit` gives `modulepackage.Read`, `moduleindex.Read`, `moduleindex.Newest` and `module.ReadsFormat`, but neither downloads nor unpacks: that is `internal/packages`. The address is `https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules/releases/download/<release>/<file>`, without a token; the timeout of a file is 2 minutes;
  - the folder into which `Lay` lays out is created by `os.Mkdir` with the mode 0755 and a random name beside the target one, not by `MkdirTemp` and `Chmod`: this way the nonroot of the image reads it, and SonarCloud (go:S2612) flagged `Chmod` as permissions to be reviewed;
  - unpacking — only regular files and the folders of `device/`, paths clean and without `..`, only `inventory.json`, `server.json`, `device.json` and `device/…`, no path twice. A link, another type of entry, an extra path is the refusal `ErrArchive`, and so is a sparse file of PAX — an entry with a record whose key begins with `GNU.sparse.` —, before the entry is read: a package holds no sparse file, and the reader of `archive/tar` unfolds one into the zeros of its holes, so 3,584 bytes of tar became a file of 500 MB (sources 4.5). The old sparse format of GNU, the type `S`, is another type of entry. The fuzz test `FuzzUnpack` holds the archive to regular files whose bytes it holds;
  - the limits are: the index 16 MB, the archive 256 MB, the unpacked content 512 MB: a package of the first slice is a few MB, and a wrong file will fill neither the disk nor the memory of the build. A regular file of zeros reaches the last limit from about 0.5 MB of gzip (measurements 5);
  - the layout is `module-index.json`, `packages/sha256-<hex>/{inventory,server,device}.json` and `files/sha256-<hex>`: an unchanged media file of a new version takes no space. The folder is assembled beside, in a temporary one, and moves into place by renaming as a whole; the command does not touch a non-empty folder. The modes are 0755 for folders and 0444 for files: the image copies the folder with root as owner, and `serve` runs as nonroot;
  - `serve` reads the folder before it opens the databases: the index's digest against the embedded one, the texts of each package of the window by `modulepackage.ReadParts`, and each file that its list names by size under its digest in `files/`. Media files are not taken into memory and are not hashed again at each start: `Lay` put them under the digest after `module-kit` read them along the chain, and the image does not change. The set — the packages' texts and the folder — goes to the catalog, and the row `startup` names the release of `modules` and the number of packages;
  - the log — the event `packages_laid` with the release and the numbers of packages and files, and the failure moment `packages`;
  - the `Dockerfile` — the stages `release-packages` and `source-packages`;
  - the CI job "Modules" — `.github/modules.sh` in the stage `toolchain`, with the submodule and the history of `main`, and the recipe as its argument: `ci-modules` for "Modules", `ci-withdrawals` for the scheduled job "Withdrawals". `just ci-modules` downloads the index and its signature, calls `release-verify`, then `go run ./cmd/modules-check` against the `modules.json` of `main`, if it is there; then it lays out the packages by `api packages` and runs on them the test of the first app's path, `TestThePinnedReleaseIsLearnedOn`, whose subtest "the board module" runs in the dev mode, and looks for the line of the test's pass. `cmd/modules-check` has the flag `-latest`, the index of the newest release. The program is outside the release binary: the release build takes only `./cmd/api`. On every pull request and push, without a path filter; "Modules" is a required check of the ruleset of `main`;
  - `release-verify` refuses for an index the signature of another release of `modules` — its source repository's digest differs —, and `modules-check` refuses a `modules.json` with another digest;
  - the first app — the recipe `just packages`, by default into `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev/packages`, the dev volume outside git, and `serve` with `API_PACKAGES_DIR` of this folder. `just dev-check` lays the packages out again for its run;
  - licenses — the modules that the packages bring into the binary are in `THIRD_PARTY`: `module-kit`, the root `spec`, `jsonschema/v6` (Apache-2.0), `go.starlark.net` (BSD-3-Clause) and `yaml/v4` (Apache-2.0 and MIT, with NOTICE). `go.starlark.net` stands on the pseudo-version that `module-kit` pinned: it is exact, and starlark has no tags.

— *Why:*
- one command for the image and the first app: the build stage and the recipe call one code, and `modules.json` is one, embedded;
- `module-kit` does no input or output, so the downloading and the unpacking are in `api`, and the check is still its: a package that `api` let through, `module-kit` read along the chain;
- an archive from a release is not a trusted file until the chain has read it, and unpacking into memory by strict rules does not let it write outside the folder;
- `serve` checks the folder at start, not at the first request: an image with the wrong release is a failure of the deployment, not of learners' attempts;
- the CI checks are by a Go program, not by shell: the rule of the current version is the same that `module-kit` gives an attempt (`moduleindex.Newest`), and tests hold it.

— *Rejected:*
- a folder per package with a copy of all its files: each version would repeat the media files, which the layout puts by digest, once;
- `serve` reading the packages whole, `modulepackage.Read`: each instance would hold in memory all the media files of all versions and would hash them at each cold start;
- loading the packages by `serve` itself at start: the image has no network at the deployment, and each revision would go to GitHub;
- the CI checks in shell and `jq`: the rule of the current version would diverge from what `api` gives an attempt.

— *Consequence:* `modules.json`; `modules.go`; `internal/packages/`; `cmd/api/packages.go`; `cmd/modules-check/`; `Dockerfile`; `.github/modules.sh`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Catalog and package in code

**R42. `PackageService`, `ModuleService` and the files of the device parts are answered by the package `internal/catalog`, from the set of packages that `serve` read at start. The open views are built once, at start, and a package whose views do not build is a start failure. A `200` answer of an open view and of a file is cached for a day, a file also by an `ETag` of its digest, a refusal is cached by nobody, and a `503` of a file comes with `Retry-After: 30`. The connected modules are the table `connected_modules`: the profile's ID, the module's ID and the time of connection, without a version.**
- **What was decided:**
  - the set of packages, `packages.Set`, on reading the folder also builds the status of each digest of the index — "gives", "no longer gives" or "does not know" —, and a map of the files of each package's device part by their paths in `device/`. `Current` gives the package of the module's current version by the function `moduleindex.Newest` of `module-kit`;
  - `catalog.New` builds the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps of each package of the window by the functions of `openview` of `module-kit`, and holds them as bytes of JSON with the format version from the module manifest. A part the module does not have, the view does not have either: the answer goes without it. The skill tiers are taken from the canonical view, `skill_tiers`;
  - open views: a digest of the wrong form is `invalid_argument` without `Refusal`; "no longer gives" is `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN`; "does not know" is `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN`. `CheckPackages` names each digest of the request in its order, and a digest of the wrong form refuses the whole request;
  - the catalog is the modules of the index whose current version suits the profile by audience: the profile's age category is in `age_categories` of its module manifest. When the deployment's list `API_CATALOG_MODULES` has one, the catalog gives only the modules it names. A module ID by the format's schema is lowercase ASCII, so the order of bytes is the order of UTF-16 units that the contract asks for;
  - `ConnectModule`: the module is not in the index — `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`; it has no current version, the version does not suit, or the deployment's list leaves it out — `CAUSE_NO_CURRENT_VERSION`; otherwise `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` under the account (`store.WriteUnderAccount`), and a module connected already keeps its time. A repeated connection of a module that is connected already gets no refusal, even if the module now has no current version: the request is repeated by the state of the profile. `DisconnectModule` deletes the row, and a module that is not connected changes nothing. The answer of both is the connected modules, read in the same transaction as the change;
  - each method of `ModuleService` reads the profile by one query — the age category and whether the consent is confirmed (`store.LearnerOf`); a consent not confirmed is `CAUSE_NO_CONSENT`, as `rpc.RequireConsent` refuses;
  - the list of connected modules is in the order of connection, and those connected in one instant by the bytes of the ID (`COLLATE "C"`). A module without a current version for the learner, or one the deployment's list leaves out, comes with an empty digest;
  - the path of a file is `GET /packages/sha256-<hex>/device/<path>`, on the same mux as Connect, under the same wrapper of CORS. A digest of the wrong form and a path that is not in the part's list are `404`; "no longer gives" is `410`; "does not know" is `503` with `Retry-After: 30`. The file is served by `http.ServeContent` — with HEAD, ranges and `304`; `Content-Type` is by the extension for the four formats of `media/`, `text/plain; charset=utf-8` for `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`, and with it `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Refusals have no body;
  - caching: `public, max-age=86400` for `200`, `206` and `304`, and `no-store` for everything else, the errors of `http.ServeContent` — a range outside the file, a file that cannot be read — too. They have no body: the text of such an error is the server's, not the contract's. A `500` is written into the log as an error;
  - the form of a digest and the name `sha256-<hex>` are one for `internal/packages` and `catalog`: `packages.IsDigest`, `packages.Name` and `packages.FromName`, and the rule of the window is one function for the layout and for the status;
  - the log — the event `package_file`: the status, the time and, on a failure, the kind of error, without the address, the digest and the path;
  - the table `connected_modules` of the migration `0004`; the module ID in it is from 1 to 128 characters, as in the format's schema. The check is `char_length`, not `<> ''`: SonarCloud reads SQL by Oracle's rules, where an empty string is NULL (plsql:NullComparison);
  - the tests are on the packages of the vectors of `spec.Format`, which the helper `internal/packages/packagestest` assembles, `Lay` lays out and `Read` reads, without a network. The second version of a module is the same vector with another version in the module manifest. The helper, like `storetest`, is excluded from Codecov's coverage: its uncovered branches are failures of the tests' environment;
  - the job "Image" checks for both images the answer `503` with `Retry-After: 30` to a file of a digest that no index knows.

— *Why:*
- views at start: there are few of them — four per package —, and a broken view is the same as a broken package, and it is found by a failure of the deployment, not by a refusal to a learner;
- `ETag` is the file's digest: the file under it does not change, and after a day the browser asks again and gets `304` instead of taking the file again;
- `no-store` for refusals: the next request may find the deployment finished, and a withdrawn version is a refusal that must reach `app` at once;
- 30 s: a deployment of a Cloud Run revision takes minutes, and `app` will ask again several times without loading `api`;
- `nosniff` and the type by the extension: the module format ties a file's signature to its extension, and `api`, on its own origin, does not let the browser read a media file as a page;
- a log row for a file: Connect's interceptor sees only methods, and without a row of their own the requests for files would leave no trace.

— *Rejected:*
- building a view on each request: the same work at each cold cache, and a failure of a view comes to the learner, not to the deployment;
- `http.FileServer` over the folder: it would serve paths that are not in the part's list, and a listing of the folder;
- holding media files in memory: each instance would hold all the files of all versions;
- `immutable` and a year: a withdrawn version must not live in the browser's cache longer than a day, how long the withdrawal takes;
- a limit on the number of digests of `CheckPackages`: the contract does not name one, and `app` would not know how to split the request; the server's limit of a message's size bounds it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/catalog/`; `internal/packages/`; `internal/packages/packagestest/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/config/`; `.github/image.sh`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The database of E3 in code

**R43. Attempts, lesson runs, the log of evidence, the cache of the skill state and the engine choices are tables of the migration `0005`, as a skeleton: keys, links to the profile, the constraints "one open" and the fields of the row's life. The fields whose meaning the code decides — the verdicts, the ladder, the words, the observation, the rubric, the fields of the choice — are added by migrations of their own, with the code that writes them. One open attempt per profile and one open lesson run per profile and lesson are held by unique partial indexes, the append-only log by a trigger, and what is abandoned is closed by two steps of `api sweep`. An applied migration is edited only equivalently.**
- **The skeleton, `0005`:**
  - `engine_choices` — the ID, the profile, the kind — a task or a lesson — and the time of the choice. An attempt refers only to a choice of a task, and a lesson run only to a choice of a lesson: in each row a generated column of the choice's kind is part of its reference, so one choice does not begin both an attempt and a lesson run;
  - `lesson_runs` — the ID, the profile, the full ID of the lesson, the module and its version, the package digest, the choice that opened the lesson run (`NOT NULL` and unique), the start, the last action, the reason and the time of the end. The reason is a word of the contract (`passed`, `unchecked`, `not_suitable`, `abandoned`), and is set together with the time;
  - `attempts` — the ID, the profile, the package digest, the module and its version, the format version and the version of `module-kit`, the task, the seed and the instance, the start, the last action, the reason and the time of closing. The start is exactly one of three: a choice (unique), a segment of a lesson run with a place from 1, or a previous attempt ("again"). The seed is the `uint64` of `module-kit` (`template.AttemptInstance`), in a `bigint` by the same bits;
  - `evidence` — `(profile_id, seq)` as the primary key, the attempt unique, the digest, the module, the time and the evidence itself as bytes of a type of `learner-model`. Neither `learner-model` nor `engine` has Go dependencies but `learner-model` for `engine`, and `spec` for them is a submodule of documents, so they build beside the tag of `api`; their versions are those of `go.mod`;
  - `skill_states` — a row per profile: the state as bytes, the cache mark — the version of `learner-model`, the release of `modules`, the format window — and the number of the last evidence taken into account, from 0. `store.HoldSkillState` creates the row, if there is none, before locking;
  - `idempotency_keys` gets the targets `attempt_id` and `lesson_run_id`;
  - each reference between these rows carries the profile — a composite foreign key `(profile_id, …)` —, so a row does not refer to a row of another profile and does not hold up its deletion;
  - the start of an attempt and of a lesson run, and their closing and end once written, do not change — triggers hold this;
  - indexes: the open attempts and lesson runs by the last action — for the job —, the profile's attempts by start, and each reference by which deletion goes has its own: the attempts of a lesson run, the previous attempt and the targets of keys;
  - the job: `store.CloseAbandonedAttempts` and `store.EndAbandonedRuns` — `UPDATE` in batches by the shared `inBatches`, with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and the period checked once more in the outer `WHERE`. The time of closing is the time of the pass by which the period is judged, not the database's clock: a pass has one clock.
- **The fields added after it:**
  - `0006` — the module and the task of a choice of a task, the number of wrong answers of an attempt and the time when the task's technique was first given on a step, the table `submissions` and the key record's target `submission_id`. The check of the key record is widened: a record "done" holds an answer or a reference to a submission. A widening is not a narrowing, but the test of "add first, remove later" sees a removed constraint in it, and the migration is marked `-- contract:` with the reason: the former release writes each record "done" with an answer, and the widened check accepts it;
  - `0009` — a choice of a lesson names the module and the local ID of the lesson, `engine_choices.lesson_id`, and the table's check requires both for a choice of a lesson, as `module_id` and `task_id` for a choice of a task. A lesson run still holds the full ID of the lesson;
  - `0010` — the rest that a choice stores, and the references to the closing of an attempt and the run end after which the choice is given as the next one, `after_attempt_id` and `after_lesson_run_id`, unique; `0011` widens the choice's kind to "nothing to give", `nothing`, with a mark of the outcome "not admitted";
  - `0012` — the fields of the consent contract at `consents` and the tables `consent_forms`, `account_texts`, `work_storage_consents` and `problem_reports`. The check "a profile only for a confirmed consent" is removed and marked `-- contract:`; a consent written by the former release during a deployment gets who gave it, the code and the state of the form by a trigger;
  - `0013` gives the child consents written before the table of ages `applied` 13 — the age by which the former code gave them —, and the same trigger gives it to a consent of the former release: otherwise a learner of 13–17 years would be asked the age again.
- **An applied migration** is edited only equivalently: the filling of `giver` in `0012` has `WHERE giver IS NULL`, which every row meets, and a check of a non-empty text is written by `char_length` — `char_length(object) >= 1` in `0012`, `char_length(module_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 128` in `0004` —, not `<> ''`, which SonarCloud reads as Oracle's NULL. On the dev database the check of `0004` was replaced by `ALTER TABLE` in one transaction, and that of `0012` kept the former form, with the same meaning.

— *Why:*
- a skeleton: the meaning of the fields of the verdict, the ladder and the choice is seen only by their code, and the schema changes only forward, so a wrong field would have to be removed by a migration `-- contract:`. The keys and the rules "one open" are needed by all the code at once;
- "one open" by an index: two devices that began an attempt in one instant will not get two open ones, whatever the code checked before the write; the second request gets a key violation and reads the open one again;
- a trigger: the append-only log is what the repeat of a verdict and the recomputation of the learner model rely on, and the database does not let it be broken, even by an error of the code;
- the job's steps with the skeleton: closing what is abandoned rests only on the fields of the row's life.

— *Rejected:*
- all the fields of the tables at once: a field whose meaning its code had not decided would have to be removed by a migration `-- contract:`;
- "one open" by a check in the code: a race of two devices would give two open attempts;
- the log without a trigger: only the code would hold the append-only rule.

— *Consequence:* `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/sweep/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Attempt without AI in code

**R44. The attempt is run by the package `internal/learning`: the beginning by the engine's choice, by a lesson's segment and by "again", the open attempt, the submission, the beginning of a step and "Done". A task that AI would check begins only where the admission gate admits its kind of call, and is refused "not admitted" otherwise. A submission is a database row with its answer, and the key record refers to it. The idempotency keys are run by the shared helper `rpc.Once`. Each closing of an attempt appends, by the same transaction, the evidence the profile's closed attempts owe.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the beginning**, `BeginAttempt`, by a key. In order:
    - the consent and the form; a provider of an own key at a profile that is not `adult` — "key not for this profile";
    - the choice by the pair "profile, ID": none — "not found", a choice of a lesson — `invalid_argument`, it began an attempt already — "already exists". "Again" — the previous attempt by the pair, open or closed, from a lesson too: the new one goes outside the lesson;
    - the module is not connected — "not found", as the text of the cause `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND` prescribes: «no module connected with the ID named»;
    - there is no current version for the learner, or the task is not in it — "no current version";
    - the task does not suit the age category — "not suitable": by its own categories, and without them by the module's audience;
    - AI would check the task, and the gate does not admit its kind of call for the profile on its route — "not admitted": a handwriting sheet with one answer slot `anywhere` asks `Reads(CallSheet)`, a workbook `Reads(CallWorkbook)`, a task with a rubric both `Reads(CallEssay)` and `Reads(CallRubric)`; an answer slot in a zone or on the whole sheet, which `tutor` does not read, is always "not admitted";
    - the open attempt is closed by the same transaction: "left", and an overdue one the way the check on request would close it, "unchecked" or "abandoned";
    - the seed is `crypto/rand`, the template's instance is `template.AttemptInstance` and `template.Instance` from the package's numbers.

    The answer is the ID, the open view of the instance (`openview.Task`, JSON of the format with its version) and the digest. The key record holds it: there is nothing in it that the learner sent;
  - **a lesson's segment** in `BeginAttempt` begins on the lesson run's package, after the full check of the lesson run; an open attempt of the segment is given as it is;
  - **the check of the attempt** at each request on it. The attempt's row is held `FOR UPDATE`:
    - none — "not found";
    - closed — "attempt closed" with the reason;
    - the package's status is "no longer gives" — close "unchecked";
    - 7 days without actions — close "abandoned";
    - the package's status is "does not know" — `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN` without a write.

    A closing by the check stays in the database, and the request gets "attempt closed". For a request with a key the closing is committed by `rpc.Keep`, without the key record: a repeat is checked again and sees the closed attempt;
  - **the open attempt**, `GetOpenAttempt`: the beginning, assembled again from the package and the instance, the verdicts of the last submission, the number of wrong answers, the techniques given on a step, the current rung with the words that the attempt stores, or its fallback text, "explain differently" on it, the techniques given on request and the leading question given on request. A reading is not an action: it does not move the last action;
  - **the submission**, `SubmitWork`, by a key:
    - a task by tap is one transaction of `rpc.Once`; the verdict is `observation.Verdicts` on the chosen options, without an observation; the answer slot's `value` is the text of the chosen one;
    - a task that AI reads goes in three parts by `rpc.Across`, and its answer slots get the verdict by what `tutor` read, with "unchecked" and an outcome only for a call that gave no reading; the submission holds its observation;
    - "not yet" in even one answer slot is a wrong answer;
    - all items correct — the attempt is closed "correct", and the answer carries the solved task: the keys of the instance by the places of the answer slots and the solution as JSON of the format;
    - refusals of form, `invalid_argument`: a task with a rubric, which `SubmitEssay` takes, work at a task with only `choice`, no work at a task that AI reads, a tap of the wrong answer slot, out of order or twice.

    A submission is a row of `submissions` with the answer — `SubmitWorkResponse` without `next` — and the number in the attempt, and the key record refers to it: the answer carries the chosen options, which are the learner's work. An answer that closes the attempt carries `next`: in a lesson the next segment or "no more segments", outside a lesson the engine's choice; a repeat adds it to the stored answer;
  - **the beginning of a step**, `BeginStep`: a step of the module's solving steps whose `gives` gives techniques, by place from 1; otherwise `invalid_argument`. The answer is the IDs of the instance's techniques, and the attempt remembers the time when the techniques were first given on a step. A repeat is by the state;
  - **"Done"**: at a task with a verdict — `invalid_argument`. At a task with a rubric without a submission — "no submission";
