  - **the keys** — `rpc.Once`, shared by `AccountService` and the attempts: the lock of the account's row, the reading of the record, the refusals "key already used" and "still going", the answer to a repeat. The fingerprint of the beginning and the submission is SHA-256; a request that carries a PIN or an age is fingerprinted by argon2id. Neither the idempotency key nor the learner's own key enters the fingerprint: there is no hash of an own key anywhere. The record carries the targets: the profile, the attempt, the submission;
  - **the consent and the form** — the shared `rpc.Learner`, the check of the learner's consent with the profile's age category by one query, and `rpc.Malformed`: the catalog, the attempts and `AccountService` call them;
  - **the list of profiles** names the profile's open attempt — the ID and the segment — and its open lesson runs;
  - **the version of `module-kit`** of an attempt comes from the list of the build's modules, `debug.ReadBuildInfo`: the library has no constant of its own. A binary that did not record it begins no attempts;
  - **the tests** take the synthetic module `choices` of the helper `packagestest`: the package vectors of `spec.Format` have no answer slot `choice` at all.

— *Why:*
- "not admitted" at the beginning: the learner does not get a task that no admitted model checks, and the contract names this refusal at the beginning of an attempt on a task that AI checks;
- a reference to the submission, and not the answer in the key record: the key record is without content, and the chosen option is the learner's work; the submission stores it as the observation anyway, and the export takes it from there;
- the verdict is recorded, not computed again on a repeat: a new image with another version of `module-kit` would give the repeat another answer;
- "not connected — not found": this is what the text of the contract's cause says, and "no current version" is for a module that is connected;
- a reading is not an action: opening an attempt on another device and leaving is not work on the task, and the period "abandoned" is 7 days without actions;
- `rpc.Keep`: a closing by the check is the truth about the attempt, not part of the request, and a rollback would bring back an open attempt that cannot be worked on. The same holds for the beginning of a new one: it closes an overdue attempt by its period or package, and not "left";
- SHA-256, and not argon2id, for the submission: the chosen option is found from the fingerprint by brute force — an answer slot has two to four options, and a slow hash does not save from this. But what is chosen lies in the submission of the same profile anyway, while the profile lives, and the key record lives no longer than 2 days and goes with the profile: the fingerprint holds nothing beyond what the database holds. The database does not hold the own key, and it does not enter the fingerprint.

— *Rejected:*
- the submission's answer in the key record: the record would hold the learner's choice;
- beginning tasks that AI checks without admission: the learner would get a task that there is nothing to check with;
- the verdict again on a repeat: see "Why";
- the fields of all future methods at once: their code decides their meaning;
- only "again", without the beginning by a choice: the main path of `app` would go unchecked.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Admission to AI in code

**R45. Admission to AI in code is the package `internal/admission` over `tutor`: the admission table of `spec` at the tag of `go.mod`, the facts from the file `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE`, the lowest rung `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`, `author` or `full`, and the platform keys. They are read at the start of `serve` and by the command `api check`, which the deployment calls before the migrations. The gate asks `tutor`'s own choice for the kind of call — `Reads(call, category, provider)` —, and the beginning of a task that AI checks begins it only where the gate admits it.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the variables:**
    - `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE` — an absolute path to the facts file, outside the git working tree, as for a secret file; on Cloud Run, a volume of an exact version of a secret. There is no variant without `_FILE`: the facts are a file, not JSON in a variable. No variable — no facts: platform AI is closed, an adult's own key is open;
    - `API_ADMISSION_RUNG` — `author` or `full`; without a value — `full`. Another value is a refusal: `Select` would silently read it as `full`. A deployment takes either rung, as its operator names it; the stub of `tutor` is still refused on a deployment, recognized by `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`;
  - **the file** is read by `tutor`: `ReadFacts` by the table of its release. An empty file or only comments — without facts; an unknown provider or fact, not that YAML, no file — a configuration refusal with the variable's name and the words of `tutor`. `serve` refuses on it as on the variables, before the log and before anything is opened;
  - **the command `api check`** checks the same — the configuration of the log, the facts file, the rung and the keys — and serves no requests. The release names it to the deployment of `infra`, which runs it as a Cloud Run job with the same image before the migrations. Success is one row `admission`, a refusal is a text on stderr and code 1;
  - **the log**: the row `startup` and the command's row `admission` carry the version of the table, `facts_file` — whether a file is given, `rung` and `rung_set` — whether the rung is named, and `platform_keys` — how many platform keys are set. The log carries no providers, facts, keys or paths;
  - **the gate** builds `tutor.Tutor` once — with the table, the facts, the rung, the platform keys and the adapters — and asks it the choice it makes before a call. `Reads(call, category, provider)` takes the kind of call — a sheet, `CallSheet`, a workbook page, `CallWorkbook`, the reading of an essay, `CallEssay`, its assessment, `CallRubric` —, the profile's age category and the route: platform AI, and with a provider of an own key, `own_key` and only that provider. Admitted is when an entry is chosen all of whose models the table admits at the deployment's rung or above. On platform AI only providers that have a platform key serve: without a key platform AI is closed even with facts. Without facts the table leaves an adult's own key open, but at the rung `full` there is no admitted model yet, so it is closed there too. The gate reads too: `ReadSheet`, `ReadWorkbook`, `ReadEssay` and `AssessEssay`;
  - **the dependency** is the Go module `tutor`, at the version of `go.mod`, with Go's proxy and checksum database, as `module-kit`; no release file enters the code.

— *Why:*
- the gate at the beginning of an attempt: a learner does not get a task that no admitted model checks, and "without facts platform AI is closed" is checked by an end-to-end test of the beginning;
- either rung on a deployment: the operator names the rung, and a deployment where only the author learns runs at `author`, whose models were checked on the author's handwriting alone; refusing it would take those models from that deployment;
- the parsing of the file at `tutor`: the file's type and its reading are `tutor`'s, and `tutor` asks the same table with the same facts before a call — `api` and `tutor` have one answer;
- the fields in the row `startup`, and not a row of their own at start: the operator sees what the revision started with, next to its version, and the row of a start failure stays one.

— *Rejected:*
- an own parsing of the facts by the types `admissionv0`: two readings of one file would diverge;
- refusing `author` on every deployment: see "Why";
- waiting for a `spec` that embeds `admission/` apart from the rest: the package adds little to the binary.

— *Consequence:* `internal/admission/`; `internal/config/`; `internal/learning/`; `cmd/api/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/build.md`.

## Handwriting check in code

**R46. The submission of handwriting is `SubmitWork` with a crop of the sheet or the crops of a workbook page's filled boxes: the reading by `tutor` (`ReadSheet`, `ReadWorkbook`), the verdict of `module-kit` and the observation at the submission. The request goes in three parts by `rpc.Across`: the checks and the key record "going" in one transaction, the AI call outside transactions, the verdict and the record "done" in the second. The platform AI key is the secret `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY`. AI spending is written by the row `ai_usage`.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the form of the submission:**
    - a sheet — a crop as PNG, whole and no more than 4096 px on a side, as `tutor` reads;
    - a workbook page — `WorkbookWork`: the crops of the filled boxes, each of a box of the task, none twice, no more than 20, each a whole PNG no larger than 512 px a side; a page left blank is no error;
    - no crop, the work of a sheet on a workbook or of a page on a sheet, not PNG, a broken or larger PNG — `invalid_argument`.

    If less than 6 s is left until the request's deadline, the submission is `deadline_exceeded` before the write and the call: a reading cut off by the deadline would stay under the key as "AI failure" forever. Taps go with the crop if the task has answer slots of choice. An adult's own key is the route `own_key` with `tutor.NewOwnKey`; it enters neither the fingerprint, nor the database, nor the log. `clock_offset_minutes` gives the family day of the quota;
  - **three parts** (`rpc.Across`):
    - the first transaction: the check of the attempt, as at each request, the form and the taps, the reserve of the quota, the record "going" with the profile and the attempt;
    - the call outside transactions, until the request's deadline minus 3 s for the write;
    - the second transaction: the attempt is held again — closed by another device — "attempt closed" with the reason, nothing is written, and the record "going" is deleted; otherwise the verdict, the submission with the answer and the observation, and the record "done", by a reference to the submission.

    A repeat while the record "going" is younger than the request timeout (`API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`) is "still going"; older — the request fell, and the repeat goes again. A repeat after "done" reads the submission's answer and does not call AI. Which path, the attempt's task decides, read without a lock — it does not change at an attempt. A request finishes and deletes its own record "going" by the time when it was made, not another one that took its place; the deletion after a refusal goes on its own context, which the request's end does not cut off;
  - **the reading** is by the mode `API_READING_MODE` names: `pair`, two readers whose readings `observation.Join` joins, or `single`, one reader whose one observation `module-kit` checks (`observation.Validate`) and that gets the verdict with no `Join`;
  - **outcome → verdict:** `Answered` — the reading, then `Verdicts` with the taps; two different readings of a pair — "unreadable", "ambiguous". Otherwise the answer slots of AI are "unchecked", and the outcome, built by `outcomeOf`, is:
    - `Failed` → `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`, as are a reading of the wrong number of observations and a reading that `module-kit` did not accept;
    - `NotAdmitted` → `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`;
    - `LimitReached` → `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT` for platform AI, and `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED` with `own_key_limit` for an own key, which names the limit by `Outcome.Limit` of `tutor`, never with `access_returns`;
    - `KeyRefused` → `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`.

    Of a pair the worst outcome is taken — the limit, then the key, then "AI failure". A wrong answer is only "not yet". The outcome is written by the request's row in the field `outcome_cause`;
  - **the observation** at the submission is the column `observation`: only the reading of the answer slots of AI; the taps are in the answer. The crop lives only in the request's memory and is dropped after the call;
  - **the port of reading** in `internal/learning`, `sheetReader` and `workbookReader`, takes the task and the own key beside the reading; the gate's adapter, `gateTutor`, drops both, and `tutor` is given the open view alone. Only the stub of the dev mode reads them, to read a right value and to refuse as a provider would;
  - **the platform key** is Anthropic's alone, `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY`, a secret with `_FILE` — on Cloud Run by a volume of a Secret Manager secret, in the first app by the author's file outside the repository. It is optional: without it platform AI has no route. `api` gives `tutor` one provider adapter, `claude.Dial`, and `API_OPENAI_KEY` or `API_OPENAI_KEY_FILE`, when set, is a configuration refusal;
  - **the spending log** is a row `ai_usage` per model of a call, next to the request's row: the method, the provider, the model, the tokens of input, output and thinking, `cache_write_tokens` and `cache_read_tokens` — what the call wrote to the provider's cache and read from it, beside `input_tokens`, which `tutor` counts without the cache —, `model_ms` — how long the model's call took at `tutor`, the SDK's retries counted in —, `retries`, `first_token_ms`, the call's outcome and `duration_ms`; a call that no model answered is one row of the outcome and the time. They are counts and durations. The log carries neither the crop, nor what was read, nor the text of an error of `tutor`;
  - **the dependency** is the root package of `tutor`, at the version of `go.mod`; the modules it brings are in `THIRD_PARTY`, and `release-licenses` takes the license `COPYING` too.

— *Why:*
- the AI call outside a transaction: a reading takes seconds, and a transaction would hold the account's row and a connection of the pool, and the account's other requests would wait for it;
- "attempt closed", and not a verdict from memory: an answer with a verdict that is not in the database would diverge from the learner model, and the contract already names this refusal at `SubmitWork`;
- one platform key, Anthropic's: the admission table admits no model of another provider, and an adapter `api` does not give is an SDK its binary does not hold; a key set for it is refused at the start rather than held with nothing to call;
- `Join` and the verdict are at `api`, not at `tutor`: the rule of two readers is carried out by `module-kit`.

— *Rejected:*
- the AI call inside the transaction of `rpc.Once`;
- a verdict from memory for an attempt closed during the reading;
- "unchecked" for a task that `tutor` does not read: the learner would solve it in vain;
- one file of keys: a secret per provider is revoked on its own.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/config/`; `internal/logging/`; `internal/store/`; `THIRD_PARTY`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/build.md`.

## The hint ladder in code

**R47. The hint ladder and showing the solution — `GetHint`, `GetRungWords`, `ExplainDifferently`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords` and `ShowSolution` and the fields of the open attempt. `api` derives the rung and its answer slot from the answers of the submissions, the fallback text by the chain of the format, and the model's words pass `leak` of `module-kit` and the form check of `tutor`. The words of the rungs are stored by the table `attempt_rungs`, and the closing of the attempt deletes them by a database trigger. The words come from the model where the deployment admits them, and the learner reads the fallback text otherwise.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the rung** is `min(wrong answers, 3)`, and before the first wrong answer there is no rung. The rung's answer slot is the first answer slot "not yet" in order in the answer of the submission that gave the rung: the first one whose number of wrong answers reached the rung. The trap of the value in it is given again by `verdict.OfSlot` of `module-kit` — by the value that the answer of the submission holds. The answer slot is stored nowhere;
  - **the fallback text** is by the chain the module format sets: on rungs 1–2 the text of the trap value, then the trap advice of its trap, then the rung template of the module manifest; on the third, the simpler case of the answer slot's item, then what the second would give; an answer slot that the code did not find gives the rung template, and the open attempt stays readable;
  - **the admission of words**: the gate's `Words(withWork, …)` asks `tutor`'s choice for the words with the work, `CallWords`, which serves the analysis of its steps and the hint at once, or for the hint without it, `CallHint`; `CorrectWords` asks `CallCorrect`;
  - **the checks of words** — on the model's question: `leak.For` on the attempt's instance and `Search.In`, then `words.Hint` with the module's explanation language and the text of the task statement, by the check `turnedDown`, which takes the form as a parameter. If they did not pass — the fallback text without an outcome; a call without words — the fallback text with a cause, as for reading;
  - **the request for words** to `tutor` is `WordsRequest`: the key of the rung's answer slot, the paragraphs of the solution, the task's traps with the one found, if the catalog describes it, the names of the techniques, the rung, the language, the model's previous questions of this attempt and the presentation style — by the age category: the profile has nothing else that goes to AI. The crop — only for the words of a rung of a task that AI reads;
  - **"Hint"** (`GetHint`, without a key): before the first wrong answer and for a task with a rubric — the IDs of the techniques and the instance's leading question. The attempt writes the time the techniques were first given and the time the question was first given — each only when the task has it: an empty answer is "nothing to hint". This is an action on the attempt. After a wrong answer — the current rung with the words that the attempt stores, or its fallback text, and the question is empty;
  - **the words of a rung** (`GetRungWords`, a key). Refusals:
    - a task with a rubric and no wrong answer — "no rung";
    - the wrong answer is not the last — "not current" with the number;
    - from the fourth wrong answer — "no more words".

    The work goes only when AI reads the task's answer slot, and it is checked as in `SubmitWork`. The words of a rung that the attempt stores already are its words with the flag "repeat", without a call. Otherwise, where the deployment admits no models for words, — `rpc.Once`: the fallback text, the outcome "not admitted", a row `fallback`. And where it admits them — `rpc.Across`, as reading: the call outside transactions, then the attempt is held again. Closed — "attempt closed", a new wrong answer — "not current", and nothing is written; the words of a rung that another request wrote meanwhile are its words with the flag "repeat": the rung's row is one. The box of the first error is a mark of the answer, only with the model's words;
  - **"explain differently"** (`ExplainDifferently`, a key, without the work). Refusals — "no rung", "not current" and "already asked". From the fourth wrong answer — rung 3, and the request names any wrong answer from the third to the last: the words that the learner reads came with the third, and there is no new rung. "Already asked" is checked after the call too — a request beside it that got there earlier took the right. Without admission to platform AI — the refusal "not admitted", and the right stays; on an own key — the outcome "not admitted" with the words that the learner reads, and the right stays too. With admission — `rpc.Across`. The words that came, or the fallback text, the learner reads on the rung from then on. If these are exactly the words they were reading — the outcome "no other words". The right is spent once the call has gone;
  - **the correct answer words** (`GetCorrectAnswerWords`, a key). The refusal "no more words" if the attempt was closed not by a correct answer, is open, the profile began another one, or the words were given already. Where the deployment admits `CallCorrect` and the privacy policy in force on the day, in UTC, of the texts `api` serves is of version 3 or later — and on the stub on any day —, `tutor`'s `CorrectAnswerWords` is called by `rpc.Across`, with the work when AI read the task. The words pass `leak` and `words.Correct` by `turnedDown`; words that fail give no words and no outcome, with the check in `words_check`, and the right stays. Words given are recorded on the attempt, `correct_words_given`. Elsewhere — an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted": there was no call, and nothing is spent;
  - **showing the solution** (`ShowSolution`, a key). The order of the checks: `can_manage` for an adult action, then "no showing", "ladder not passed" and "an adult is needed". Then the closing "solution shown" and the task solved in the answer. A repeat — the same answer from the key's record;
  - **storage:**
    - the columns `attempts.requested_techniques_at`, `leading_question_at` and `correct_words_given`;
    - the table `attempt_rungs` — a row per rung: the source `model` or `fallback`, the words, and for "differently", the time, the source and the words;
    - the trigger `attempts_words_go` — the closing of the attempt, `closed_at` from empty, nulls the words of its rungs in the same transaction, and the number and the source remain;
    - the key's record holds the answer without words, and a repeat adds to it the words that the attempt stores — with the outcome that the request got;
  - **the log:** a words call writes a line `ai_usage`, as reading does, and the outcome is the field `outcome_cause`. The line `request` names the check that rejected the model's words — the field `words_check` from the closed set `empty`, `leak` and `form`: an empty question, a leak by `leak` of `module-kit` — and a task for which the leak check cannot be built —, or the form by `tutor`. The field is only on an answer where the words came and became the fallback text or were turned down. The log carries neither words, nor questions, nor fallback text.

— *Why:*
- the answer slot comes from the answer of the submission, not from a column of its own: the answer holds the verdicts of the answer slots and what was read, so the same `module-kit` gives the same trap;
- a trigger, not a deletion at every place of closing: six paths of code and the job `api sweep` close an attempt, and a missed path would keep the words longer than the attempt;
- the path of the words call whatever the deployment admits: when it admits models, a change of the facts or of the rung is enough;
- an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted" for the correct answer words where they are not admitted: the contract is fulfilled, `app` does not get `unimplemented`, and the right is not spent, as with "explain differently", while there is no call;
- `can_manage` in the handler: the right to showing chooses the kind of action of the request, and the table of methods knows one right per method;
- `words_check` in the log: a set of words that became the fallback text left no trace of which check turned it down, and the log names the check without a record of the words, which `api` does not keep.

— *Rejected:*
- storing the rung's answer slot or its trap in the submission: the answer of the submission gives it again;
- the words in the key's record: they would outlive the closing of the attempt by the record's day;
- deleting the words at every place of closing — for the reason above;
- the ladder without the words call;
- `unimplemented` for the correct answer words;
- correct answer words whose right is spent even without a call: a second request would get "no more words", though there were no words at all;
- the presentation style from the profile: `PresentationStyle` carries only voicing, and it does not go to AI;
- the cause of the fallback text as a column of `attempt_rungs` beside the source: a field of the database about the attempt that only the operator needs;
- an event of its own in the log for the cause: the line of the request is one per answer anyway and already carries its outcome.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/logging/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The lesson in code

**R48. The lesson is led by `internal/learning`: `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun` and `CompleteLesson`, the segment's attempt in `BeginAttempt` and `next` in the answers that close an attempt. The lesson run is checked at every request on it, and the end that the check found is written before the refusal "run ended". The choice of a lesson names the lesson by a column of its own, and a segment's attempt closed "unchecked" ends the lesson run by a database trigger.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **opening** (`OpenLesson`, without a key). A choice that is not of a lesson — `invalid_argument`. The lesson run of this choice or an open lesson run of the same lesson — that one, after the check; an ended lesson run of the choice — "run ended". An open lesson run of the lesson from another choice that the check ended is not a refusal: this choice begins its own. Otherwise a new one — on the package of the current version, if the module is connected (otherwise "not found"), it has a current version for the learner with this lesson (otherwise "no current version"), and the lesson and each of its tasks suit the age category (otherwise "not suitable"). Two openings at once — one lesson run: an insert at a savepoint, and on a violation of the uniqueness of the choice's lesson run or of the lesson's open lesson run the request reads the row again;
  - **the check of the lesson run**, at opening, by ID (`GetLessonRun`) and before a segment's attempt. The order:
    1. a package that the image's index does not know — "package unknown", nothing written;
    2. a package that the image no longer gives — the end "unchecked";
    3. 30 days without actions — the end "abandoned";
    4. the module is not connected, there is no current version for the learner, the lesson or one of its tasks does not suit by the lesson run's package — the end "not suitable" together with the closing of its open attempt "not suitable".

    The end is written, and the request gets "run ended" with the reason. A check that passed is an action on the lesson run. "Lesson passed" checks only steps 1–3;
  - **the answer** `LessonRun` — the ID, the lesson's whole open view (`openview.Lesson`) with the format version, the digest, the places of the task segments that have ended and the segment's open attempt with its place. The list of profiles carries the open lesson runs of each profile;
  - **the segment.** The place is a task segment of the lesson, otherwise `invalid_argument`. The segment has ended already, or before it there is a task segment that has not ended, — "segment unavailable" with the places of those that have ended. The open attempt of this segment — that one. An attempt abandoned by its term is closed by the check, and the same request begins a new one. Otherwise an attempt on the lesson run's package, with its ID and the place. Refusals of admission ("not admitted") do not end the lesson run. A segment has ended — the attempt is closed "correct", "solution shown" or "done", which a segment of a task with a rubric ends with: the places of the segments that have ended the code reads from the attempts and does not store;
  - **`next`** — in the answers of `SubmitWork` with "correct", of `ShowSolution` and of "Done": the place of the next segment of any kind or `segments_ended`. A repeat of a submission adds `next` to the answer of the submission, the record of the key of showing holds the answer with it, and a repeat of "Done" on an attempt closed "done" gives it again by the state of the attempt. Outside a lesson `next` is the engine's choice; a repeat whose close gave no choice gives no `next`, as the first answer had none;
  - **"lesson passed"** (`CompleteLesson`) — the end "passed" when all task segments have ended, otherwise "segments not ended" with the places. A repeat on a lesson run that ended "passed" — the same answer;
  - **the database** — `engine_choices.lesson_id` with a check that a choice of a lesson names the module and the lesson; the trigger `attempts_end_run` ends an open lesson run "unchecked" when its attempt was closed "unchecked";
  - **the last action of a lesson run** is the latest of what is written in the lesson run's row and the last action of its attempts. A request on the lesson run writes its own, and an action on an attempt is written at the attempt. The step `abandoned_runs` of the job `api sweep` counts it the same way — and in choosing a batch too, so that a batch of recent ones does not stop the pass, — and the term of 30 days is `store.RunAbandonedAfter`;
  - **the order of locks:** a request on an attempt of a lesson first holds its lesson run (`store.HoldAttemptRun`), as requests on a lesson run do, and the beginning of an attempt holds the lesson run of the profile's open attempt. So the closing of an attempt that ends a lesson run and the end of a lesson run that closes an attempt take the rows in one order;
  - **the engine choice** in `RunEnd` is given when the request itself ended the lesson run.

— *Why:*
- the end found by the check is written before the refusal: otherwise every request on a withdrawn package would find the same anew, and the check on request is a path of the end;
- the last action from the attempts, not a write to the lesson run's row from a request on an attempt: an action on an attempt has one source — the attempt itself, — and requests that only read an attempt do not move the lesson run;
- the lesson run before the attempt: the trigger "unchecked" writes the lesson run's row after the attempt, and the requests on a lesson run close its attempt after the lesson run's row, and in different orders two requests could deadlock;
- the trigger "unchecked": an attempt on a withdrawn package is closed by the check of a request, the beginning of another attempt and the job after a deployment, and a missed path would leave the lesson run open on a package that is not there;
- the places of the segments that have ended — from the attempts: they hold the place and the close reason anyway, and a second source could diverge from them;
- `next` is any segment, not only a task segment: after a task the lesson can go on with an explanation, and `app` opens the lesson on it.

— *Rejected:*
- checking at opening that the code can begin each task segment: "not suitable" would also mean "not admitted", and the contract does not name that refusal for opening;
- skipping a segment that the code will not begin: nothing in a lesson is skipped;
- the lesson in the choice as one full ID: the choice of a task names the module and the task separately, and the module package is looked up by the module ID;
- moving the last action of a lesson run from a request on an attempt — for the reason above;
- ending the lesson run "unchecked" only at its next request, without a trigger: the lesson run ends in the transaction of the attempt's closing.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/sweep/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The learner model in code

**R49. The learner model is led by the package `internal/model` over `learner-model`: the graphs of the current versions, the evidence of a closed attempt, the log and the cache with its mark. Evidence is written by the catch-up of owed evidence under a lock of the cache row — at a closing in a request or by a job, before the skill map, progress and an adult's correction of a level, and by the step `evidence` of the job `api sweep`. `GetSkillMap`, `GetProgress` and `SetModuleLevel` are in code.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the library** is `learner-model` at the version of `go.mod`. It has no Go dependencies. It names no version of its own in code, and the cache mark takes it from the build — `version.LearnerModel()`, like `ModuleKit()`. `serve` and `api sweep` do not start a build without it;
  - **the graphs** — `learnermodel.NewGraph` of the current version of each module of the index, one for all learners (`packages.Set.Current`): the bands of the manifest, the skills with an assessment, anchor skills, prerequisites and tier, `retired_skills` and the descriptions of the tasks. They are built at start, and a graph that the library rejects is a start failure. A module without a current version is "no current version", and its evidence waits;
  - **evidence:**
    - the task (`model.Describe`) — the full ID, for a template its own; the band by place and ID; the difficulty; the skills of the task and of the items as full IDs, anchor skills as they are; the key — from the author, for a template from the solver, the tags — from the author; for an answer slot — the check kind, `time` among them, and the number of options of the choice;
    - the start band — `BandByGrade` by the profile's grade and the bands of the attempt's version;
    - the outcome — by the close reason and the wrong answers: "correct" without wrong ones — from the first submission, with wrong ones — after hint number `min(wrong ones, 3)`; "solution shown"; "left", "abandoned" and "not suitable" — "left unfinished"; "unchecked". "Done" and an attempt of a task with a rubric give no evidence, nor do "left", "abandoned" and "not suitable" without wrong answers;
    - the items — by the answers of the submissions in order: whether it was submitted, the reasons of the submissions without a verdict before the first verdict, the first verdict and the wrong answers before it, the verdict at the end, the wrong ones before the first "correct";
    - the traps — those that the program found by the value "not yet": `verdict.OfSlot` of the instance's answer slot anew, the skills — from the catalog of the attempt's version, the places of the items;
    - the time of practice — the attempt's last action; the technique at a step and on request; the leading question on request, `leading_question_on_request`; the number of "differently"; "has seen the solution already" — not for a template;
  - **the catch-up** (`Model.Settle`) — under `FOR UPDATE` of the cache row, which it creates if there is none. The mark is the version of `learner-model`, the release of `modules` and the format window. If it matches — the tail of the log after the number counted is appended by `Append`; if older, absent or the cache cannot be read — recomputation from the log by the same step `Append` from the empty state; if newer in at least one part by semver — the state is computed for the answer, and the cache is not written. Then each attempt that owes evidence, by `closed_at, id`: the evidence → `Append` → a row of the log with the evidence that the append returned. The evidence of a version withdrawn as `wrong_key` goes into the log, but not into the state: the recomputation cuts it off. The debt is the closed attempts on packages that the image holds, without a row of the log. What cannot be read or built stops nothing: a row of the log that cannot be read or that the model rejects goes into the state neither at an append nor at a recomputation, and an attempt whose evidence cannot be built or the model rejects stays in the debt and is not written — a fixed release will build it;
  - **who calls the catch-up:** each closing in `internal/learning`, in the same transaction, and "correct" after the closing submission is written; a closing by the job after a deployment or by its step in `api sweep`, in the same transaction as the closing; `GetSkillMap`, `GetProgress` and `SetModuleLevel` — before reading or writing; the step `evidence` of the job `api sweep` after `abandoned_attempts` — the profiles with a debt in pages, each in a transaction of its own. So `api sweep` reads the packages, as `serve` does, from `API_PACKAGES_DIR`. An attempt on a package outside the window gets no evidence (Д27);
  - **the order of locks:** the account, the lesson run, the attempt, the profile's row `FOR KEY SHARE`, the cache row. The profile's row comes before the cache: the deletion of a profile takes it `FOR UPDATE` before its cascade reaches the cache, and the rows of the log refer to it. The job's step takes only the profile and the cache row, and reads the closed attempts without a lock: their closing does not change. The step counts as done a profile about which it wrote at least one piece of evidence;
  - **`GetSkillMap`** — the module is connected (otherwise "not found"), it has a current version for the learner (otherwise "no current version"). A learner's action checks consent, as any learning request does, and an adult action checks the right `can_view`, which the handler checks; an adult's viewing does not require consent. The answer is the digest of the current version; the band of the module's level and when an adult last set it; the state of each skill of the graph with the assessment `verdict`: without practice — only the number of pieces of evidence, with practice — the estimate, the uncertainty and the retention at the time of the request, mastered — the band, the cautious probability and the review date or "time to review"; each anchor skill that the graph names as a prerequisite or in `teaches` — with its mastery, the reason and the skills that teach it. The engine's modules for reading are the connected ones that have a current version for the learner;
  - **`teaching_modules`** of each anchor skill of the skill map, at any mastery of it: the modules of the pinned index whose current version suits the profile by audience and names the anchor in `teaches` of its index record, but for those connected to the profile. The order is by the bytes of the ID: the schema of the format writes the module ID in lowercase ASCII, and for ASCII the order of bytes and the order of UTF-16 code units, which the contract asks for, coincide. The list is computed before the request's transaction: the index does not change while `serve` runs. `teaches` is taken from the index record, not from the package's graph, and `module-kit` builds the record from the same graph. The current version is by `packages.Set`, as for the catalog, so a withdrawn newest version that teaches the anchor does not offer the module if the current one does not teach it;
  - **`GetProgress`** — the profile's attempts, newest first, 50 at a time; the page marker is an opaque base64 of the beginning and the ID of the last attempt, a foreign one — `invalid_argument`, and so is one of a time before 1970 or after the year 9999: an attempt begins by the service's clock, and the contract gives its beginning as a Timestamp of protobuf, which ends with the year 9999. For an attempt — the full ID of the task, the version, the full ID of the lesson, the close reason, the wrong answers, the beginning and the closing; `chosen_at` and `reason_code` — for an attempt outside a lesson those of the choice of its task, for a segment's attempt those of the choice that began the lesson run, and only for a choice with a code, so an attempt "again" has none; `essay_submissions`; `lesson_title` and `skill_titles`. Repeating traps — on the first page, from the log: a trap of two closed attempts or more by full ID, without the evidence of versions withdrawn as `wrong_key`, with `description` and `trap_advice` of the catalog of the version of the last of them; if its package is not in the image, there is no trap (Д27). The first page also carries the last correction of each module's level of the log;
  - **the titles** of progress come from the package of the version the attempt went on, not the current one, as the traps do; when the image no longer holds it, they are empty. The lesson's title is at an attempt of a run, open or closed, by `lessonOf`. The skills' titles are only at an attempt closed "correct", "solution shown" or "done": the primary skills of its items, each item's own or else the task's, each once, in the order of the items and of their lists, by the titles of the package's graph; a skill the graph does not know has no title. `primarySkills` of the choice is not used: it sorts the skills and adds the task's beside the items' own. Nothing is stored for them: the titles live in the package.

— *Why:*
- the catch-up of owed evidence, not evidence only in the transaction of the closing: an attempt is also closed by the job `api sweep` — in a batch, without packages in memory, — by a trigger and by the previous image during a deployment. One function under one lock gives evidence to each of them in the order of closing, and there is no second path that could diverge from the first;
- `wrong_key` is not appended at an append: otherwise what was appended would diverge from what a recomputation gives;
- what cannot be read is skipped: the catch-up goes at each closing and each reading of the model, and one attempt whose evidence cannot be built would otherwise refuse the profile everything — neither finishing a task nor opening the map. The append and the recomputation skip the same, so the cache and the log do not diverge;
- the profile's row before the cache row: the deletion of a profile and the catch-up would otherwise take two rows in different orders and could deadlock;
- the graphs at start: the current version is one for all and changes only with the image, and a graph that the library rejects is better seen at start than at every request;
- the traps from the log: the evidence carries the traps that the program found anyway, and the digest for the filter `wrong_key`, and a second list could diverge from it.

— *Rejected:*
- evidence strictly in the transaction of the closing on all paths: the job `api sweep` would build it itself, and the closings of the previous image would stay without evidence;
- the task's description and the trap catalog in the attempt at its beginning: evidence would be built without a package too, but the attempt's row would grow for a case that in format v0 happens only with a move of `api` to a new line of `spec`;
- storing the traps found in an attempt apart from the log — for the reason above;
- an error of building evidence is a refusal of the request: for the reason above;
- evidence that the model rejects — into the log without the state: the log is append-only, and a fixed release could no longer rewrite it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/model/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/sweep/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The engine choice in code

**R50. The choice is led by `internal/learning` over `engine`: "something else" — `ChooseNext`, `next` outside a lesson for "correct", showing the solution and "Done", and `RunEnd` of a lesson run that the request itself ended. The engine's input is the learner model after the catch-up, the engine's modules with the pool and the lessons that the code will begin by the request's route to AI, the lesson runs, the last closed attempt and the summary of issues. Each choice is written with everything that a repeat needs — migrations `0010` and `0011`, — and "nothing to give" too.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the library** is `engine` at the version of `go.mod`. It names no version of its own in code, and the record takes it from the build — `version.Engine()`; `serve` does not start a build without it;
  - **the engine's modules** are the connected ones that have a current version for the learner and that the deployment's list of modules names, when it has one: the engine chooses none of the lessons and tasks of a module the list leaves out. The graph of each is the same one that the learner model gets;
  - **the route to AI** — the pool, the lessons and the open runs that the engine gets go by the route that the request names — `own_key_provider`, or at `SubmitWork` the provider of `own_key` —, as `BeginAttempt` by the same route would begin. Every request that gives a choice — `ChooseNext`, `next`, `RunEnd` — passes its route; the record of a choice gets no field for it, and a repeat gives the choice from the record;
  - **the pool** is the tasks of the current version that suit the learner and that `BeginAttempt` would begin: one rule `admitted` for the beginning and for the pool. It asks the gate of the task's kinds of call on the route — `CallSheet` for a sheet with one slot `anywhere`, `CallWorkbook` for a workbook, both `CallEssay` and `CallRubric` for a task with a rubric —, so such a task is not begun only while its calls are not admitted; a task in a zone and on the whole sheet is not begun (Д24). Each task has the traps of the values of its items. When none of the kinds that read the work is admitted for the learner — `readsAdmitted`: a sheet, a workbook page, or an essay's two calls admitted together —, the pool is limited to tasks a tap checks: a learner does not write a work that nobody can read;
  - **the lessons** are those that suit the learner whole and each task segment of which the code will begin: the engine does not give a lesson in which the learner would get stuck (Д26);
  - **the records:**
    - of the lesson runs — the lesson, its skills and the end; the lesson's skills on an earlier package are named as the graph of the current version names them, and for a lesson run whose package left the image, the lesson's skills come from the current version: so a lesson passed stays passed. An open run whose next task segment the code would not begin waits, and neither it nor its lesson goes to the engine;
    - attempts of a task with a rubric closed "Done" — the task and the time of the closing;
    - the last closed attempt — the task, whether "left", the lesson of the lesson run, the result on each primary skill;
    - the summary of issues — for a skill, the time of the last issue by the skills that the record of a choice keeps, with the reason code and the result of the skill's last closed attempt; for a module, the number of choices; for a task, when its solution last went to the device;
    - the result on a skill — from the attempt's evidence: the items where the skill is primary, with the first verdict, how many of them are "correct" and whether the solution was shown;
  - **the record of a choice** is a row of `engine_choices`, migration `0010`. The references `after_attempt_id` and `after_lesson_run_id` name the closing or the end after which the choice was given as the next: a repeat of the request finds the same choice. "Nothing to give" is a row of its own kind `nothing` with a flag whether the answer carried "not admitted", migration `0011`; in the answer its `choice_id` is empty, as the contract requires;
  - **the skill from the map** holds: `next` and `RunEnd` call the engine with the skill of the last choice, if the engine's modules still know it, and `ChooseNext` with the request's skill, and without it returns "My subjects". A skill that none of the engine's modules knows is "not found", and the open attempt stays;
  - **"something else"** (`ChooseNext`, with a key) closes the open attempt "left" — or as the check of the request would close it, — and gives a choice. A repeat by the key — the same answer;
  - **"nothing to give"** carries the outcome "not admitted" only when closed AI left out of the pool a task that the code would begin with it, a workbook page or a task with a rubric that its admission alone left out among them: otherwise there is no outcome, as the contract requires;
  - **the room of the quota** that the choice asks is the larger of the typical tasks with handwriting and with a rubric: the choice gives no task whose start the quota refuses;
  - **a choice that cannot be made** — the model or the engine rejected the input, the task cannot be described — gives an empty choice for `next` and `RunEnd`, and `app` asks for "something else": the answer to a closing is not rejected. A database failure rejects, as always. For `ChooseNext` both are a failure;
  - **a repeat of `next`:** a repeat of `SubmitWork` outside a lesson finds the choice by the closed attempt — and "nothing to give" with its outcome, — and if there was no choice, `next` is empty, as in the first answer; a repeat of `ShowSolution` takes the answer from the key's record;
  - **an end that nobody will get** asks for no choice: `OpenLesson` whose check ended an open lesson run of the lesson from another choice begins its own and does not call the engine.

— *Why:*
- the pool "what the code will begin": otherwise the engine would give a task that the beginning rejects with "not admitted", and the learner would see a refusal after the choice;
- the skills of the issue in the record: the summary is gathered from the records, and it does not need a package that left the image;
- `after_*` in the record of a choice: the answer to a submission is stored without the next, and a repeat must give the same choice without calling the engine again;
- the outcome only for a narrowed pool: the contract requires "not admitted" only when closed AI left the learner without tasks, not when there is nothing to give for another reason;
- an empty choice on a refusal of the engine in the answers to a closing: the learner has already answered, and a refusal would take the verdict from them.

— *Rejected:*
- a pool of all tasks that suit the learner: the engine would give what the beginning rejects;
- the skills of the issue by the package when the summary is gathered: the package may no longer be in the image;
- "nothing to give" without a record: the skill from the map that "something else" without a skill gave to the engine would hold by the earlier record, and a repeat would lose the outcome;
- the choice in a separate package `internal/choice`: the rule "can be begun", the selection by category and the admission gate live in `internal/learning`, and a separate package would repeat them.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `go.mod`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Deployment jobs in code

**R51. The release of `api` names to the deployment of `infra` four commands by the roles of its record `api_jobs`: `api check` — before the migrations, `api migrate` — the migrations of both databases, `api after` — when the new revision has taken all the traffic, and `api sweep` — on a schedule. `api after` closes "unchecked" the open attempts and lesson runs on packages that the image no longer gives, one at a time, in the order of a request, and writes the evidence of those whose package the image holds. The same two steps go in the daily `api sweep` too, before the closing of the abandoned ones.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the deployment commands.** `docs/build.md` names them at the release's tag, and `infra` runs them by role, not by name:

    | Role at `infra` | Command | When | What it reads |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `check` | `api check` | before the migrations | the file of facts and the admission rung |
    | `migrate` | `api migrate` | before the revision takes requests | the direct strings of both databases |
    | `after` | `api after` | when the revision has taken all the traffic | the pooler string of the database of `api` and the image's packages |
    | — | `api sweep` | on a schedule | what `serve` opens, but for the origin of `app` |
  - **`api after`** is one pass of two steps, `gone_attempts` and `gone_runs` — `logging.AfterSteps`, — with the flag `-for`, as for `sweep`: 9 minutes by default. It reads `API_LOG_LEVEL`, `API_DATABASE_URL` with `_FILE` and `API_PACKAGES_DIR`; it opens neither OpenFGA nor the sign-in service. It reads the packages as `serve` does, and what the image "no longer gives" it judges by their index. The log is a line `swept` per step, with counters; exit 1 if the pass is not clean;
  - **"no longer gives"** — the image's index knows the digest, but the version is withdrawn or outside the window. A digest that the index does not know the job does not touch, as the check on request does not: it is a package of the previous image, and the index is cumulative;
  - **closing one at a time** — each attempt and each lesson run in a transaction of its own, which holds the rows the same way as a request: the account `FOR KEY SHARE` with a check of the mark "being deleted", the lesson run, then the attempt. The attempt is closed "unchecked", the words of its hints are deleted by the trigger, and its lesson run is ended by the trigger `attempts_end_run`. A lesson run without an open attempt is ended "unchecked" by itself, and an open attempt that the first step did not reach is closed with it. The job holds the attempt and reads it again before closing: the daily job closes the abandoned ones in a batch without holding their lesson runs, and an attempt that it closed "abandoned" while the job held the lesson run stays "abandoned", and the lesson run is ended "unchecked" by itself. What a request closed while the job was choosing, and an account that a deletion marked, are neither work nor a failure;
  - **the evidence** is written by the catch-up `model.Settle` in the same transaction, as with a closing in a request. An attempt on a package outside the window does not get it: the package is no longer in the image (Д27);
  - **an end of a lesson run that the job found** asks for no engine choice: nobody will get it, as with the end of a lesson run of another choice at `OpenLesson`;
  - **in `api sweep`** the same steps go after the deletion of profiles by period and before `abandoned_attempts`, in the order of `logging.Steps`: an attempt that neither the job after the deployment nor a request reached the pass will close "unchecked", not "abandoned";
  - **`dev-check`** calls `api after` after stopping the server, on the dev database of Neon.

— *Why:*
- one at a time, in the order of a request: a batch `UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED`, as for abandoned attempts, would hold the attempt before the lesson run that the trigger takes, and a request holds them in the reverse order, and they would deadlock;
- a command of its own, not a flag of `sweep`: the job needs one pooler string, and `sweep` also opens OpenFGA and the sign-in service — the job of `infra` would have to be given their secrets;
- the steps in `sweep`: the daily job is the safety net, and without the steps the safety net would close such attempts "abandoned" — another outcome in the evidence.

— *Rejected:*
- a batch `UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED` — an order of locks reverse to that of a request;
- a flag `api sweep -after` — the job needs neither the rights nor the sign-in service;
- closing only by the job after the deployment, without the steps of `sweep`: a failed job would leave the attempts until the check on request, and otherwise until "abandoned" after 7 days;
- closing also the attempts on a digest that the image's index does not know: the previous image during a deployment would close the attempts of the new one.

— *Consequence:* `cmd/api/after.go`; `cmd/api/sweep.go`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/logging/`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.15.0

**R52. The legal texts, the consent contract with the operator's confirmation of the form, the problem report, the category by the country's age of digital consent and the export `api.zip` are code of `api` by the parts of `spec`: `legal/`, `consent-age/` and `export/` come in the Go module `spec` as `spec.Legal`, `spec.ConsentAge` and `spec.Export`, each with its own `embed.FS` and its types.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the table of rules** — the rows of the methods; `GetLegalTexts` is open, as the open views are. The adult and the learner for the export of a profile and the storage of works are one row `can_learn`, and the handler itself asks for `can_manage` for an adult action;
  - **the texts** — the package `internal/legal`: the part is read by the rules of its text, `spec/legal/README.md` — the schema, `legal.files`, `legal.versions`, `legal.set-texts`, `legal.markdown`, `legal.operator`, and with the part of the previous release also `legal.in-force-unchanged`, — on `goldmark` with GFM and `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema`, and it passes all the vectors of `legal/vectors/reading/`. The names in the refusals of the rules match the vectors; for the step of the schema only the step itself is compared: the paths of the errors of its own schema library need not match the paths of ajv. The platform has the texts of the tag, a copy has the folder `API_LEGAL_DIR`, which `serve` and `api check` read at start, and a folder that a rule refuses stops the start;
  - **the versions in requests** — one check, by the day of the request in UTC: the request names the current version of each of its texts in force and no other one; a text in force that the request did not name, and a next version before its day, are `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT`. `GetLegalTexts` gives the next versions in `next_texts`. The record of consent stores the version, and the account the versions of the policy and the terms, the table `account_texts`;
  - **the category** — the package `internal/consentage` by the text of the part `consent-age/`, on the vectors of `consent-age/vectors/category/`: the category, the day and `applied`, and "ask again" — when the country's age today is above `applied`. The country code is one of the codes of the schema; the EU, the EEA and the United Kingdom stay closed by the list of countries;
  - **the consent contract:**
    - below the country's age — a parent's consent with a request code unique among those waiting;
    - from that age — the learner's own consent: their profile is created at once and waits for `GiveOwnConsent`;
    - `RestateAge` recomputes the category and, if the consent must be given by someone else, puts the profile to wait for a new one — by a new row of consent. The earlier confirmed one stays a minimal record, and its scan for its own period;
    - `CAUSE_AGE_NEEDED` — for learning requests (`rpc.Learner`);
    - the limit of those waiting is `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS`, and only consents without a profile are counted;
  - **the scan of the form** — the port `internal/forms` with two adapters: a Cloud Storage bucket through its JSON API with the token of the service account — only on a deployment, `API_CONSENT_FORMS_BUCKET` and `API_CONSENT_FORMS_PREFIX`, — and a folder outside git, `API_CONSENT_FORMS_DIR`. Without them `SendConsentForm` is `unavailable`. The table `consent_forms` remembers where the scan lies and until which day: for a deleted confirmed consent — 5 years, for a waiting one — today; the daily job erases it, step `consent_forms`. Each sending puts the scan under an object name of its own, and the database writes the row about it before the write to the storage, with the period "today", and binds it to the consent in the request's transaction. So a scan whose transaction did not go through, and one replaced by a new one, are erased by the same job, and the transaction does not wait for the storage. The operator — `api consents list | confirm | reject`;
  - **the problem report** — `ReportProblem` with a key and a period of review of 3 days, the table `problem_reports`, whose references to the account, the profile and the attempt become empty at deletion; the operator — `api reports list | review`, and the step `problem_reports` deletes those reviewed a year ago;
  - **the storage of works** — `SetWorkStorage` and a record of each turning on and off; a teenager over an adult — a record with it. While it is on, `PutWork` keeps each work of the profile, and switching it off erases them in its transaction;
  - **the export** — the package `internal/export`, `api.zip` by the part `export/`, checked by its schemas in tests:
    - evidence, the state and observations — protobuf-JSON with the field names of `.proto`;
    - the descriptors of `learner-model` — an image of `buf build` from the `.proto` file of its release, which `release-verify` accepted. The recipe `just learner-model-proto` puts them in place, and the CI job "Modules" compares them;
    - the export reads the database in one read-only snapshot, and the rungs and submissions of the profile's attempts by two queries;
    - what the package tells an attempt — traps, techniques, the leading question — is taken while the package is in the image (Д27). The items — from the answers of the submissions; for an item without a verdict "correct" or "not correct" — the verdict of its last reason, for one not submitted — "unchecked";
    - the stored works go in `works`;
  - **the message limit** — 11 MiB, for the sake of a scan of up to 10 MiB;
  - **the database** — migrations `0012` and `0013`; the deletion of an account erases its accepted texts too.

— *Why:*
- in one move and everything at once: the first app needs a contract shared with `app`, and a partial move would leave `app` on the answers "not implemented";
- the port of the storage and the folder — on the model of the port of the sign-in service: the code is checked without a bucket, and the real bucket comes with the deployment of `infra`;
- the rules of `legal/` in Go: a self-hosting copy reads its folder at start, and there is no Node in the image; a divergence from the check of `spec` is caught by the vectors;
- the descriptors from the release's file, not from the Go module of `learner-model`: the export must carry the `.proto` of the version that computed, from its verified file, as the text of the part `export/` requires, and a new dependency, a `.proto` compiler, is not needed — the image is assembled by `buf` of the stage `toolchain`.

— *Rejected:*
- the scan of the form without a storage, `unimplemented` until the bucket — the consent contract would go without its step;
- the folder of texts of a copy later — its rules will be needed by the first self-hosting;
- a `.proto` compiler at run time — a needless dependency in the binary;
- writing the scan to the storage inside the request's transaction — a failure of the transaction after the write would leave a scan that the database does not know about, and a slow storage would hold the row of consent.

— *Consequence:* `spec/`; `go.mod`; `internal/legal/`; `internal/consentage/`; `internal/forms/`; `internal/export/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `cmd/api/`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/build.md`.

## The packages of format 0.4

**R53. `modules.json` pins a release of `modules` on the tag of `spec` of `api`, with the digest of its index, and its packages of window 0.4 go into the image. In the learner model an answer slot of the kind `time` is a kind of its own, and a leading question on request goes into evidence of kind 2 as the field `leading_question_on_request`. The path of the first app on the pinned packages is checked by a test of the CI job "Modules".**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the pinning** — `modules.json` with the release and the digest of the index, by the same rules as every pin of a release of `modules`; the packages add 15.0 MB to the image (measurements 1.3);
  - **format 0.4 in the model** — `learner-model` at the version of `go.mod`: `CHECK_KIND_TIME`, the kind of evidence 2 and its field 20. Evidence of kind 1 in the log stays as it is: the field is "not written" for it, and the model reads it as "there was no question". The attempts of the author's dev database closed before the field where "Hint" gave the question are written the same way: the model counts their answers as data without help, and the append-only log does not correct them. The cache of the state is recomputed on a change of the version of `learner-model`;
  - **the question in the evidence** — `store.Owed` takes `leading_question_at` of the attempt, and the evidence writes `LeadingQuestionOnRequest` from it. `api` gives the leading question only on the request "Hint" before the first wrong answer — that is what the field means;
  - **the kind `time`** — `checkKinds` maps it to `learnermodel.CheckKindTime`; a rubric still has no kind in the model, and there is no evidence with it (О-17);
  - **the export** — the `.proto` and the descriptors of `learner-model` from the release's file, `just learner-model-proto lay`: the JSON of the evidence carries `leading_question_on_request`;
  - **the release test** — `internal/learning/release_test.go`, `TestThePinnedReleaseIsLearnedOn`. It reads the folder that `API_RELEASE_PACKAGES` names, as `serve` does, and checks that the engine gets each lesson of `olympiad-math` for an adult whose handwriting AI may read, that the answer slot `time` is described by the kind `time`, and the owner's path: the connection `ConnectModule`; "something else" gives a lesson or a task, and what is given begins; the first lesson opens, and its first task segment begins; a tapped task from the engine's pool with a leading question — `GetHint` gives the question, a wrong option gives a wrong answer, `GetHint` after it gives the words of rung 1, the key gives "correct", — and evidence of kind 2 with a question on request and the outcome "after a hint". It walks the owner's path on the board module too, on the stub of `tutor`. The test takes the task by its properties from the engine's pool, not by ID. Without the folder it is skipped: `ci-test` goes without network and on vectors. `just ci-modules` after `modules-check` lays out the release with `api packages`, runs this test with `-v` and fails if the line of its pass is not there: a renamed test would otherwise leave the job green. The job "Modules" has 25 minutes: the build of the test in the stage `toolchain` goes without a cache. The verdict of the answer slot `time` the test does not check — AI reads it, — it is held by the vectors of `spec.Format` in `module-kit`;
  - **`dev-check`** — the owner's catalog with `olympiad-math`, `ConnectModule`, `LICENSE` of the device part — `200`, and "something else" gives a lesson or a task.

— *Why:*
- the latest release on the same tag of `spec` as `api`: the next move has less to catch up;
- `learner-model` with format 0.4 together with its packages: with them in the image the first app gives tasks with a question and `time` at once, and evidence written without the field would stay in the log forever as kind 1 without a question;
- the release test in "Modules", not in `ci-test`: it needs network and the packages, and "Modules" goes to the release on each pull request anyway. The vectors of `spec.Format` hold the rules, and the release test holds that exactly the pinned packages give the path of the first app.

— *Rejected:*
- only `dev-check` and the job "Image" without the release test: they check neither the path of a task with a question nor the evidence, and `dev-check` runs only at the author's;
- choosing the test's task by ID: the next release of `modules` may rename or withdraw it.

— *Consequence:* `modules.json`; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; `internal/store/model.go`; `internal/model/`; `internal/export/`; `internal/learning/release_test.go`; `justfile`, `ci-modules`; `.github/modules.sh`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the job "Modules"; `scripts/dev.sh`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## `tutor` v0.1.24

**R54. `api` gives `tutor` one provider adapter, Anthropic's: an OpenAI key that is set is a configuration refusal. The admission gate asks for the same choice that `tutor` makes before a call — `ChooseCall` by the kind of call, for every call `api` makes. The tutor's words are admitted and come from the model.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the dependency** — `tutor` at the version of `go.mod`. Its provider adapters are plugged in by the service, `tutor.Config.Adapters`, and its root pulls in no SDK; a provider key without an adapter is an error of `tutor.New`;
  - **the adapter** — `admission.Adapters`, only `"anthropic": claude.Dial`, with no option of its own: the adapters of `tutor` take nothing from the environment. The binary has no `openai-go/v3` and `coder/websocket`, and neither has `THIRD_PARTY` of the `justfile` (measurements 1.4);
  - **the stub** — with `API_TUTOR_STUB=1`, off a deployment alone, the gate gives `tutor` the stub's adapter, `admission.StubAdapters`, instead of `claude.Dial`, and a key `stub` instead of the platform's: the adapter makes the client of Claude's catalog with no key, for its names and prices, refuses every call, and answers a check of a key by the key's ending. A key of Anthropic beside the switch is a refusal of the configuration;
  - **the OpenAI key** — `API_OPENAI_KEY` and `API_OPENAI_KEY_FILE` stay declared, but when set they are a refusal that names the variable, not the value: a deployment that still passes the key learns of it at start, instead of holding a key that nobody calls;
  - **the gate** asks every call by its kind, `ChooseCall`: the reading by the kind of the task, `Reads`; the words without the work — "explain differently" and a task a tap checks — by `CallHint`, and with the work by `CallWords`, which admits only with the hint on the analysis's model, so the gate compares no models itself — `Words`, and the kind is `admission.WordsCall`, by which the quota reserves too; the words after a correct answer by `CallCorrect` in `CorrectWords`, called by `TellCorrectWords`. The gate no longer holds the table, the facts and the rung itself: `tutor` holds them, and the choice of both is one;
  - **the own key** — the gate answers whether an own key is open by `OwnKeyOpen`, the choice of `ChooseCall` for the kinds of call `api` makes, and `ListOwnKeyProviders` lists the providers by it; `CheckOwnKey` calls `Tutor.CheckKey` by the gate's `CheckKey`. An own key without a provider or without a value the submission and the words methods reject before the gate: the gate would ask for the route of the platform, and the call for that of the own key;
  - **the outcome** — `outcomeOf` takes the whole outcome of `tutor`: the provider's refusals are told apart — a limit with a cause and a refused key, and for a pair the worse outcome —, and the reason of a limit of the own key goes as `own_key_limit` too;
  - **the tests** — the gate admits the words on the rung `author` with an Anthropic key and with an own Anthropic key, and not on the rung `full`, for a child, without a platform key and with an own OpenAI key; an OpenAI key and a configuration that `tutor` rejects are a configuration refusal; the adapter is only Anthropic; an own key without a provider or a value is `invalid_argument`. The tests of `learning` with the way out to Anthropic blocked pass: none of them calls the provider.

— *Why:*
- the tutor's words are what the first app lacked;
- only Anthropic: the admission table lets in no OpenAI model, and an adapter that `api` does not give is an SDK that is not in the binary and that need not be checked for licenses and vulnerabilities. A new provider is an adapter and a variable;
- `ChooseCall` is the same choice that the call will make: a record admitted on another prompt will not open a task that the call then rejects.

— *Rejected:*
- both adapters: the configuration is the same, but the binary is a third bigger, and an OpenAI key opens nothing;
- `table.Select` in the gate: it takes the capability's first record, whatever prompt it measured.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/config/`; `internal/learning/`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Quota in code

**R55. The tariffs, their caps per family day, the typical tasks and the reserve by the kind of call are a table of the package `internal/quota`, which a pull request changes; the unit is whole microdollars. There are three tariffs: `closed_slice` — profile $0.50, account $1.50 —, `probe` — $0.08 and $0.10 — for a run where the quota runs out, and `dev_check` — $5.00 and $15.00 — for the account of `dev-check` off a deployment alone. The account's tariff is changed by the operator's command `api tariff -tariff <tariff> <code>`, which finds the account by its invite code.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **where the values are** — in the code of `api`, the package `internal/quota`, not in the deployment's variables: these are values of the product, changed by a pull request of the configuration. The account's tariff knows its caps only from this table, and for a tariff that is not in it, and for an account without a tariff, platform AI is closed. The list of tariffs of an invite code is the same one: `invite` keeps none of its own;
  - **the unit** — `quota.Micro`, whole microdollars, like the quota counter of the export. The dollars of `tutor` are converted up to a whole microdollar: the quota errs towards spending, not towards a free call. Below zero and not a number is zero;
  - **tariffs:**

    | Tariff | Profile | Account | What for |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `closed_slice` | $0.50 | $1.50 | the closed slice, the code's default |
    | `probe` | $0.08 | $0.10 | a run with `app` where the author sees the quota run out: enough for a typical task and a few more checks |
    | `dev_check` | $5.00 | $15.00 | tenfold `closed_slice`, the account of `dev-check` on the dev projects alone |
  - **the typical task** — $0.06 with handwriting and $0.06 with a rubric. With handwriting, two checks, words and praise: by the prices that `tutor` expects, `Tutor.Price`, ≈ $0.050, and the first app measured $0.014 per check (measurements 2.1). With a rubric, two readings with an assessment, ≈ $0.06;
  - **the reserve by kind** — when `Tutor.Price` gives no expected price: a sheet — $0.014 (measurements 2.1), words — $0.0182, the hint without the work — `CallHint`, "explain differently" and the words of a task a tap checks — the same as the words, since the same prompt and model without the picture costs no more, reading an essay and the assessment — $0.015 each, a workbook page — $0.014 per crop. A kind to which `tutor` gives an expected price, as the words after a correct answer, `CallCorrect`, has no value in the table;
  - **changing the tariff** — `api tariff -tariff <tariff> <code>`. The code is the only argument, written in any way, as `invite.Read` reads it; one statement finds the account by the code it is bound to. A code that nobody redeemed and an account with the mark "being deleted" are a refusal without a change, and what is not a code is a usage refusal that does not repeat the argument. The output is only the tariff the account now has, and the log writes only a failure with the moment `tariff`: neither the account ID nor the code. The tariff of the code itself does not change: it passes to the account only on redemption;
  - **`dev_check`** — the quota is counted as on any tariff — the reserve, the settlement, the room for a typical task —, and only the caps differ, so the check still runs on the real count. A run spends 174,210 micro-dollars of the account, 62,210 of them the owner's profile: `closed_slice` holds 8 runs a day, `dev_check` 86 of the account and 80 of the profile (measurements 8);
  - **`dev_check` off a deployment alone.** `quota.DevOnly` marks it. `api invite -tariff dev_check` and `api tariff -tariff dev_check` are a usage refusal when `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is set, naming it, before the database is opened (`config.Operator.DeployedBy`). From an operator's own terminal, where the operator's commands run, no variable tells a deployment's database, so `serve` deployed reads `dev_check` as a tariff with no caps: platform AI is closed to such an account "by default", `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`, and `GetQuota` gives it no share. The caps are given, not taken away: `quota.CapsOf` has those of `dev_check` only when told it is off a deployment, which `serve` tells `learning` and `account` by `OffDeployment` when `config.Config.Deployed` is false, and `store.Charge.OffDeployment` and the argument of `store.QuotaSettled` carry to `capsOf`; a caller that does not say so gets none;
  - **how `dev-author` gets it** — `scripts/dev.sh`, `check_account`: the invite code of the check's account is kept in `<UID>.code` — `dev-author.code` — beside the dev file, where it really lies, in the volume `novatrail-api-dev`, readable by its owner alone; `owner_account` writes it as soon as `CreateAccount` answers, so that a step that fails after does not lose it. With the code kept, `api tariff -tariff dev_check` gives the account the tariff at each run; an account with no code kept is deleted by `DeleteAccount` on the fresh sign-in of its custom token and made anew by `api invite -tariff dev_check`, whose code the file then keeps; a deletion the sign-in service did not finish, refused "account gone" after, stops the check naming `just dev sweep`. A redeemed code opens no account again, so it may go on the command line of `api tariff`. `dev-app` keeps `closed_slice`: its quota is what the runs of `app` see.

— *Why:*
- code, not deployment variables: two deployments of one release with different caps would be two platforms that the log is silent about, and ten numbers are too few for a parser of their own and refusals of configuration of their own;
- whole microdollars: a sum of integers comes out the same in any order, and the export writes the counters the same way;
- `probe`: a run with `app` has to show the author the exhaustion within a few checks, and the caps of `closed_slice` run out only after ≈ 16 tasks; on `probe` a new task with handwriting closed after two or three checks (measurements 3). Only the operator can give it to an account;
- the code as the argument: an invite code is redeemed and will not give an account a second time, and the operator holds it anyway;
- `dev_check`: on `closed_slice` the owner's profile of `dev-author` ran out after a few runs of `dev-check` in a day, and the check stopped at its first step of handwriting.

— *Rejected:*
- deployment variables and an embedded YAML file — for the reasons above;
- dollars as a floating-point number: the sum would depend on the order of settlements;
- changing the tariff by the account ID: the ID would leave the platform;
- changing the code's tariff too: it gives nothing anymore, and the record of what the code was issued with is more useful;
- a value of its own for the typical task from `Tutor.Price` on each request: it is a value of the configuration, and the price of `tutor` changes with its release without a pull request of `api`;
- for `dev-check`: waiting for a new family day after a stop — it stops chains of tasks; setting its account's counters to zero at its start — it writes to the counters past the count of the quota;
- for the path of `dev-author`: `api tariff -uid` off a deployment — the account's ID is rejected above, and the UID would be a second key of the command; an update of the dev database by hand — a step of the author's that the next new account would need again;
- for the guard: the commands' refusal alone — it catches a command run as a job, and none from a terminal.

— *Consequence:* `internal/quota/quota.go`; `internal/store/quota.go`; `internal/config/config.go`, `operator.go`, `agreements.go`; `cmd/api/invite.go`, `tariff.go`, `serve.go`; `internal/learning/learning.go`, `quota.go`; `internal/account/account.go`, `quota.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`.

**R56. The family day in code is a pure function `quota.Clock` and three fields of the account: the offset, the start and the end of the current day. The offset and the day are written in the first transaction of the request to platform AI, which holds the account's row anyway, not in a transaction of their own. An offset outside −12…+14 hours or not a multiple of a quarter of an hour is skipped, and the day goes by the previous one.**
- **What was decided:**
