  - **the day function** — `quota.Clock{Offset, Day}`: `Sent` is the clock after a request with an offset, `Today` the day as of now, without a write. A new offset is stored at once, and takes effect from the next day: the current one ends at its own time. The next day ends at the first midnight by the offset after "now" that is no earlier than 20 hours after the end of the previous one, and begins at the midnight 24 hours before its end — or at the end of the previous one, if that midnight has not come yet or came before it. So a day is from 20 to 44 hours after a change of the offset and exactly 24 without one, and after a break without requests it is the day that holds "now". While there was no offset, the day is UTC. A server clock that went back stays within the stored day;
  - **the start of the day** — the column `accounts.day_starts_at`, migration `0014`: the first day by a new offset may begin at the end of the previous one, not at midnight, and the counters are keyed by the start of the day. Deleting an account erases it together with the offset and the end of the day;
  - **where it is written** — `store.FamilyDay` in the first transaction of each request that calls platform AI: there the reserve is taken too, and the account's row is already held `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` by the idempotency key. Only what changed is written. A request with an own key writes no offset: it comes only with platform AI. The start of an attempt and "something else" carry no offset and read the day by `store.Today`;
  - **the log** — the offset is in no line.

— *Why:*
- the request's transaction, not one of its own: the reserve is needed within the same day, and a transaction of its own would hold the account's row a second time and could diverge from the reserve;
- a column for the start, not deriving it from the end: the start of the first day by a new offset cannot be derived from the end;
- the offset is stored at once: so it is in mathtrail-standalone too (sources 7.1), and "now" after a break already belongs to the next day, which goes by the new offset.

— *Rejected:*
- a transaction of its own for writing the offset — for the reason above;
- refusing a request with an offset out of bounds: such a request is counted by the previous one;
- the start of the day by the account's earliest counter: a day without a reserve has no counter.

— *Consequence:* `internal/quota/`; `internal/store/quota.go`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/learning/quota.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R57. The quota counters are two tables, `account_quota` and `profile_quota`, a row per day with a reserve, and the flag "platform AI closed" is the table `platform_ai` of one row, migration `0015`. The race of two reserves is held by the account's row, which a request under a key holds `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` anyway, and the reserve reads the counters `FOR UPDATE` — against the settlement, which goes in a transaction of its own. The step `quota` of the job `api sweep` deletes the counters a day after the end of their day.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **tables** — one per owner: the profile and the account each have their own foreign key with `ON DELETE CASCADE`, the primary key "owner and start of the day" and the end of the day, by which the job deletes them. Spent and in reserve are whole microdollars, no less than zero. A counter is created by the first reserve of the day;
  - **the flag** — the row `platform_ai` with the time when platform AI was closed, empty when it is open; a second row is kept out by the key. `store.ClosePlatformAI` sets it, keeping the time of the first closing, and `store.OpenPlatformAI` clears it;
  - **the reserve** — `store.ReserveQuota` in the transaction that holds the account's row: the account's tariff, the day's counters `FOR UPDATE`, the check of both caps (`quota.Room` — the profile's first), then the addition to the reserve of both by one insert with `ON CONFLICT`. If it did not fit, nothing is written, and the cause is the profile's cap, the account's or "no tariff". `store.QuotaFits` is the same check without a write or a lock;
  - **the settlement** — `store.SettleQuota`, in a transaction of its own: the reserve leaves both counters, and the price of the call is added to what is spent. A counter that no longer exists is not a failure;
  - **the period** — the step `quota` of the job `api sweep`, before `keys`: it deletes the counters whose day ended more than a day ago. The job runs once a day, so a counter lives no longer than 2 days after the end of its day;
  - **deletion** — the profile's counters go with the profile by the key, the account's in `ClearAccount` together with the account's fields, and with them the start of the day. Deleting a profile does not touch the account's counters;
  - **the export** — `QuotaCounters` of the profile and the account: the start of the day, spent and in reserve, in the order of the days.

— *Why:*
- two tables: each has a simple key and its own cascade, and a table with the kind of owner would keep two partial unique indexes and a check that exactly one of the owners is set;
- the account's row, not a lock of the day of its own: every request under a key holds it anyway, and the counters of a new day do not exist before the first reserve — there is nothing to lock; a conditional `UPDATE` on two counters would need the first rolled back when the second did not fit;
- `FOR UPDATE` of the counters: the settlement does not hold the account's row, and without a lock the reserve could read the counters before the settlement;
- the settlement in a transaction of its own: the call has already been made, and the settlement must not roll back with a refusal of the request's second transaction — an attempt closed by another device.

— *Rejected:*
- one table with the kind of owner — for the reason above;
- `FOR UPDATE` of the day's row and a conditional `UPDATE` — for the reasons above;
- deleting the counters more than 2 days after the end of their day: with a daily job a counter would live up to 3 days, past the period of the counters.

— *Consequence:* `internal/store/quota.go`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/sweep/`; `internal/export/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R58. Every call of platform AI takes a reserve in the first transaction of the request by `Tutor.Price` of the kind of call and is settled right after the call in a transaction of its own by `Usage.USD`, before the second transaction and whatever happens to the answer. Settling the running requests when an instance stops needs no mechanism of its own: those that live to see the stop settle themselves, and those cut off leave the reserve as the spending of their day.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **where the reserve is taken** — the phase `Prepare` of `rpc.Across`, the first transaction, which holds the account's row and writes the key's record "going", — right after the family day and only when the call will go: platform AI, the call is admitted, the rung has no words yet. The amount is `quota.Reserve`: the expected price of `tutor` through `admission.Gate.Price` or the value of the kind. Reading a sheet is `CallSheet`, of both readers together; the words of a rung and "differently" reserve by the kind of call of the request they send — `CallWords` when the crop goes, `CallHint` without it, as `tutor` picks it. The reading of a workbook page reserves by `Price(CallWorkbook)`, or else by the value of the kind for each filled box, and a page left blank, which goes to no model, reserves nothing. A submission of an essay reserves one charge of `Price(CallEssay)` and `Price(CallRubric)`, or else of the values of the kinds, whole or not at all, kept as two parts that each call settles by its cost; an essay not read gives the assessment's part back, settled at nothing. An own key neither takes nor spends the quota;
  - **if it did not fit** — there is no call, and the request answers as the contract requires:
    - reading a sheet — the AI's slots "unchecked", the outcome `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`, the end of the day, — no wrong answers are added;
    - the words of a rung — the fallback text with the same outcome, and it becomes the rung's words, as on a failure of AI;
    - "explain differently" — the refusal `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`, and the right stays. The refusal is `rpc.Keep`: the day written by the request stays;
    - a tariff without caps — the same, but with the outcome or the refusal `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED` and without the next day: such platform AI is closed "by default", not until tomorrow;
  - **where the settlement is** — the phase `Call`, right after the call: `store.SettleQuota` in a transaction of its own, on a context without the request's cancellation, with a limit of 5 s. The second transaction goes only after it: if it refuses — another device closed the attempt — the settlement stays. A settlement that failed leaves the reserve as the spending of the day, and the request's line names the failure;
  - **the price** — `cost`: "not admitted" is zero, there was no call; no record at all is the reserve; all records exact is their sum, so for a pair where one reader refused it is the record of the one that answered; a record by the worst case is the reserve, or the sum of the exact ones if it is larger;
  - **the log** — the line `ai_usage` carries the model's price `cost_micro_usd` and the sign `billed`: the worst case goes into the log. An exhaustion is the line `quota_exhausted` beside the request's line: the method and the cap — `profile`, `account` or `tariff`, without IDs or sums;
  - **an instance stopping.** On a stop the server waits for the running requests, and they settle themselves. A request cut off by the stop leaves the reserve as the spending of its day, like a crashed process, and a day after the end of the day the counter goes;
  - **a repeat** by the key after "done" reserves nothing: it answers from the record without reaching `Prepare`.

— *Why:*
- the reserve in `Prepare`: the account's row is already held there, and the reserve goes together with the record "going" — if one rolled back, so did the other;
- the settlement before the second transaction and in its own: the call is paid for even if the answer is refused afterwards, and a rollback of the second transaction must not return money to the quota;
- no mechanism for the stop: the server's waiting on a stop lets the requests finish anyway, and one cut off is the same case as a crashed process, whose reserve stays as spending.

— *Rejected:*
- the settlement in the second transaction: the refusal "attempt closed" would roll it back, and the reserve would stay as spending while the price is known;
- a phase of settlement in `rpc.Across` itself: only requests of AI need it, and in `Across` `Call` sets them anyway;
- a log of reserves in the database for settling after a crash: the reserve stays as spending, and the log would add data about attempts with no need;
- a reserve by the worst price: it would close checks long before the cap.

— *Consequence:* `internal/rpc/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/quota/`; `internal/store/quota.go`; `internal/logging/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R59. Platform AI is closed to a new task that AI reads — by a quota that is not enough for a typical task, by the flag "platform limit" or by a tariff without caps — by one rule at the start of a task, of a segment and in the engine choice. The flag is set by the outcome "limit reached" of a call on the platform's key and is read on every request; the operator's command `api reopen` opens it. The cause of "nothing to give" is kept by the choice's record, migration `0016`. An open lesson run whose next task segment the code would not begin waits and does not go to the engine.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the start of a task and of a segment** — for a task that AI reads, on platform AI, after admission: the flag is the refusal `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT`; what is left of the day the account holds being less than a typical task for the profile or the account is `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`; a tariff without caps is `failed_precondition` `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`. The typical task is `quota.TypicalHandwriting` for a task with handwriting and `quota.TypicalRubric` for one with a rubric. The refusal rolls the start back, and the lesson run stays open; an open attempt of the segment is given as it is and waits. The day is by the time of the request, `store.Today`: the start carries no offset;
  - **the engine choice** — the same rule, `closedTo`, with room for the larger of `quota.TypicalHandwriting` and `quota.TypicalRubric`, so that it gives no task whose start the quota refuses: when AI is closed, the pool and the lessons have only tapped tasks, and the choice's record marks the pool as limited. "Nothing to give" with a pool so narrowed carries the outcome "not admitted", "quota" with the time of the next day, or "platform limit". The cause and the time are kept by the columns `engine_choices.nothing_cause` and `next_family_day`, migration `0016`, and `not_admitted` is written as well, for the release that still serves during a deployment. A choice recorded before `0016` reads as "not admitted";
  - **the own key** — neither the quota nor the flag touches it. Every request that gives an engine's choice names the route of the own key, and the choice judges by it: on the own key neither the quota nor the flag narrows the pool or keeps a run waiting, only the admission of its provider does, and "nothing to give" there carries "not admitted" alone;
  - **the flag** — the row `platform_ai`. It is set by the outcome "limit reached" of a call on the platform's key — right after the call, together with the settlement —, and the first setting is a warning `platform_limit` in the log with the method, without IDs. While it is set, no reserve is taken and there is no call: reading is "unchecked", the words are the fallback text, both with the outcome "platform limit", and "differently" is the refusal `resource_exhausted`. The flag is read on every request that would call platform AI, and at the start and in the choice: one row by the primary key, by one function `platformLimit`;
  - **the command** — `api reopen`: clears the flag, prints whether it was set, and writes the line `platform_ai_opened` with `was_closed`. The limit at the provider the operator lifts themselves, before the command;
  - **a run that waits.** The next task segment is the first task segment of the lesson that has not ended, on the run's package. Whether the code would begin it is judged by the same rule as the pool and the lessons: the task suits the learner, AI is admitted to read it, and with AI closed by the quota or the flag, only a tapped task. If it would not, the run is not in the engine's input, and its lesson is not among the module's lessons. The lesson is removed too: otherwise a run on an earlier package, whose lesson in the current version begins whole, would come back as a new lesson — `OpenLesson` gives the open run of a lesson. The cost of this rare case: a removed lesson of the author's order, while the run waits, does not hold back the lessons that come after it — the engine reads the order by the lessons it got;
    - **a package the image no longer gives** does not wait: such a run goes to the engine, and an open one ends "unchecked". Its segments ended on the earlier package, and judging them by the current version would mean taking the wrong segment;
    - **it waits, it does not end.** The run stays open, and the period of 30 days runs as before. When AI opens, the next choice gives it by the engine's first branch. A run whose task segments have all ended waits only for "lesson passed" and always goes to the engine;
    - **"nothing to give"** carries an outcome also when a run is waiting whose segment is rejected only by AI: by the quota, the flag or admission;
    - **the engine's input.** `engine` accepts a last attempt of a segment whose lesson is not named among the open runs: `Last.Lesson` is read only by the first branch, comparing it with the runs, and the checks of `Choose` do not link `Last` with `Runs`. This needs no new field of the choice's record: which run waited follows from `pool_limited` of the record, the run's package and the attempts of its segments closed before `chosen_at` — by `chosen_at` a repeat restores the runs and the closed attempts that the choice saw from the export. There is no code of the repeat in `api`, and `store.EndedSegments` reads the segments as of the request: a repeat has to limit them by the time of the choice;
    - **an open attempt of a segment** does not change the rule: "something else" first closes it "left", and `app` finds the open attempt with the profile;
    - in code — `history` in `internal/learning/choose.go` (`waits`, `nextTask`, `withoutLesson`); `store.Run` has the run's ID;
  - **the order and the reads:**
    - the reserve for reading is only on the path of `rpc.Across`: on `rpc.Once` nobody would settle it;
    - the order of the counters' locks — the profile, then the account — in the reserve, the settlement and the deletion of an account, which deletes the profiles before the account's counters: otherwise the settlement and the deletion could wait for each other;
    - a failure of the settlement and a failure of setting the flag the request's line names together;
    - the day of the start and of the choice is by their time of the request, not by a second reading of the clock;
    - "not admitted" is the only outcome without a call that `tutor` tells apart from a call without records, so a failure without records is counted as the reserve, as for a call with no message at all; the two readings of the account's row per reserve — the tariff and the day — are left: they are in one transaction, which already holds the row.

— *Why:*
- one rule for three causes: a learner does not begin a task that nobody can read today;
- the flag is read, not cached: there are several Cloud Run instances, and a cache would keep closed an AI that the operator opened, or open one that another instance closed;
- a warning, not information: it is a signal to the operator that platform AI is closed to everyone;
- the cause in the choice's record: a repeat of `SubmitWork` takes `next` from the record, and without the cause the outcome "quota" would become "not admitted";
- a run that waits: with AI closed, the engine's first branch would give again and again the lesson of the open run whose segment the start rejects, and the learner would never get to the tapped tasks.

— *Rejected:*
- a cache of the flag in the process for a minute — for the reason above;
- the quota at the start of a tapped task: the code checks it without AI;
- the cause of "nothing to give" only in the answer — a repeat would lose it;
- giving a waiting run to the engine as ended, "abandoned" with a made-up time: the engine would read an end that did not happen, and the choice's record would not match the database on a repeat;
- a change of `engine` where the first branch would give only a lesson from those that `api` gave, or "nothing".

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/quota/`; `cmd/api/reopen.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.15.1

**R60. `.claude/settings.json` takes the template's keys word for word: the session hook of `spec`, compaction at 400 thousand tokens, the claude.ai connectors turned off and `claudeMdExcludes`. The Go of the devcontainer comes from the container of `spec`, and `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` is the template's kind `go`, file for file.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the hook** calls `node` with `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/spec/tools/session/main.mjs`: it stops a prompt that starts another task in the same session and lets the same task and a prompt without a task phrase through; RUN.md has the template's paragraph about one session;
  - **the measurement before** the keys is the row of `api` in the measurement of the sessions' tokens (measurements 4.2).

— *Why:*
- the keys at once: RULES.md says that the hook is in `.claude/settings.json`, and the hook's file comes with the same tag; without the keys the rule would describe what `api` does not have;
- the tasks of `api` need no claude.ai connectors, and compaction at 400 thousand is the platform's threshold of a session.

— *Rejected:*
- only the hook, and compaction and the connectors later: these keys of the template have nothing of their own for a repository, and `api` takes them as they are.

— *Consequence:* `.claude/settings.json`; `.devcontainer/gopls/`; `RUN.md`; `CLAUDE.md`.

## Withdrawal on a schedule

**R61. A withdrawal of a version is noticed by the job "Withdrawals" — `.github/workflows/withdrawals.yml`, once a day at 05:17 UTC and by hand. It takes the newest release of `modules` by the redirect of the public `releases/latest`, checks the signature of its index by `release-verify`, and fails while the index of the newest release withdraws a version that the pinned release gives. The failing run is the signal to the author: there are no permissions beyond `contents: read`, and no repository settings or secret either.**
- **What was decided:**
  - "gives" — an entry of the pinned index whose format version is in the window of `api` and which it did not mark `withdrawn`. This is also an earlier version on which lesson runs still go, not only the current one: a withdrawal closes them "unchecked" too, but also only with a pull request;
  - the rule is `module-kit`'s, not one of its own: `moduleindex.WithLatest(release, latest)` gives the pinned index with the withdrawals of the newest, as for the command `open` of `canvas`. An index that does not keep the entries of the pinned one as they are — for example, the index of an older release — is `ErrLatest`, and the job fails too;
  - in code — `packages.Withdrawals`: the pinned index by the digest of `modules.json`, the newest by `moduleindex.Read`, and a file that does not read as an index is `ErrNewestIndex`. The list is in the order of modules and versions, with the withdrawal reason and a mark of the current version;
  - `cmd/modules-check` gets the flag `-latest`; it does not combine with `-main-config` and `-main-index`, and an empty one is a command-line error, not a check of a pull request. Without withdrawals the command prints "no version that <release> gives is withdrawn" and exits with 0, with withdrawals a line per version, and exits with 1;
  - the recipe `just ci-withdrawals`: the tag comes from the address that `https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules/releases/latest` leads to, only `…/releases/tag/vX.Y.Z`, otherwise a refusal. A tag lower than the pinned one by `sort --version-sort` — the mark "latest" was set by hand — is replaced by the pinned one: the index of an older release would fail every run. The index and the bundle of the newest come from the public address, without a token, and `release-verify` on every run, even when the newest is the pinned one. The index of the pinned one is held by its digest, and it needs no signature: when the newest is the pinned one, it is the same file, without a second download. Then `modules-check -latest`;
  - the workflow — one job on `ubuntu-24.04` with a timeout of 20 minutes: `actions/checkout` with the submodule `spec` — for `release-verify` — and `.github/modules.sh ci-withdrawals` in the stage `toolchain-core`, whose image it restores from the cache by the path and the key of the job "Image"; a schedule runs on `main`, which holds the entry. The recipe runs `release-verify` and `cmd/modules-check`, and no test, so it needs no nss_wrapper. `.github/modules.sh` takes the recipe as an argument, `ci-modules` or `ci-withdrawals`;
  - the job is not a required check and does not run on a pull request: its signal is about a release of `modules`, not about a pull request;
  - the test — the index vectors of `spec` `withdrawal` and `next-release` from `spec.Format`: the first withdraws `example/smallest` 0.1.0, the current version, and `example/times-tables` 0.1.0, already withdrawn in the pinned one, gives no signal; the second and the pinned index in the role of the newest give nothing; `next-release` the other way round — `ErrLatest`. With indexes of its own — a withdrawal of an earlier version without the mark of the current one, and a withdrawal of an entry outside the window, which gives no signal.

— *Why:*
- a failing run: it needs no write permissions, settings or secret, and the notification goes to whoever last changed the cron line, that is, the author, who commits (sources 4.4). The failing run repeats every day while the withdrawn version stays current — exactly as long as the harm lasts of a withdrawn version that goes into new attempts unnoticed;
- once a day: a withdrawal reaches learners only with a pull request and a deployment anyway, so a day is the order of the author's reaction. The minute is not round, because the start of every hour is a time of high load (sources 4.4);
- the newest by the redirect, not through the REST API: the address is public, without a token and without the API's rate limit, like the other files of the release. GitHub's newest is the most recent release that is not a prerelease or a draft by its creation, and a new release is the newest by default (sources 4.4). `modules` puts out releases by the shared `release.yml` on one line, so it is also the newest by semver. A release older than the pinned one the recipe replaces with the pinned one, and a release between the pinned one and the newest the check would not tell from the newest: that is possible only if the mark "latest" of `modules` is set by hand. The releases of `modules` are immutable, and their index files are uploaded before the publication, so a release that the address already names does not stand without an index;
- the newest's signature on every run, even when the newest is the pinned one: no file of a release enters CI unverified, and a run has one path, so `release-verify` is checked every day, not only on the day of a new release.

— *Rejected:*
- an issue from the job: it needs `issues: write`, and whether an issue reaches the author depends on their subscription to the repository, which cannot be checked — the subscription API requires the scope `notifications`;
- a pull request for a new release from `GITHUB_TOKEN`: it needs the setting that lets GitHub Actions create pull requests, which is off in `api`, and CI would not run on such a pull request, so the required checks would wait (sources 4.4). A move to a release is also a task with a decision, not one line;
- a pull request from a PAT or a GitHub App: the same pull request, but with a secret in the repository and a step of the author's;
- four times a day — up to four emails a day for the same pull request; once a week — a withdrawn version would go into new attempts for a week;
- the rule in shell and `jq`: it would diverge from what `module-kit` gives;
- the newest by the REST API's list of releases with a token: a token in the stage `toolchain-core` for what the public address gives;
- the newest by the tags of `git ls-remote`: a tag that has no release yet would give a false failing run;
- checking the newest in the job "Modules" on every pull request: it would be silent while there is no pull request — precisely the time a withdrawal must not go unnoticed.

— *Cost:*
- a failure of the network, GitHub or Sigstore fails the job too, and it can be told from a withdrawal only by the run's log: `modules-check` prints the lines of withdrawn versions, and a failure names its step;
- a public repository without activity for 60 days loses its scheduled workflows until they are enabled on the Actions tab, and the notifications then go to whoever enabled it (sources 4.4);
- the run is free: standard GitHub-hosted runners in public repositories stay free (sources 4.4).

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/withdrawals.yml`; `.github/modules.sh`; `justfile`, `ci-withdrawals`; `cmd/modules-check/`; `internal/packages/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.16.3

**R62. `api` works the way of the template's kind `go`: the `.claude/settings.json` of the template, a thin CLAUDE.md with the checks of each part in its rule `.claude/rules/<part>.md`, external facts in `docs/sources.md` and measurements in `docs/measurements.md`, and the plugin `gopls-lsp` of `spec` loaded by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` of the devcontainer. The contract's `AccountService.GetQuota` has the rule `toLearn` in the table of methods.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **`.claude/settings.json`** — what the template's generator gives the kind `go` with the name `api`, with the line of `claudeMdExcludes` that keeps `spec`'s own rules, `**/api/spec/.claude/rules/**`, out of this repository's sessions. It holds neither `extraKnownMarketplaces` nor `enabledPlugins`: the plugin `gopls-lsp`, from `spec/claude-plugins/`, comes by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` of `containerEnv` in `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, from this container's own path;
  - **CLAUDE.md** — by the template: the reader of `docs/`, the exclusion of the rules of `spec`, the plugin, the budgets of size and the rules of the parts — [go](../.claude/rules/go.md), [modules](../.claude/rules/modules.md) and [ci](../.claude/rules/ci.md) —, each with the checks of its part in its `paths`;
  - **the documents** — `docs/` in English; a claim about the outside world cites its record of [sources.md](sources.md), and the results of a measurement go to [measurements.md](measurements.md);
  - **`GetQuota`** — the rule `toLearn` in `internal/rpc/methods.go`, as the skill map has: for a learner `can_learn`, for an adult `can_view`, and the handler checks the right by the action.

— *Why:*
- the rules of `spec` at the tag describe a repository built by its template, and the rules of a repository that differs from it would describe what `api` does not have;
- a rule of a part is loaded only when a session reads or edits a file it matches, so a session pays only for the checks of what it changes;
- the plugin by the container's own path: the marketplace that `.claude/settings.json` records lands in the `~/.claude` the containers share, by the path of one of them, which the others do not have;
- `GetQuota` under a rule: a test holds the table of methods equal to the contract's methods, and a method the table does not know the checks reject.

— *Rejected:*
- the move to the tag alone, with the way of working of its line taken later: the rules and the template at the tag would describe a repository other than `api`;
- the plugin by `extraKnownMarketplaces` and `enabledPlugins`: the shared `~/.claude` above.

— *Consequence:* `.claude/settings.json`; `.claude/rules/`; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/sources.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `internal/rpc/methods.go`.

**R63. The documents of `api` are measured by budgets of size: `docs-budgets.json` keeps the block `repository` of `spec` and gives a limit of its own only to a section or an entry that stays over it, each its size plus 10–20%. RUN.md holds its rules and table 1 alone, with nothing that RULES.md or CLAUDE.md says: a task outside the plan of an epic is a row of table 1, its body in `run/TASKS.md` until it is done, and done, it goes with its gist sentence to the current epic's archive, between epics to the archive of the epic that comes next. An epic's archiving measures its sessions as a row of the measurement of the sessions' tokens (measurements 4.2).**
- **Budgets.** The sizes are read by `docs-check-repo` itself (measurements 4.1). A section near its limit that the next edit adds to is compressed, or its limit is raised in that edit.
- **RUN.md.** What repeats RULES.md and CLAUDE.md — the hook of a session, the list of what `docs-check-repo` checks, the container, the language, the rules of citation — is not there, and neither is the history of the epics: it is in the archives. The rules keep their numbers, by which the plan and the archives cite them.

— *Why:*
- a limit is raised only for what remained over, and in review the edit of `docs-budgets.json` is seen;
- RUN.md is read by every session in full, and what it repeated from RULES.md and CLAUDE.md, which are loaded into every session anyway, was paid twice; the archives are not read whole;
- a row of table 1 and a body in `run/TASKS.md` lose nothing that a section of RUN.md for tasks outside an epic held.

— *Rejected:*
- limits of their own for sections within the block — a margin larger than `spec` sets for itself;
- a section of RUN.md for the tasks outside an epic, even as an empty heading: table 1 and `run/TASKS.md` hold them;
- rules without numbers — they are cited by number.

— *Consequence:* `docs-budgets.json`; `RUN.md`; `run/TASKS.md`; `docs/plan/`; `docs/measurements.md`; `CLAUDE.md`.

**R64. RUN.md's rule of what needs the author's confirmation takes the template's exception for a task's checks without the author: the path in the browser with Playwright and a custom token on the dev project, the calls of AI on the stub of `tutor` in the dev mode of `api`, a real call only where the task checks the model's words, under the provider's spending limit and the task's cap, and nothing on devices. A move to a newer release of a Go library compares the code `api` builds of it by syntax trees without comments.**
- **The comparison** — of each Go file without tests that differs, the syntax tree with the comments dropped: equal trees are the same code, and a file `api` does not build, such as a command of the library, is left out. The `.proto` files of `learner-model` are laid again by `just learner-model-proto` and compared as they are.

— *Why:*
- the exception is what lets a task that builds the stub or the custom token, and every task after it, be checked without the author;
- the libraries are best moved while their code is the same: a later move would mix with code of their own;
- the trees rather than `git diff` of the text: a library's move may rewrite its comments alone, and a tree without comments shows exactly what code changed.

— *Rejected:*
- staying on the older releases of the libraries until a task needs them: the move would come with that task's own code.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `go.mod`.

## The own key in every choice

**R65. Every choice of the engine goes by the route to AI that the request names, as `BeginAttempt` by the same route would begin a task: `ChooseNext`, `next` of `SubmitWork` and `ShowSolution`, and the end of a lesson run that `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun`, `CompleteLesson` or the beginning of a segment gives. On the own key the pool, the lessons and the runs that wait are judged by the admission of its provider, with no quota and no flag, and "nothing to give" carries "not admitted" alone. A profile not of the category `adult` that names a provider is refused `CAUSE_KEY_NOT_FOR_PROFILE`, and `SubmitWork` with `own_key_provider` beside `work` or `own_key`, `invalid_argument`. The route is not written to the choice's record.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the route** is the provider of the own key without the key, `own_key_provider`, at `ChooseNext`, `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun`, `CompleteLesson`, `ShowSolution` and `Done`, and `BeginAttempt` names it by its own field. At `SubmitWork` it is the provider of `own_key`, or `own_key_provider` when the submission sends neither `work` nor `own_key` — a task a tap checks. Empty is platform AI;
  - **the choice** — `choose` in `internal/learning/choose.go` asks `Gate.Handwriting(category, provider)`, and `closedTo` — the quota and the flag — only on platform AI. `candidates`, `begins`, `waits` and `history` take the provider, so that the pool, the lessons and an open run that waits go by `admitted(task, category, provider)`, the same rule as the beginning of a task. An admitted own key leaves no task out for AI, so "nothing to give" carries an outcome there only when its provider is not admitted — `closedCause` gives "not admitted";
  - **the requests that give a choice:** `nextChoice`, `nextAfter`, `endRun` and the checks of a run — `checkRun`, `checkRunGiving`, `holdRun`, `openRun` and `segmentBegun` — carry the provider of the request; the end of a run that `BeginAttempt` of a segment finds gives a choice by that `BeginAttempt`'s provider. A repeat gives the choice from its record: under an idempotency key it names the same route, as below, and a repeat of "lesson passed", which goes by the state of the run, gets the recorded choice whatever route it names;
  - **the refusals:** `ownKeyFits` at each of these requests, before anything is read or written; `Done` gives no `next`, and checks the provider all the same; at `SubmitWork`, `own_key_provider` beside `work` or `own_key` is `invalid_argument`, after the checks of the own key and of the provider, so that a child's request gets "key not for this profile" as everywhere;
  - **the record** stays as it is: the admission of handwriting depends on the category and the provider, not on the task, so the pool is either whole or of the tasks a tap checks, and `pool_limited` with `age_category` is what a replay needs. On the own key "nothing to give" has one cause, `not_admitted`, which `nothing_cause` of the migration `0016` keeps;
  - **the idempotency key:** `own_key_provider` is part of a request's fingerprint (`internal/idem` leaves out `idempotency_key` and `own_key` alone), so a repeat that names another provider is `CAUSE_KEY_ALREADY_USED`, as at `BeginAttempt`.

— *Why:*
- the contract asks it: an owner with an own key whose platform quota is spent would get only tasks a tap checks, or "nothing to give" with the quota, though the beginning by the key takes the handwriting;
- the route is one parameter through the same functions, so `ChooseNext` and `next` go by one route, never by two;
- no route in the record: a replay needs nothing more, and the record keeps no data of which provider the owner uses, which the export does not carry either.

— *Rejected:*
- `ChooseNext` by the route first, and `next` with the end of a run later: between the two they would go by different routes;
- a column of the provider or of the route in `engine_choices`: new data of the owner in the database and the export with nothing that reads it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/idem/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The stub of tutor in the dev mode

**R66. In the dev mode `api` calls a stub of `tutor` instead of `tutor`'s readings, words and assessment: `API_TUTOR_STUB=1`, refused on a deployment, as the emulator of the sign-in service is, and beside a key of platform AI. The stub reads a crop by its shape — wider than tall the right value, taller than wide a wrong one, square illegible —, words texts of its own, ends a call on an own key by how the key ends, and spends what `tutor` expects the call to cost; admission and the prices stay `tutor`'s. `just dev` runs on the stub, `just dev-ai` on the models.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the switch** is `API_TUTOR_STUB=1` in `config.Admission`, so `api check` refuses it before the migrations too. The dev mode is off a deployment: neither `K_SERVICE` nor `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is set, as for the key file of the service account. With the switch, a variable of a deployment, a key of Anthropic — `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY` or its `_FILE` — or a value other than `1` is a refusal of the configuration that names the variables. The lines `startup` and `admission` carry `tutor_stub`;
  - **admission** — `admission.Load` gives `tutor` the stub's adapter, `admission.StubAdapters`, and a key `stub` that stands for the platform's: the adapter makes the client of Claude's catalog with no key, whose names and prices `tutor` reads for the choice and `Tutor.Price`, and refuses every call of a model. So `ChooseCall`, the pool, the start of a task and the reserve of the quota go on the real table, facts and stage, and no SDK client with a key exists in the process. The stub admits no kind of call itself: `Stub.Reads` and its other admissions answer as the gate does;
  - **the stub** is the package `internal/tutorstub`, behind the ports of `internal/learning` — `admitter`, `sheetReader`, `workbookReader`, `essayReader`, `rubricAssessor`, `wordsTeller` and `correctTeller` —, by the option `learning.WithTutor`, which `serve` gives with the switch;
  - **the reading of a sheet.** The crop's size, by `png.DecodeConfig`, picks the value of the one slot bound anywhere, which `module-kit`'s verdict finds on the instance (`verdict.OfSlot`), out of the key, the options of a choice written by hand and the whole numbers 0–999:
    - wider than tall — the first value it holds right;
    - taller than wide — the first trap value it holds not right, else the first such value of the rest;
    - square, or no value found — `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`.

    The stub reads by as many readers as the gate's mode of reading has — one in `single`, two otherwise —, each the same: a value with the crop box of the whole crop, `0 0 1000 1000` in thousandths, and a reason with no box, as the rules of an observation have it. A crop that is no PNG, a task with no slot bound anywhere, or a request cut short fails as `tutor`'s reading does;
  - **the reading of a workbook page** — each filled box by the shape of its crop, as a sheet: wider than tall the box's right value, taller than wide its first trap value or a value its check holds not right, square — or no value found — `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`, the values from the box's check on the instance by `module-kit`'s verdict. Every reader gives every box of the page in the task's order, whatever order the crops came in, a box that sent no crop `UNREADABLE_BLANK`, and a value with no crop box, as `tutor`'s reading of a page gives it. Of `tutor`'s checks before its call it keeps those of the form: a task that is no workbook page or has no box, a crop of a place with no box or of one box twice, more than 20 crops, a crop that is no PNG or is larger than 512 px a side, and a request cut short fail. A page with no crop goes to no model, with no usage and no refusal of an own key, as `tutor`'s;
  - **the reading of an essay** — by the shape of the sheet's crop: wider than tall a text of the stub's own — synthetic, in English, with no number, since a reading of an essay names no language —, taller than wide `UNREADABLE_BLANK`, square `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`. One reader, as `tutor`'s one model, gives the observation of the one slot bound `sheet`, a value with no crop box. Of `tutor`'s checks before its call it keeps those of the form — no open view of the task, a task that is no handwriting sheet, has no slot bound `sheet`, two, or one bound otherwise, a crop that is no PNG or is larger than 4096 px a side — and a request cut short;
  - **the assessment** — every criterion met but the last — a rubric of one criterion is not met —, each with a synthetic evidence that nothing shows, keeps or logs, and a feedback of its own of two sentences, in English or Russian, English in another language, with no number and no quote, which pass `words.Feedback` against any text read — at most three sentences, at most 80 words in English, letters of the language's script — and the leak check of a task none of whose leak phrases they hold. Of `tutor`'s checks before its call it keeps no text, no criterion or a blank one, no topic, no style, a tag of the feedback's language that does not parse, is the root, has no name in English or a first subtag `words.Feedback` cannot read, and a request cut short, each with no mark;
  - **the ports carry what only the stub reads:** the instance of the task, `ReadSheet(ctx, reading, task, own)` and the like, and `own`, the value of the request's own key, empty on platform AI. The phases `Call` of the requests pass `req.GetOwnKey().GetKey()`. The gate's adapter, `gateTutor`, drops both, so `tutor` still sees the open view alone and takes the key from `Route.Own`; the stub reads the key's ending and keeps nothing, and the key goes neither to the log nor to the database;
  - **the words** — three questions each in English and Russian, without a number, which pass the leak check and the form check; the first that `Asked` does not hold, so "explain differently" gets other words. Another language gets English, which the form check turns down into the fallback text;
  - **the words after a correct answer** — a praise — of a step of the work, or of the option chosen when no crop goes — and a question as the last sentence, in English and Russian — on the technique that helped, or, when the request names none, on how the answer was found —, which pass `leak` and `words.Correct`. They reach the learner on any day: the day from which the privacy policy lets them go to a provider is for the route to a provider, and `WithTutor` marks the service as on the stub;
  - **the endings of an own key** — past the checks of the request's form that `tutor` makes before its call, every call of the stub on an own key ends as a provider refuses it: `-refused` is `tutor.KeyRefused`, `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`; `-limit` is `LimitReached` with no reason, `own_key_limit` `LIMIT_CAUSE_UNSPECIFIED`; `-limit-owner`, `-limit-tier`, `-limit-balance` and `-limit-payment` are `LimitReached` with `owner_limit`, `tier`, `balance` and `billing`, `LIMIT_CAUSE_LIMIT`, `_TIER`, `_BALANCE` and `_PAYMENT`. The endings of a limit are tried the longest first, so none is taken for a shorter one it ends in. On `-limit-tier` the stub gives when access returns too, 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month by the wall clock, `time.Now` — the stub has no clock of the service, and the dev mode runs on the real one —, in `Outcome.AccessReturns` and in the `LimitError`, as the adapter of Claude gives the ceiling of the tier; the other endings of a limit give no time, as `tutor` gives none for the owner's limit, the balance and billing. Another key, and platform AI, read and word as above. The constants are `tutorstub.KeyRefused` and those beside it;
  - **the check of a key** — the stub's adapter answers `Check` by the ending of the key `Dial` got — `-invalid` the key refused, `-noperm` no rights, `-silent` no answer, any other key taken, the endings of a call too —, so that `CheckOwnKey` gives the contract's five ends through `Tutor.CheckKey` with no call. On the stub the checks of an own key have no limit — `serve` gives `account.WithoutKeyCheckLimit` beside `learning.WithTutor` —, since no key reaches a provider there and the runs of `app` in Playwright check the stub's endings again and again; on the models and on a deployment the limit stands;
  - **the spending** — the expected price of the kind of call on the route, `Tutor.Price`, which is the reserve of the quota, shared by the readers in whole micro-dollars; a kind of call with no expected price spends nothing priced, and the quota keeps its reserve by the value of the kind as spent. So the settlement spends the reserve to the micro-dollar, and the quota runs out after as many calls as on the models. A refused call has no tokens, so the stub gives no usage, as `tutor` gives none for a refusal without tokens; an own key takes no reserve, so nothing is settled, and "limit reached" on it never closes platform AI. The usage names the provider `anthropic` and the model `stub`. The flag "platform limit" the stub never sets: on platform AI it gives no "limit reached";
  - **the runs** — `just dev` (`scripts/dev.sh run`) unsets the dev file's key of platform AI and sets the switch; `just dev-ai` (`run-ai`) keeps the key and unsets the switch, for a task that checks the words of a model under the cap its text names. `just dev-check` starts on the key Anthropic refuses, with the switch unset, then runs a server of its own on the stub, with facts of the check's own — Anthropic's for an adult and for a child under 13 —, for what only the stub gives: the words after a correct answer, an own key's limit before the reading on platform AI, a child's workbook page by a parent's consent that `api consents confirm` confirms, and an essay on the skill only essays teach, ended by "Done" with its criteria. The dev file, `just dev-ai`, `api check` and the first server of `dev-check` keep the facts the author declared.

— *Why:*
- the executor of `app` walks the path with no paid call, and the stub has to give "correct", a wrong answer, an unreadable one and every refusal of an own key, or the hint ladder, the essay and the endings cannot be walked;
- behind the ports of `learning`, not as an adapter of `tutor`: an adapter sees the open view and the crop, never the key of the task, and could read no right value, while `tutor` would still admit, choose and price with it;
- the shape of a crop is what a scenario of Playwright can draw — a horizontal stroke, a vertical one, a dot — without recognizing handwriting;
- the expected price makes the quota run out on the stub as on the models;
- the admission as the gate's: a task is no more open on the stub than on the models, and a child's is open only by the facts of a child;
- the observation as `tutor` gives it, so that what `api` joins and judges on the stub is what it will on the models;
- the value of the own key as an argument of the port: the value of `Route.Own` is closed, `Provider()` alone outside `tutor`, and the gate's adapter shows in one place that `tutor` gets nothing new;
- `just dev` on the stub: a session cannot spend money by mistake, and a real call is the exception a task names.

— *Rejected:*
- the stub as `provider.Dial` alone: no right value, as above;
- the reading always right — no wrong answer, no ladder — or always illegible — neither; every essay read — `app` could not reach "unreadable"; every criterion met — `app` would show no criterion unmet;
- a stub that spends nothing — the quota could not be run out on it;
- a build tag that leaves the stub out of the release: the refusal at the start is the guarantee the emulator has, and lint and coverage would need a second build;
- `just dev` on the models with `just dev-stub` beside it, and `dev-check` on the stub alone: the check would no longer reach the adapter of Claude;
- the endings read by the stub's model in `internal/admission`, with the calls through the real `tutor`: `tutor`'s checks before the call and its failure for any other key would break the stub's path, which reads and words behind the ports; the value of the key in the request's `context` — implicit, and every port's caller would carry a key it does not see;
- a page or an essay admitted on the stub for every category and route — a child's would begin on the stub where a child's sheet does not; a crop box on each value of a page — `tutor`'s reading of a page gives none;
- a text in the module's language — the reading names none; a feedback that quotes the text — a quote is the one part of the rule that depends on the text, and the stub's text is fixed;
- the facts of a child in the dev file — they would hold for the first server of `dev-check` and for `just dev-ai` too, on a model.

— *Consequence:* `internal/tutorstub/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/config/`; `internal/account/`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `justfile`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R67. The local build of `app` signs in by a custom token of the test account `dev-app`, which `just dev-app` lays in the file `custom-token` of the Docker volume `novatrail-dev-token`, anew every half hour, while it keeps `api` up for `app` at `http://api:8080` on the stub of `tutor`, or with `API_DEV_APP_AI=1` on the models; `app` mounts the volume read-only. The account has an owner's profile and a synthetic child under 13. The recipe is over `scripts/dev.sh`, with no Go code, and the volume is a difference of the devcontainer of `api` from the container of `spec`.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **`just dev-app`** (`scripts/dev.sh app`) runs on the stub: the build, `migrate` and the country `US`, the packages of the pinned release laid afresh into a folder of its own run, as `dev-check` lays them, `api reopen`, `serve` on port 8080 on those packages with its log in the terminal, then an ID token of `dev-app` and the account by an invite code with an owner's profile of the category `adult`, by the same steps as `dev-check`, so that a repeat makes nothing. Then it lays the custom token and lays it anew every 1800 s until the server stops or Ctrl-C. On the way out it stops the server, deletes the file and a temporary one half laid — a token past its hour is not left behind —, and checks the server's log as `dev-check` does, for the custom token too, and for the code of the consent's request this run made: a log that fails fails the run. A run that stops before it lays its first token — a second one beside a run that goes on, whose server finds port 8080 taken — leaves the file where it is. It prints the path of the file and the host `api` with its port, never the token;
  - **the facts** — on the stub its server takes `child_facts` of `scripts/dev.sh`, the facts of the server of `dev-check` on the stub, in place of the dev file's: Anthropic's for an adult and for a child under 13. On the stub nothing reaches a provider, so they govern the stub's admission alone;
  - **on the models** (`app_mode`), with `API_DEV_APP_AI=1`: the dev file's key and facts, not the child's, for the author's check by hand of a task that checks a model, under the cap its text names; the quota of the account's tariff bounds what a day spends there;
  - **the account** — the UID `API_DEV_APP_UID`, by default `dev-app`, apart from `dev-check`'s `dev-author`: the runs of `app` spend the quota of their own profile, by the expected prices of the stub, and leave their attempts there;
  - **the child** — after the owner's profile, `app_child` takes the first profile of the account that is not the owner's, of the pseudonym `Dev child`, the category `under_13` and the consent's need `CONSENT_NEED_NONE`; with none, it takes back the account's consents with no profile, as `dev-check` does, so that those a run stopped half way leaves do not pile up to the most an account may have waiting, and `child_profile`, which `dev-check` shares, makes one: a consent for a child of 9 of the country `US` requested by `RequestChildConsent`, a synthetic scan sent by `SendConsentForm`, `api consents confirm` of the request's code, `CreateChildProfile`. Either way the run prints the profile ID, so a repeat makes no second child; one who has turned 13 or waits for a consent again is not taken, and a new one is made beside it;
  - **the forms** — its server, as both of `dev-check`, sends the scans of parents' forms to a folder of the run, `API_CONSENT_FORMS_DIR`, which goes with it: no synthetic scan stays in the folder of the dev file, and a scan not there is erased already for the deletion of a profile later (`internal/forms`);
  - **the file** — `/home/vscode/.novatrail-token/custom-token`, or the folder `API_DEV_TOKEN_DIR`: one line, a token with the claims of the custom token of `dev-check` and `uid` `dev-app`, an hour long, so the file always holds one with half an hour left. It is written to a temporary file of the same folder, mode `600`, and renamed over the old one, so `app` never reads half a token. A folder that is not there is refused with a word of Rebuild Container, not made: `app` would not see it;
  - **the volume** — `novatrail-dev-token` at `/home/vscode/.novatrail-token`, which the Dockerfile makes as `vscode` with the mode `700`, as it does `.novatrail-dev`. A volume can be mounted read-write in one container and read-only in another (sources 2.7). The container of `app` is `vscode` on the same base image, `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base:3.0.12-noble` (`.devcontainer/Dockerfile` of `app`, `main` d5ceae6), so the mode lets it read. Docker copies the folder of the image into a new volume, but whether its owner passes too, the page does not say (sources 2.7); in this container the volume `novatrail-api-dev` belongs to `vscode` with the mode `700`, as its folder in the image. So the image of `app` makes the folder as `vscode` too, whichever container makes the volume first;
  - **the address** — `app` reaches the server at `http://api:8080`, by the alias `api` of the devcontainer in the network `novatrail-api`;
  - **the functions** `custom_token` and `id_token` take the UID; `exchange` swaps a custom token on stdin for an ID token; the steps of the legal texts, of the account, of the migrations, of the packages, of `reopen` and of the start of the server are functions that `check` and `app` share;
  - **the release** has no tool: the image takes from the build only `/api` and the folder of the packages, and the binary has no new code.

— *Why:*
- the executor of `app` cannot run a recipe in the container of `api`, and a custom token lasts an hour at most (sources 2.6): a fresh one has to lie where `app` takes it alone;
- a file in a volume is how the devcontainers of NovaTrail share their sign-ins already, `novatrail-claude` and `novatrail-gh`, and needs no port and no new language;
- the server and the token in one recipe: `app` needs both, and the author starts one thing;
- an account of its own keeps the quota of `dev-check` from running out by the runs of `app`;
- the child and its facts: the workbook pages of the pinned release are for a child under 13 alone, and `app` walks a child's page on the stub.

— *Rejected:*
- a script of Node on the network `novatrail-api` that issues a token per request: no devcontainer changes, but a new language in `scripts/` and a port that signs in any container of the network;
- a one-off `just dev-token`: the author would lay the token before each run, and the executor of `app` could not check without the author;
- the volume `novatrail-api-dev` read-only in `app`: it holds the key of the service account;
- `dev-author`: the runs of `app` would spend its daily quota, and the step of the quota of `dev-check` would hit the cap; a UID with no account and an invite code each run: new code for each run, and the account deleted after;
- a Go program or a route of the binary in the dev mode: a second adapter of the sign-in service, and a tool in the release;
- a child made on each run and deleted on the way out — the attempts of `app` on it would go with it, and a run killed from outside would leave one; the facts of a child in the dev file — they would hold for `just dev-ai` on a model too; the child in the account of `dev-check` — the runs of `app` would spend its quota.

— *Consequence:* `scripts/dev.sh`; `justfile`; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.16.7, and `tutor` by its kinds of call

**R68. The admission gate asks `tutor` by the kind of call: "explain differently" and the words of a task a tap checks by `ChooseCall(CallHint)`, the words with the work by `ChooseCall(CallWords)`, which admits only with the hint on the analysis's first model, and the words after a correct answer by `ChooseCall(CallCorrect)`. The quota reserves by the same kind, `CallHint` by a value of its own in `internal/quota`. The line `ai_usage` carries each model's own time, its retries and when its first text came.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the gate** — `Gate.Words` asks `ChooseCall(admission.WordsCall(withWork))` — `CallWords` with the work, `CallHint` without it — and admits when the entry has a model. It compares no models itself: `tutor` does, by the choice the call makes;
  - **the quota** — the words of a rung and "explain differently" reserve by the kind of the request they send to `tutor`, as `tutor` picks it: `CallWords` when the crop goes — the words of a handwriting sheet —, `CallHint` without it — a task a tap checks and "explain differently". The kind comes of the request, not of the attempt read before the transaction, so the reserve is that of the call that goes. `tutor` gives `CallHint` no expected price, so its reserve is the value of the kind in `internal/quota`, the words': the same prompt and model without the picture costs no more. `CallCorrect` has expected tokens, so it reserves by `Price(CallCorrect)`, with no value of its own. A call that comes back with its usage settles at what it cost;
  - **the log** — the line `ai_usage` carries per model `model_ms` (`Usage.Latency`, the SDK's retries counted in), `retries` and `first_token_ms`, beside the call's `duration_ms`. They are durations and a count — no identifier, no work, nothing read and no words —, which the platform's rule of logs lets in; of a pair they say which reader held the call.

— *Why:*
- the gate by the kind of call: the gate and the call make one choice, so an entry of `hint` on another prompt can no longer admit an "explain differently" that the call then refuses;
- a value of its own for `CallHint`, rather than the price of the words: that is the price of a call with a picture, and the values of the configuration stay in its table;
- the times in the log: the time of the whole call does not say which reader of a pair was slow or whether the SDK sent it again, and `tutor`'s eval runner measures latency by the same span.

— *Rejected:*
- reserving `CallHint` by its worst price: a profile's cap would close at the first "explain differently";
- reserving it by `Price(CallWords)`: one kind of call priced as another, which `tutor` tells apart;
- the kind of the reserve by `readsAI`, the read of the attempt's task before the transaction, which the gate asks by: a read that fails gives "no work", and the reserve would be the hint's while the crop goes.

— *Consequence:* `internal/admission/`; `internal/quota/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/logging/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.17.1, the legal texts by the day and the export of version 2

**R69. The legal texts are read on the day of the request in UTC: the current version of each and its next one, `next_texts`, with `Cache-Control` of an hour and no longer than until 00:00 UTC of the nearest day of a next version, and a request that names a next version before its day is `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT`. A copy's folder is read by the rules of the part `legal/` with `legal.operator`, whose refusal stops the start. The devcontainer gives the container its name in its network, `--network-alias=api`.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the day** — the clock of the service that asks: `legal.Service` has `WithClock`, as `account.Service` has, and both are `time.Now` in `serve`, which sets neither; `Spec()` reads the tag on the day of its first call, and `cmd/api` reads a copy's folder on the day it starts. `Texts.On(at)` gives a view of the day of `at` in UTC — `Current`, `Names`, `NamesOne`, `Refs`, `List`, `ListNext` and `KeptFor` —, so the checks and the answers of one request take the moment of its service. `Texts` keeps every version in force of each text, so a request past midnight is checked against the texts of its own day with no restart. The pure `Versions(passport, today)` gives the current and next versions, and the test of the vectors calls it rather than a copy of the rule;
  - **the lifetime** — `KeptFor`: an hour, or less, until 00:00 UTC of the nearest day of a next text of the place `app`, never below zero, in whole seconds rounded down in `max-age`. With no next version it is an hour, and no other rule is needed on the day before a change: a version becomes current at midnight only as a next version that the answer already gives;
  - **the folder** — `Load(at)` and `LoadDir(dir, at)` read the part on the day `api` starts, by the rules in the order `spec/legal/README.md` gives them: files, versions, set-texts, markdown, `legal.operator`, in-force-unchanged. `legal.operator` reads the heading on `goldmark`'s tree, as `legal.markdown` does: a heading of level 2 whose children are all text and together read `Who we are` — GFM splits the text of a heading at the places where an autolink could start, so its pieces are joined, and their character references and backslash escapes are resolved by `goldmark`'s writer of HTML, as micromark resolves them in `spec`'s reader —, so `## Who&#32;we are` is accepted there and here, `## Who&nbsp;we are` refused. Its refusal names the file or `texts.privacy-policy`, as the vectors do, and `serve` and `api check` fail on it, as on any other rule;
  - **the repeat** — `RequestChildConsent` and `RestateAge` check the version of `parent-consent` inside the action under the idempotency key, at its moment, not before the key. `rpc.Once` keeps no refusal of the action that is not `Kept`, so a repeat under the same key after 00:00 UTC of the day of a new version gets its first answer, not `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT`, and the check and the record take one moment;
  - **the export** — an attempt's `correct_answer_words_given` is read from the column `attempts.correct_words_given`, true once the words after a correct answer were given;
  - **the alias** — `"--network-alias=api"` after `"--network=novatrail-api"`, with the template's comment; [build.md](build.md) names `http://api:8080`, and the line of the run of `scripts/dev.sh` the host `api` and its port, with no scheme: SonarCloud reads a URL of `http` to a host that is not loopback as clear text (S5332).

— *Why:*
- the day from the service's clock: the account's handlers have one already, and the tests run in parallel, which a clock of the package would not allow;
- all the versions in force kept in `Texts`: the day changes while `api` runs, and an image read once a day would serve a new version a day late or ask a restart at midnight;
- the check after the key: a repeat is the same request, and `rpc.Once` gives a repeat the first answer only of an action that ran;
- refusing the start on `legal.operator`: the text of the part asks it of every reader, and a copy with no `Who we are` tells its users no operator.

— *Rejected:*
- a clock of the package `legal`: a state of the whole package that parallel tests share;
- serving a copy with no current policy, rather than a failed start, when its folder breaks `legal.operator`: the text of the part does not allow it;
- the texts checked before the key: a repeat past midnight would get a refusal that its first answer did not.

— *Consequence:* `internal/legal/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/export/`; `cmd/api/`; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Stored works in code

**R70. `api` keeps the stored works of `WorkService` in its own database at Neon: the table `works`, one work by attempt, the last one put, as the contract's `StoredWork` in binary protobuf built anew of the fields the contract names, beside its kind by the attempt's task, its size and when it was kept. A work put holds the profile's row `FOR SHARE`, and switching the storage off erases the works in its transaction after it updates that row; the deletion of a profile or of the account takes them by the cascade. The export names them newest first, without their bytes, and `app` reads each by `GetWork`. `api` has no limit of its own on the number or the sum of a profile's works.**
- **What the contract asks:** `PutWork`, a learning request, while the storage is on, refused `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF` and, over 4,194,304 bytes of the history, the contexts and the picture, `CAUSE_WORK_TOO_LARGE`; a form refused `invalid_argument` with no `Refusal`; the kind and when it was kept set by `api`; `ListWorks` and `GetWork` by the learner or by an adult with `can_manage`; `DeleteWork` by whoever decides the storage, as `SetWorkStorage`; no idempotency key. The text `storage-consent` promises "our database at Neon", that "Nobody reads the works we keep", that the server "never sends them to an AI provider", and that switching off "erases the copies on our server at once, and the copies for recovery within a day".
- **What was decided:**
  - **where** — the database of `api`. The copies for recovery are Neon's history, whose window is 1 day by default on the Launch plan (sources 3.1). Switching off erases the works in its own transaction, the deletion of a profile or of the account by `ON DELETE CASCADE` of both references, and `api sweep` needs no step;
  - **the table** — migration `0017`: `works (profile_id, attempt_id, kind, size_bytes, work, stored_at)`, the key `(profile_id, attempt_id)`, the references to `profiles` and to `attempts (profile_id, id)`, `kind` one of `handwriting`, `essay` and `drawing`, `size_bytes` from 0 to 4,194,304, and the index `(profile_id, stored_at DESC, attempt_id)` of the list. A repeat of `PutWork` is an upsert, and `stored_at` is the time of the last one;
  - **the bytes** — a `StoredWork` of `attempt_id`, `history`, each submission's `context` and `history_length`, and `picture`, with no `kind` and no `stored_at`, which come of the columns. Connect's binary codec keeps the fields it does not know — `connectproto.NewBinaryCodec` without `DiscardUnknown` —: the message as it came would keep them, and `GetWork` would give them back past the count of 4 MiB. A `kind` or `stored_at` that `app` sends is not taken, so `app` may put back a work as `GetWork` gave it;
  - **the race with switching off** — `PutWork` runs under `store.WriteUnderAccount` and holds the profile's row `FOR SHARE`, which conflicts with the `UPDATE profiles` of `SwitchWorkStorageOff`; `FOR KEY SHARE` would not, since that update takes `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. So switching off updates the consent's record, then the profile, then erases the works, and a comment says why: erased first, a work put between the erasure and the update would stay after the switch, or the two would deadlock on its row. The locks go in the order of the deletion: the account, then the profile. The work is encoded before the transaction;
  - **the order of the locks of a profile** — `SetWorkStorage`, `RestateAge`, `GiveOwnConsent` and `WithdrawOwnConsent` hold the profile's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` before its consent, in the order of the deletion of a profile, which holds the profile and reaches the consent by the cascade, so they wait for it rather than deadlock;
  - **`PutWork`**, in this order: the consent and the age, by `rpc.Learner`; an attempt ID that is not canonical, empty too, `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`, as `hold()` reads one; the size, `CAUSE_WORK_TOO_LARGE`; the form — the history a JSON array and each context a JSON object, in UTF-8, and the picture with PNG's signature, as the contract names it, and each `history_length` not below zero, which the schema of the export asks, `minimum: 0` —, or `invalid_argument` naming the field; an attempt not the profile's, `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`; the kind; and in the transaction the storage off, `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF`. A message over 11 MiB is `resource_exhausted` with no `Refusal` before it is read;
  - **the kind** — by the task of the attempt's package in `set.Packages`, whatever its status, withdrawn too: an answer slot with a rubric makes an essay, one checked by a key or a predicate a handwriting, and none a drawing. A digest the index does not know is a deployment going on, `unavailable` with `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN`, as at every request on an attempt, whatever work was kept. A package out of the window, or a task its package does not hold, leaves the kind of the work kept of the attempt before, and with none `PutWork` is `failed_precondition` with `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN`;
  - **`ListWorks` and `GetWork`** — `ACTION_LEARNING` checked for the profile of the account alone, not for its consent or its age, `ACTION_ADULT` by `can_manage`, `permission_denied` with no `Refusal` without it, as `work.proto` says and `GetQuota` does; any other action `invalid_argument`. The list is without the bytes; `GetWork` of an attempt with no work kept, or not the profile's, is `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`;
  - **`DeleteWork`** — the right first, as for `ListWorks`: the adult's `can_manage`, the learner's request for the profile of the account alone. Then who decides, `store.WorkDecider`, read with no lock: the consent held `FOR UPDATE` would wait in the other order than the deletion of a profile. The other kind of action is `CAUSE_LEARNER_DECIDES` or `CAUSE_ADULT_NEEDED`, an attempt not the profile's `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`, and no work kept is "erased" all the same;
  - **the code** — in `internal/learning`, the package that holds the set of packages and `rubric(task)`, with `(*Service).Works(db, rights)`; `serve` wires it as `rpc.Handlers.Work`;
  - **the export** — `store.ExportProfile` reads the works in the export's snapshot, newest first, and the profile's file names each — the attempt, the kind, when kept and the size, read from `size_bytes`, never from `work` —; `app` reads each by `GetWork` into a file of its own beside `api.zip`. The form `PutWork` checks is what lets `app` write a work a device sent; `FuzzWorkExported` of `internal/learning` holds it, on the file `exportv4.Work` that `app` writes;
  - **no limit of its own** — one work by attempt and 4 MiB a work bound it, and the export carries no work's bytes, so no answer grows with them;
  - **what is not stored** — an essay is stored as a stored work, its strokes and its picture, by the choice of whoever consents, and the server holds neither what was read nor the words of the feedback anywhere.

— *Why:*
- the database: the text in force names it, and its erasure and deletion are those of every other record of the profile, in one transaction, with the history window the text promises;
- the profile's row as the lock: the switch changes it, so one lock orders a work put and the switch with no table or advisory lock of its own;
- the message built anew: what is kept and counted is what the contract names, and nothing more reaches `GetWork` or the export;
- the form checked at `PutWork`: a work the export cannot write as JSON would break the export of the whole profile, later, for a request that is not the device's;
- the order of the checks: a refusal by the work itself comes before anything is read of the attempt, and the transaction holds only what needs the lock;
- the learner's requests on the works without the consent and the age: `app` builds the export by them, and the learner's `ExportProfile` goes while the profile waits.

— *Rejected:*
- a bucket of Cloud Storage: the erasure would go outside the transaction, and the text in force names the database;
- several works by attempt, or the versions of one: the contract says the last one put stands;
- the history and the contexts in columns of `jsonb`: `jsonb` reorders the keys and drops the duplicates, and the export would no longer give them as the canvas gave them;
- the message of the request kept as it came: the unknown fields above;
- `FOR KEY SHARE`, or the erasure before the profile's update: the race above;
- the consent held in `DeleteWork`: the deadlock above;
- a limit of `api`'s own on the sum: the platform rejected one, and the works leave the export's one answer;
- the kind sent by `app`: the contract sets it by the task.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/`; `internal/account/`; `internal/export/`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The board module

**R71. The release of `modules` that `modules.json` pins carries the module of `tutor`'s board, `novatrail/check-board`, and the test of the job "Modules" walks the owner's path on it, in the dev mode. Its tasks are all read by AI, so at the stage of admission `full` `api` refuses them "not admitted", until a model of `tutor`'s admission table passes that stage; at the stage `author` they begin for an adult, on platform AI once the deployment's facts and key open it, and on Anthropic's own key at once.**
- **What the module is** — "Задачи на листе", mathematics, for adults, explanations in `ru`: the eleven tasks of `internal/board/tasks.go` of `tutor`, `equation-1` to `album`, each on `handwriting_sheet`, one item and one answer slot bound `anywhere`, its check `number`, with a solution, traps and leak phrases, with no leading question or worked example; one lesson, `board`, of the eleven in the board's order; four skills, no `teaches`. Its device part is `LICENSE` alone, the same file as the other packages'.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the release test** — `TestThePinnedReleaseIsLearnedOn` runs the subtest "the board module", `boardIsLearnedOn`, on the gate and the stub of `tutor` as `just dev` builds them: every task and the lesson of the module reach the engine for an adult on platform AI, `ListCatalog` gives its current version, `ConnectModule`, `OpenLesson` of `board` and its first task segment begins; a tall crop is "not yet" with one wrong answer, `GetRungWords` on the work gives the stub's words, a wide crop closes the attempt "correct", and `GetProgress` writes its evidence;
  - **where `api` answers "not admitted"** —
    - at the stage `full` `BeginAttempt` is `failed_precondition` with `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`, by platform AI and by Anthropic's own key alike; the engine gets none of its tasks or lessons, and with it alone connected `ChooseNext` gives "nothing" with "not admitted". `GetCorrectAnswerWords` is the outcome "not admitted" too. A deployment runs at the stage its operator names, `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`, and at `full` when it names none;
    - a child does not see the module at all: its audience is adults, so it is not in the child's catalog, and that is no refusal of admission;
    - locally — `just dev`, `just dev-ai` and `just dev-app`, at the stage `author` with the facts for an adult — every task begins;
  - **the packages laid** — after `modules.json` changes, `just packages` lays the packages into an empty folder only, so the old folder is removed first; `just dev-app` and `just dev-check` lay their own.

— *Why:*
- the subtest on the stub: the stub reads by the key, with no provider, through `tutor`'s own admission and prices, the way the author's `just dev` runs; the board's tasks have no tap to check them by;
- a subtest of the same test: `ci-modules` runs that one test by name and looks for the line of its pass, which a failed subtest withholds.

— *Rejected:*
- a reader of the test, as `WithReader`, in place of the stub: it would hold neither `tutor`'s admission nor the dev mode the author runs;
- a test that `full` refuses the module: it would fail on the good news that a model passed the stage, and the test of the gate on the vectors, `TestATaskOfHandwritingAsksTheGate`, holds the rule.

— *Consequence:* `modules.json`; `internal/learning/release_test.go`, `stub_test.go`, `learning_test.go`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The providers of the own key

**R72. `ListOwnKeyProviders` names the providers of the admission table, in its order, whose own key `tutor` admits at the profile's category for one kind of call `api` makes at least, by the code and the name the table writes. A profile not of the category `adult` gets none, with no refusal. The gate's `OwnKeyOpen` answers whether a provider is open, one function for the list and for the check of a key, so that a provider out of the list is "provider closed" with no call. The list follows the stage the deployment names: on the release of `tutor` that `go.mod` pins, it is Anthropic at the stage `author` and nobody at `full`, as the tests of the gate hold.**
- **What was decided:**
