  - **the function** — `admission.Gate.OwnKeyOpen(category, provider)`: `ChooseCall` of each kind of call `api` makes, `ownKeyCalls` — `CallSheet`, `CallHint`, `CallWords`, `CallCorrect`, `CallWorkbook`, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric` —, on the route `own_key` of the provider, with a stand-in for the key, since the choice reads the provider alone. It is open when one kind gets an entry with models. A provider `api` gives no adapter of, one the table does not know, and none are closed;
  - **the list** — `Gate.OwnKeyProviders(category)`: the providers of `admission-table.json` of `spec.Admission`, read in the order of its JSON when the gate is loaded, since `tutor`'s table keeps them in a map and gives none out, each kept when `OwnKeyOpen` holds;
  - **the handler** — `internal/account/ownkey.go`, after the checks of `toManage`: another account's profile and one that is not there are "no profile" by the checks, and one gone since, by the handler, which reads the category alone by `store.LearnerOf`, in one query: the request is an adult's, and no consent of the profile's refuses it — the contract names `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE` alone. A category not `adult` gets the empty list before the gate is asked: the table opens no own key below 18, and the contract gives none whatever it says. With no gate, `account.WithOwnKeys`, the method is `unimplemented`; `serve` gives it the gate it loads, the dev mode's too, whose stub gives Anthropic's adapter, so the list is the same there;
  - **no call** — the list calls no provider, so the limit of the checks of a key does not count it.

— *Why:*
- the list and the check do not part: `app` offers no provider whose key would always get `PROVIDER_CLOSED`, and at `full` it offers none, though the table's cells leave Anthropic and OpenAI open to an adult;
- the kinds of call `api` makes, not every kind of `tutor`'s: a provider open only to a kind `api` does not call would be a key with nothing to do here;
- the order of the JSON, not of the codes: the contract names the table's order.

— *Rejected:*
- the list by the cell `own_key` of the table alone — then OpenAI is always in it, and on a deployment both providers are listed and both closed;
- every kind of call of `tutor`, as `CheckKey` asks: the list would name a provider for calls `api` does not make.

— *Consequence:* `internal/admission/admission.go`; `internal/account/ownkey.go`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R73. `CheckOwnKey` checks the owner's key by `Tutor.CheckKey`, through the gate's `CheckKey`, and gives the contract's five ends: `ACCEPTED`, `INVALID`, `NO_PERMISSION`, `PROVIDER_CLOSED` and `UNSPECIFIED` with the outcome `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`. A provider `OwnKeyOpen` closes is `PROVIDER_CLOSED` with no call. Each check that reaches the gate counts against the account's limit of the checks of a key, which the stub of the dev mode lifts. The key lives in the request's memory alone, and the line `request` names the end by the field `key_check`. The stub answers the check by the ending of a synthetic key.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the handler** — `internal/account/ownkey.go`, after the checks of `toManage`. The profile's category is read by `store.LearnerOf`, as for the list of providers, and a profile gone since is `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE`. Then come two refusals, with no call. An `own_key` that is not there, or has no provider or no key, is `invalid_argument` with no `Refusal` at all, by `rpc.Malformed("own_key")`, as `ownKeySent` of the requests of an attempt: the key is malformed before it is anybody's. A category not `adult` is `CAUSE_KEY_NOT_FOR_PROFILE`, `invalid_argument`;
  - **the limit** — after the refusals and before the gate, `countKeyCheck` counts the check on the account: a check beyond the limit is `resource_exhausted` with `CAUSE_KEY_CHECKS_EXHAUSTED` and `next_key_check`, with no call, and a provider closed counts as a check. The dev mode's stub has none, `account.WithoutKeyCheckLimit`;
  - **the check** — `admission.Gate.CheckKey(ctx, category, provider, key)`: `OwnKeyOpen` first, and a provider it closes is `KeyProviderClosed` with no adapter dialled. Otherwise it is `Tutor.CheckKey` on the route `own_key` with `tutor.NewOwnKey`. `tutor` checks every model it would call with the key, by `count_tokens` at Anthropic, which is free (sources 10.7), and takes the worst end: wrong over no rights over no answer. A limit at the check is "accepted";
  - **the ends** — `KeyAccepted` → `ACCEPTED`, `KeyWrong` → `INVALID`, `KeyNoRights` → `NO_PERMISSION`, `KeyProviderClosed` → `PROVIDER_CLOSED`; `KeyUnanswered` and any other end → `UNSPECIFIED` with `outcome` `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`, an answer, not a refusal;
  - **the time** — the check ends a second before the request's deadline, `checkReserve`, so that a provider slower than the request's deadline, which `internal/config/config.go` sets, is the outcome "no answer", not `deadline_exceeded`. With less than two seconds left — a client's short `Connect-Timeout-Ms` — the check does not begin, and the request is `deadline_exceeded`, as the reading of an attempt is (`admission.ErrTooLittleTime`): the client's deadline is no provider's silence;
  - **the log** — the line `request` carries `key_check`, a word of `logging.KeyChecks`: `accepted`, `invalid`, `no_permission`, `provider_closed` or `unanswered`. With "no answer" it also carries `outcome_cause` `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`. Neither the key nor its provider goes there: the provider is a field of `ai_usage` alone, and a check is no call of a model with tokens, so it writes no `ai_usage`. The error of `CheckKey` names the models and how they failed, never the key; the answer says all that the operator needs, and the log keeps the end alone;
  - **the stub** — `stubModel.Check` of `internal/admission/admission.go` answers by the ending of the key that `Dial` got: `-invalid` → `provider.ErrKeyRefused`, `-noperm` → `provider.ErrNoRights`, `-silent` → an error of neither kind; any other key is taken. So all five ends go through `Tutor.CheckKey` itself, with no call, and a provider closed to the key — OpenAI — is `PROVIDER_CLOSED` on the stub too. The check takes a key that ends in `-refused`, `-limit`, `-limit-owner`, `-limit-tier`, `-limit-balance` or `-limit-payment`, by which the stub then refuses the calls of AI on that own key — the key refused, or the limit with its cause —, so `app` connects such a key as any other and meets the refusal at the call, as on the models a key that was taken and then ran out. `docs/build.md` names the endings for `app`.

— *Why:*
- the platform has the key checked when it is connected, through `tutor`, and `api` calls no provider round it;
- `OwnKeyOpen` before `CheckKey`, though `tutor` closes the same provider itself: one function for the list and for the check, so that a provider out of the list is never a key `api` dials;
- the malformed key before the category: the requests of an attempt refuse so (`ownKeySent`), and `app` meets one order;
- no `Refusal` on a malformed key: a `Refusal` of `CAUSE_UNSPECIFIED` is a cause the contract never sends, and `app` reads the cause before the code;
- `key_check` and not the error's kind: the end is a word of a closed set, which the operator counts, and the line of a check that went wrong is no failure of `api`, so it is no error either;
- the stub by the ending: `app`'s scenario types a synthetic key and sees each end in Playwright, with no call. The stub's model keeps the end its key gave at `Dial`, never the key;
- `Tutor.CheckKey`, which checks the models of every kind of call `tutor` would make with the key, while `OwnKeyOpen` asks the kinds `api` makes: `tutor` alone knows the models it would call, and a key without rights to a model of a kind `api` does not make would be `NO_PERMISSION` all the same.

— *Rejected:*
- the provider in the line `request`: the table of the log gives it to `ai_usage` alone, and `key_check` says enough;
- a line `ai_usage` for each model checked: a check has no tokens and no price, and it would fake a call;
- the stub always "accepted" — `app` would not see the four other ends;
- a deadline of the check's own shorter than the request's: a check on the models takes under a second (measurements 6), and the request's deadline is the bound `app` already waits by;
- a refusal of a profile that is not the owner's by a flag of its own: the category is enough, since only the owner's profile is `adult` — a child's age is 0–17 at its consent and at `RestateAge` —, as `ownKeyFits` of the requests of an attempt has it;
- the kind of the error of a check that got no answer in the line `request`: it would make the line of a provider's silence an error of `api`'s, while a call of a model that failed is no error either (`ai_usage`, `failed`);
- refusing the stub's keys of a refusal or a limit at the check: `app` could never reach the call's refusal on the stub.

— *Consequence:* `internal/admission/admission.go`; `internal/account/ownkey.go`; `internal/logging/events.go`; `internal/rpc/refusal.go`; `internal/rpc/rpc.go`; `internal/rpc/checks.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The order of a profile's locks

**R74. A request that holds a profile's consent holds the profile's row first, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, by `store.HoldProfile`, and its consent after it: `SetWorkStorage`, `RestateAge`, `GiveOwnConsent` and `WithdrawOwnConsent`, all by `consentOf` of `internal/account`. That is the order of the deletion of a profile — the profile's row `FOR UPDATE`, then the consent by the cascade —, so the two wait for each other rather than deadlock, and the request that comes after the deletion answers `CAUSE_NO_PROFILE`.**
- **The deadlock it prevents:** `SetWorkStorage` that held the consent `FOR UPDATE` and then updated the profile — `SwitchWorkStorageOn` and `SwitchWorkStorageOff` —, and `RestateAge` that held it and then updated the profile's category and country, while the deletion of a profile, `deletion.Profile` and `IdleProfile`, holds the profile `FOR UPDATE` and reaches the consent by the `DELETE` of its cascade. Two such transactions on one profile would take the two rows in opposite orders; Postgres breaks the one that waited first, and it is `unavailable`.
- **The lock** — `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the one an `UPDATE` of the profile's row takes anyway: it waits for the deletion's `FOR UPDATE` and for `PutWork`'s `FOR SHARE`, as the update after it did, and lets a row that references the profile — an attempt, a key record — be written meanwhile. No other request holds a profile's consent and then changes the profile: `ConsentFor` of `CreateChildProfile` holds a child's consent that has no profile yet, and `SendConsentForm` may hold one a profile waits for — the parent's consent `RestateAge` asks —, but it writes the consent and its form alone, so the deletion only waits for it; `WorkDecider` reads the consent without a lock, and the deletion of the account holds the account's row `FOR UPDATE` before any of them. A change that makes `SendConsentForm` write the profile holds the profile first too.

— *Why:*
- the profile before the consent, rather than the deletion's order turned: the deletion holds the profile to check it is the account's and to lock out every write of it, and its cascade order is Postgres's;
- one place, `consentOf`: every request that holds a profile's consent goes through it, so the order cannot part between them.

— *Rejected:*
- the consent read without a lock, as `WorkDecider` reads it: `RestateAge` replaces the consent by a new one and `GiveOwnConsent` changes its state, and two such requests on one consent would then both write by what they read;
- `FOR UPDATE` of the profile, as the deletion takes it: it would also make every attempt, run and key record written on the profile meanwhile wait, by their foreign keys, for no gain;
- `FOR KEY SHARE` for `GiveOwnConsent` and `WithdrawOwnConsent`, which do not update the profile, so that they would not wait for a `PutWork`'s `FOR SHARE`: one lock of one function keeps one order for all four, the wait is that of one insert, and a `FOR KEY SHARE` that `SetWorkStorage` then raises by its `UPDATE` would be an upgrade under the deletion's waiting `FOR UPDATE`;
- one helper for every hold of a profile's row — `HoldProfile`, `ProfileFor`, `ProfileIdle`, `PutWork`'s, `HoldSkillState`'s —, with the lock as a parameter: they differ in what they read and check, and the order is held by the callers this entry names;
- the order left as it falls, with `app` repeating `unavailable`: the user sees a refusal for nothing, and an `IdleProfile` of the daily job meets a learner's request too.

— *Consequence:* `internal/store/profiles.go`; `internal/account/consents.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The `spec` tag: v0.17.9 and sign-in through Google alone

**R75. `api` moves to `spec` v0.17.9: the submodule, and the Go modules `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` and `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts` in `go.mod`, at the tag. The sign-in is through Google alone, and sign-in through Apple is `spec`'s open question Т-41: Д3 waits for `spec`'s answer to it, not for the first deployment.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the tag** — the submodule and the two Go modules at one tag; the move moves no other module;
  - **Apple** — `api`'s documents name no sign-in through Apple by the first deployment: they name Т-41, and so does Д3, whose return is `spec`'s answer to Т-41;
  - **a phone** — the first slice serves a phone too, which changes nothing of `api`: it serves `app` by the same contract, whatever its screen.

— *Why:*
- Apple by Т-41: `spec` decided the first slice signs in through Google alone and keeps Apple as its own question, and a session of `api` that read "by the first deployment" would wait for a provider no epic brings.

— *Rejected:*
- the sign-in through Apple kept by the first deployment until Т-41 is answered: the log would promise what `spec` has put off.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/sources.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

## `tutor` v0.1.35: the words after a correct answer in the gate

**R76. `api` moves to `tutor` v0.1.35, and its admission gate knows the kind of call `CallCorrect` — the words after a correct answer — as it knows the words: `Gate.CorrectWords` asks `ChooseCall(CallCorrect)` on the route, and `Gate.TellCorrectWords` calls `Tutor.CorrectAnswerWords`. `CallCorrect` is one of the kinds of call `api` makes for the own key, and the quota reserves it by `Price(CallCorrect)`, with no value of its own in `internal/quota`. The stub of the dev mode words a praise of its own with the question last, in English and Russian. The port of `internal/learning` is `correctTeller`, beside `wordsTeller`, which `GetCorrectAnswerWords` calls.**
- **What `tutor` gives for these words:** `Tutor.CorrectAnswerWords` with `CorrectRequest` — the route, the task's open view, the crop of the work or none (none when the task's answer slots are bound `choice` alone), the key and the solution, the techniques, which may be none, the language of explanations and the style — and `CorrectAnswer`, the words and the outcome. It is one kind of call with the work and without it, `CallCorrect`: the capability `correct_answer_words`, one model, with expected tokens. `tutor` checks neither the leak nor the form: `api` does, by `leak` and `words.Correct` — exactly one question and it the last sentence (the breach `Last`), at most two sentences, at most `HintWords` words, the letters of the language —, and gives no words when they fail, as there is no fallback text of them. No task, no style or a crop that is no PNG fail before any call.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the gate** — `Gate.CorrectWords(category, ownKeyProvider)` is `ChooseCall(CallCorrect)` on the route `Handwriting` takes, admitted when the entry has models, as `Gate.Words`; `Gate.TellCorrectWords(ctx, request)` calls `Tutor.CorrectAnswerWords`, as `TellWords` calls `Words`;
  - **the own key** — `CallCorrect` joins `ownKeyCalls`, since the list and the check of an own key count the kinds of call `api` makes. The words after a correct answer are on the model of the words, so they open no provider that the reading, the words and the hint leave closed, and add no model to the check of a key: the tests of the gate hold both;
  - **the quota** — `quota.Reserve` reserves the expected price of `CallCorrect` on its route, and `internal/quota` has no value of the kind: it keeps one only where `tutor` gives no expected price, as for `CallHint`;
  - **the stub** — `TellCorrectWords` gives a praise and then a question, its last sentence, in English and Russian: the praise of a step of the work when the crop goes, and of the option chosen when it does not; the question on the technique that helped when the request names techniques, and on how the answer was found when it names none, as the prompt of `tutor` asks. The words hold no number and no word that tells the learner's sex, and another language gets English, which `words.Correct` of that language takes when its script is Latin. Of the checks `tutor` makes before its call the stub keeps those of the request's form: no task, no style, no language or a crop that is no PNG fails with no usage; the words of the task and the size of the crop it does not read. Like `TellWords`, it asks no admission: the handler asks the gate's `CorrectWords` before the call. The call spends the expected price of `CallCorrect` on its route, so the quota runs out on the stub as on the models; its `CorrectWords` is the gate's;
  - **the port** — `correctTeller` in `internal/learning/hints.go`, beside `wordsTeller`: `CorrectWords` and `TellCorrectWords`. `Service` holds it as `praiser`, the gate by default, and `WithTutor` gives the stub's; `GetCorrectAnswerWords` calls it by `rpc.Across`.

— *Why:*
- `CallCorrect` in `ownKeyCalls`: the list and the check are of the kinds of call `api` makes, and it changes no answer of them, which a test holds;
- the reserve by `Price`: `tutor` measured the tokens on the calls that admitted the words, and a value of `api`'s own would be a guess.

— *Rejected:*
- a value of `CallCorrect` in `internal/quota`, as the words': `tutor` gives the expected price, and two values of one kind would part;
- the admission of these words by `Gate.Words(false, …)`, the hint's kind: `tutor` admits them by an entry and a prompt of their own, and the hint's entry would admit words the call then refuses;
- the stub's praise with no question when the task names no technique: `words.Correct` asks exactly one, and `tutor`'s prompt asks how the answer was found then.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `internal/admission/admission.go`; `internal/learning/hints.go`; `internal/learning/learning.go`; `internal/tutorstub/tutorstub.go`; `internal/quota/quota.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The words after a correct answer in code

**R77. `GetCorrectAnswerWords` gives the tutor's words after a correct answer — the model's praise and its question on the technique that helped — through `correctTeller`, by `rpc.Across` as `GetRungWords`: the attempt a correct answer closed, held, the family day and the reserve of `CallCorrect` in the first transaction, the call outside any, and the answer on the attempt held again. The words pass `leak` and `words.Correct`, by the check `turnedDown` the ladder shares; words that fail, or a call with none, give no words and leave the right. Words given are recorded on the attempt, `attempts.correct_words_given`, so a second request is "no more words", and the export carries it as `correct_answer_words_given`. On the route to a provider the words go from the day, in UTC, that the privacy policy in force of the texts `api` serves is of version 3 or later — 2026-11-27 for the tag's — and before it are "not admitted" with no call and nothing spent; the stub words them on any day. `just dev-check` gets a server of its own on the stub for them.**
- **What the package gives** (`formatv0.Task` of `spec`; `CorrectRequest` of `tutor`):
  - **the key** — `check.key` of each answer slot of the instance that has one, in their order, joined by "; ": a number, a fraction, a time `H:MM`, or the option that is right of a choice, as `CorrectRequest` asks; a predicate has none, and a task of predicates alone sends an empty key. A correct answer is right in every slot, and the words may praise any of them; of a task of one slot, it is that slot's key;
  - **the solution** — the paragraphs of `solution` that have text, as the words of a rung send them; **the techniques** — the titles of `techniques` in the module's catalog, none when the task names none; **the language** — `manifest.languages.explanation`; **the style** — by the age category, as for the words of a rung;
  - **the crop** — of a task AI read, the crop of the sheet the request sends, checked as in `SubmitWork`; of a task a tap checks, none: `CorrectRequest.Crop` is `nil`, and a work sent is malformed.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the rule of the day** — `policyCovers`: on the stub, always; otherwise `legal.Texts.On(now).Current("privacy-policy")` is 3 or more, on the day of the request in UTC, by the texts `serve` gives `learning.WithTexts` — the tag's, or a self-hosted copy's folder. The words are admitted only when `CorrectWords` admits and the policy covers; otherwise `rpc.Once` answers "not admitted";
  - **once** — the right is spent when the words reach the learner, and only then: `attempts.correct_words_given`, a column of the migration `0018`, which a `CHECK` holds true only on an attempt closed `correct` (`coalesce(close_reason = 'correct', false)`: a `NULL` close would pass a bare comparison). The checks of "no more words" are the same on the first transaction and on the attempt held again after the call — closed otherwise or open, a later attempt of the profile, or the words given —, so a request beside another that got the words, or that a new attempt left behind, is "no more words" after its call, and what it spent stays spent;
  - **the answer** — the words, once they passed; no words and no outcome when they failed a check, which the line `request` names in `words_check`; no words with the outcome of a call that gave none — the failure of AI, the own key's refusal or limit —, or of the quota that did not hold it, with when the next family day begins. The record of the key holds the answer without the words, and a repeat gets it `repeated`. The words are kept nowhere;
  - **`dev-check`** — after the run on the provider with the refused key, on a server of its own on the stub of `tutor` (`stubbed` of `scripts/dev.sh`), with no key of platform AI: the engine's task of handwriting, a wide crop that the stub reads as the right value, then `GetCorrectAnswerWords` with it, whose words must end with the question; the log of that server must hold none of them and be clean. No call reaches a provider.

— *Why:*
- the mark when the words are given, not when the call goes: a right spent with no words would give a second request "no more words", though there were no words at all, and a call that failed or words that failed a check give none either; the export's `correct_answer_words_given` names the same fact, so one column serves both. A call beside another may spend twice, and so may a request asked again after words that failed: the quota bounds both on platform AI, as for the words of a rung, and on an own key the learner's own limit does; `app` asks the words once;
- a boolean, not a time: the contract and the export ask whether, and `api` keeps what is needed alone;
- the policy by the texts `api` serves, not by a date in code: the tag's `legal.yaml` names the day, so the day moves with the texts, not with a release of `api`. The threshold is a version of the tag's numbering, 3: a self-hosted copy that keeps its own history of the policy meets it when its own version 3 or later is in force, and whether that version names what goes to AI for these words is its operator's to see to, as every text of its folder is;
- `dev-check` on a server of its own: the check calls the provider with a key it refuses, so that it spends nothing, and on it the reading of handwriting never comes back correct, so no step of the check on that server can reach the words. The stub is the one way to them with no call; its words are the stub's.

— *Rejected:*
- the right spent once the call went, as for "explain differently": that right is to a call, and the words the learner reads stay; here a call with no words would leave the learner with none;
- the key of the slot whose item `tutor` reads the statement of — the slot bound `anywhere`, or else the first `choice` — alone: on a task of several slots the model would praise one answer of several; on a task of one slot both are the same;
- a step of `dev-check` on the provider's server: "not admitted" before 2026-11-27 and the key's refusal from it, with no correct answer to reach the words by;
- the date 2026-11-27 as a constant: a later version of the tag's `legal.yaml` that moves the day would need a release of `api` too.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/hints.go`; `internal/learning/ladder.go`; `internal/learning/learning.go`; `internal/store/attempts.go`; `internal/store/hints.go`; `internal/store/export.go`; `internal/store/migrations/0018_correct_words.sql`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The limit of the checks of a key

**R78. `CheckOwnKey` checks no more than ten keys on an account in any hour, across the edge of a window too: the times of the checks are `accounts.key_checked_at`, of the migration `0019`, held `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and written before the check, so that a check beside it waits and counts it. A check beyond the limit is `resource_exhausted` with `CAUSE_KEY_CHECKS_EXHAUSTED` and `Refusal.next_key_check`, when the next check opens, with no call. Every check that reaches the gate counts, a provider closed with no call too. The times go with the account and are not in the export. On the stub of the dev mode there is no limit.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the count** — `countKeyCheck` of `internal/account/ownkey.go`, after the refusals of the profile, the malformed key and the category, and before `Gate.CheckKey`: in a transaction of `store.WriteUnderAccount`, `store.HoldKeyChecks` holds the row and reads the times, the times older than `KeyCheckWindow`, an hour, are dropped, and with `MaxKeyChecks`, ten, left the request is refused and nothing is written; otherwise the time of this check is added by `store.WriteKeyChecks`, and the transaction commits before the check. The clock is the service's, as for the PIN, read once the row is held, so that a check that waited for another is counted after it; the times are a sliding window, as the PIN's `pin_failed_at`;
  - **what counts** — every check that reaches the gate, whatever it ends in: a provider closed counts, so that trying providers does not get round the limit, and so does a check the client left too little time for (`deadline_exceeded`), which is the client's. The refusals before it count nothing: they check no key; nor do the calls of an attempt with an own key, which tell a key refused by their outcome — Д40;
  - **the refusal** — `resource_exhausted` with the `Refusal` `CAUSE_KEY_CHECKS_EXHAUSTED` and `next_key_check`, when the next check opens: the time of the oldest of the last `MaxKeyChecks` checks of the window, plus `KeyCheckWindow` — once it leaves the hour, a check fits —, the times sorted first, since the clocks of two instances may differ. The line `request` says `resource_exhausted` and has no `key_check`, since no check went;
  - **the stub** — `account.WithoutKeyCheckLimit`, which `serve` gives on `API_TUTOR_STUB=1` alone. `just dev-check` checks two keys a run on the account of `API_DEV_UID`, so five runs fit in an hour, and a sixth meets the limit until the oldest leaves it;
  - **the deletion** — `ClearAccount` clears the times with the account's fields, so they also go when the account's row is deleted;
  - **the export** — no: the times are a service field of the account, of the kind of the PIN's count and lock, which the format of the export, `spec/export/README.md`, leaves out ("A secret or a service field of `api` — … the hash of the PIN and its counter and lock"), as the export holds everything except secrets and service data. They name no key, no provider and no end, and live while they count, an hour.

— *Why:*
- the times rather than a count with the start of a window: no more than ten fit in any hour, across the edge of a window too, as for the PIN; ten times are a small array on the account's row;
- the lock of the account's row, not a count in the query: two checks side by side would both read nine and both check; `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` waits for the other check alone and not for the other writes under the account, which hold the row `FOR KEY SHARE`;
- written before the check, in a transaction of its own: the check takes up to the request's 30 s at a provider, and no transaction is held over a call of AI. A check that fails then is counted all the same, and that is what the limit is for;
- the provider closed counts: else trying the table's providers one after another would go free, and `app` never sends a provider out of the list `ListOwnKeyProviders` gives;
- no limit on the stub: the stub calls no provider, so nothing is tried there, and the account `dev-app` of `just dev-app` lives in the dev database, where `app`'s scenario in Playwright, six keys a run, would meet the limit on its second run within an hour;
- not in the export: a field kept to guard the account, with no content of the owner's, as the PIN's count, which the format leaves out.

— *Rejected:*
- 20 a day and 5 in a quarter of an hour: two windows to keep on the account's row, where one sliding hour, as the PIN's, bounds a run of checks alone;
- an answer `OWN_KEY_CHECK_UNSPECIFIED` with no call: it is "the provider gave no answer", false here, and `app` would offer a repeat that meets the same refusal;
- the limit on the stub too, with `dev-app` clearing its account's times as it starts and every half hour — the runs of `app` within half an hour would still meet it;
- a table of its own, a row a check: ten times of one account need no rows of their own, and they go with the account's fields;
- the times in the export: a service field, as above;
- a daily job that clears the times: what stays between checks is at most ten times, which the next check drops once they are older than an hour, and the account's deletion clears.

— *Consequence:* `internal/account/ownkey.go`; `internal/account/account.go`; `internal/store/keychecks.go`; `internal/store/deletion.go`; `internal/store/migrations/0019_key_checks.sql`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The rung "author" on a deployment and the deployment's list of modules

**R79. `api` moves to `spec` v0.17.12, which lets a deployment name its rung and its list of modules: on a deployment it runs at the rung its operator names — `API_ADMISSION_RUNG=author` with `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is not refused, and unset it is `full` —, and its own key follows the rung, since its providers are those admitted at the rung, with no change of code. The deployment's catalog is the list `API_CATALOG_MODULES`, the IDs of modules separated by commas; unset or empty, the whole pinned index. An ID the pinned index does not hold is a failed start of `serve` and a refusal of `api check`. A module the list leaves out keeps its current version but is offered no more, as a module without one: the catalog does not give it, its connection is refused and connected already it comes without a digest, the engine chooses none of it, and a new attempt, a new lesson run and its skill map are refused `CAUSE_NO_CURRENT_VERSION`; a run or an attempt already open on it goes on to its end on its package.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the tag** — the submodule, and `spec` and `spec/contracts` in `go.mod`, at the tag; it changes neither `contracts/`, `format/`, `types/`, RULES.md nor the devcontainer, so `.devcontainer/` stays as it was;
  - **the rung** — `config.AdmissionFrom` refuses no stage on a deployment; the stub of `tutor` is still refused there, and beside a platform key;
  - **the own key** — `ListOwnKeyProviders` and `CheckOwnKey` already ask `tutor` at the deployment's rung: at `author` they name and check the providers `tutor` admits at `author`;
  - **the list** — `config.catalogModules`, read by `ServeFrom` and by `CatalogFrom` of `api check`: each ID between the commas, trimmed; an empty ID or one named twice is a refusal with the variable's name, and an empty value is no list, as every variable of `api` reads empty as unset. `packages.Set.List` checks the IDs against the index and refuses them all at once by `packages.ErrNotListed`, naming them; `serve` calls it after reading the packages, and `api check` reads the index alone, `packages.ReadIndex`, from `API_PACKAGES_DIR`, by the digest `modules.json` pins, and checks it by `packages.NotInIndex`, only when a list is given: with none, `api check` reads and checks no folder. `List` is called before the set is served, since the handlers read the list with no lock. Both refusals name `API_CATALOG_MODULES`;
  - **what the list narrows** — `packages.Set.OfferedFor`, the current version of a module on the list, in place of `CurrentFor` where a module is offered: `ListCatalog`, `ConnectModule` and the digests of the connected modules (`internal/catalog`), the engine's modules (`engineModules`), the package of a new attempt and of a new run (`packageOf`), the skill map and its modules of the engine (`GetSkillMap`); `Teachers`, the modules that teach an anchor skill, leaves out what the list leaves out;
  - **what it does not narrow** — `CurrentFor` itself: the full check of an open run reads it, so a run on a module the list leaves out goes on; and `Current`, by which `learner-model`, the export and the record of a choice read the current versions, so the module's evidence keeps its graph.

— *Why:*
- a variable and not a file: two IDs, set by the deployment beside the rung, as `infra` passes the other values that are no secret; a file would need a volume of its own;
- the list checked by `api check` too: it is the job before the migrations, so a typo stops the rollout before anything changed, as a fact the admission table does not know does;
- "no current version" for a module the list leaves out, and not a cause of its own: the contract has none, and `app` reads "no current version" already as a module that is shown but cannot be begun, by the contract's `ConnectedModule`, which is what a module the list leaves out is to be;
- `OfferedFor` beside `CurrentFor`, not a filter in `CurrentFor`: the full check of an open run asks `CurrentFor`, and a filter there would end the open runs "not suitable", while they are to go on to their end;
- the IDs refused all at once: the operator fixes the list in one pass.

— *Rejected:*
- a file of the list, as the facts are — above;
- an empty value as an empty catalog: every variable of `api` reads empty as unset, and an empty catalog is no deployment's;
- ending the open runs and attempts on a module the list leaves out — they go on to their end on their package;
- a refusal of the requests of a connected module out of the list beyond the new ones, such as its open views: the open views are given by digest without a token, for any package the image gives;
- a word for a module of the list that the index holds with no current version: it hides as it would without a list, by a withdrawal, which `ci-withdrawals` watches;
- the list typed as `formatv0.ModuleID` in `config`, which reads the environment as strings.

— *Consequence:* `internal/config/admission.go`; `internal/config/catalog.go`; `internal/config/serve.go`; `internal/packages/listed.go`; `internal/packages/read.go`; `internal/packages/set.go`; `internal/catalog/modules.go`; `internal/learning/begin.go`; `internal/learning/choose.go`; `internal/learning/lesson.go`; `internal/learning/progress.go`; `cmd/api/check.go`; `cmd/api/serve.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Which limit of the own key ran out

**R80. The outcome "limit reached" of an own key carries `own_key_limit`, which names the limit that ran out by the reason `tutor` gives, `Outcome.Limit`: `owner_limit` — `LIMIT_CAUSE_LIMIT`, `tier` — `LIMIT_CAUSE_TIER`, `balance` — `LIMIT_CAUSE_BALANCE`, `billing` — `LIMIT_CAUSE_PAYMENT`, and none or a reason `api` does not know — `LIMIT_CAUSE_UNSPECIFIED`. It carries `access_returns` where `tutor` gives when access returns, `Outcome.AccessReturns`, and none where `tutor` gives no time. It holds in every answer whose call may meet the limit — `SubmitWork` with work, `GetRungWords`, `ExplainDifferently` and `GetCorrectAnswerWords` — and in their repeats under the idempotency key; on platform AI the limit is `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT`, with no `own_key_limit` and no time.**
- **What was decided** (`provider/refusal.go` and `tutor.go` of `tutor`; the contract's `cause.proto`):
  - **the function** — `outcomeOf(tutor.Outcome, route)` of `internal/learning` builds the outcome of a call that gave no reading or no words: `joined` of `SubmitWork`, `worded` of `GetRungWords` and `ExplainDifferently`, and `praised` of `GetCorrectAnswerWords`;
  - **the reasons** — a map by the constants of `tutor`, `LimitOwner`, `LimitTier`, `LimitBalance` and `LimitBilling`; a reason it does not hold — of a newer `tutor` — is `LIMIT_CAUSE_UNSPECIFIED`, and so is none;
  - **`own_key_limit` with no reason** — the field is there beside every `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED`, with the cause unspecified when `tutor` names none, which is the contract's "the provider said no more than that a limit ran out";
  - **the time** — `outcomeOf` sets `access_returns` to `Outcome.AccessReturns` as it is, with no reckoning of `api`'s own, and leaves it unset where it is zero, so the contract's "only when the provider says so" stays `tutor`'s reading of the provider;
  - **the repeat** — every answer kept under the key is the protobuf the first answer wrote, with its outcome: `submissions.answer` for `SubmitWork`, and the key's record for the words of a rung, "explain differently" and the words after a correct answer. So `own_key_limit` comes back as it went, with no change of the store, and a repeat names the same time, even once it has passed: the outcome is of that call, and a new call asks the provider again;
  - **platform AI** — keeps `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT` with no time and stays closed until the operator opens it: a time `tutor` gives on the platform's key does not open it;
  - **the log** — the request's line names `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED` by `outcome_cause`, and not which limit: the limit is of the owner's account at the provider, which the operator has nothing to do with.

— *Why:*
- the reason of `tutor`, not `api`'s own reading of the provider's refusal: the adapters of `tutor` read it, and `api` sees the outcome alone;
- the field with no reason too: `app` reads one shape beside every limit of the own key, and "not named" is a value of the contract;
- an unknown reason as unspecified, not as a failure of AI: the limit still ran out at the provider, and the owner still has it to lift.

— *Rejected:*
- a time of `access_returns` of `api`'s own: it would promise what the provider did not say;
- no `own_key_limit` when `tutor` names no reason — two shapes of one outcome for `app`;
- which limit in the request's line of the log — above.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/attempt.go`; `internal/learning/hints.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The workbook page in code

**R81. A task on a workbook begins, enters the engine's pool and is checked by its kind of call: the beginning and the pool ask the gate of `Reads(CallWorkbook)` for a workbook and of `Reads(CallSheet)` for a sheet with one slot `anywhere`, and `SubmitWork` with `WorkbookWork` reads the crops of the filled boxes by `tutor`'s `ReadWorkbook`, the verdict of each box and each item by `module-kit`, as on a sheet. On the models a workbook page is admitted by the entry of the author's word in `tutor`'s admission table, at the rung `author` alone, until 2027-01-17; at `full` it is still "not admitted". On the stub of the dev mode it is admitted as the gate admits it, and read box by box by the shape of each crop.**
- **What was decided** (`workbook.go` and `price.go` of `tutor`; the contract's `attempt.proto`; the module format's workbook):
  - **the kind of call** of a task is `callOf` of `internal/learning`, by its canvas: `CallSheet` for a handwriting sheet with one answer slot `anywhere` and the others `choice`, `CallWorkbook` for a workbook with a box at least, every answer slot of which is a box, as the format has it; none for a task a tap checks, a zone or the whole sheet. The rule `admitted` of the beginning, the pool and the lessons asks the gate's `Reads(call, category, provider)`, through a port of `internal/learning`, `admitter`, the gate's by default; "AI not admitted" of the pool is neither kind admitted, and a workbook left out by its admission alone narrows the pool, as a sheet does;
  - **the form of `WorkbookWork`**: each `BoxCrop` names by `answer_slot` an item of the task and a slot of it bound `box`, from 1 — the place of the box among the item's boxes, since a workbook's every slot is one —, no box twice, no more than 20 boxes, and each crop a PNG whole and no larger than 512 px a side, as `tutor` reads it; the boxes go in the order sent, which `tutor` puts in the task's. No box at all is a page left blank and no error: `tutor` reads its every box blank, "unreadable". Anything else, the work of a sheet on a workbook and of a page on a sheet among it, is `invalid_argument`, before the record "going";
  - **the reading** is a port beside `sheetReader`, `workbookReader`, the gate's `ReadWorkbook` by default, and the stub's by `learning.WithTutor`; it takes the instance and the own key for the stub, as `sheetReader` does, and the gate's adapter drops both. What it gives has the shape of a sheet's reading, and goes the same way: `observation.Join` of the two readers — in the mode `single` one reader reads, and its one observation over every box gets the verdict with no `Join`, as a sheet's does —, `observation.Verdicts`, the observation kept, the outcome by `outcomeOf`;
  - **the quota**: the reading reserves `tutor`'s expected price of a page, `Price(CallWorkbook)`, or, where `tutor` expects none, the value of `internal/quota`, 14,000 microdollars, for each filled box; `reserveFor` takes the number of crops. A page left blank goes to no model, and reserves and spends nothing;
  - **the words**: a rung's words and those after a correct answer take no work of a workbook page — with it `invalid_argument` —, and without it go as for a task a tap checks, by the hint without the work; the rung is the first slot "not yet" by the order of items and slots, as on a sheet;
  - **the kinds of call** `api` makes, of which an own key is open when one is admitted, hold `CallWorkbook` too.

— *Why:*
- the admission by the kind of call, not by the form: `tutor` admits each kind by the prompt an entry measured, and the reading of a sheet admits nothing of a workbook's;
- the order of the boxes free: `tutor` reads them in the task's order whatever they come in, and a refusal would make app's order a fault of the learner's submission;
- no reserve for a blank page: it calls no model, and `cost` would spend the reserve of a call with no record of usage;
- the words without the work of a page: their request carries one crop of a sheet (`WordsRequest.Crop`), and the box of the first mistake on a page is no part of the `tutor` that `go.mod` pins.

— *Rejected:*
- the work of a page in the words — above; Д42;
- a refusal of boxes out of the task's order — above.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/task.go`; `internal/learning/attempt.go`; `internal/learning/begin.go`; `internal/learning/quota.go`; `internal/quota/quota.go`; `internal/admission/admission.go`; `internal/tutorstub/tutorstub.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The essay in code

**R82. A task with a rubric begins, enters the engine's pool and is submitted where the gate admits both its kinds of call, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric`: `SubmitEssay` reads the crop of the sheet by `tutor`'s `ReadEssay`, judges the reading by `module-kit` (`observation.Essay`), assesses the text read by `AssessEssay` and gives the feedback once it passes the leak check and `words.Feedback`, in three parts by `rpc.Across`. A submission is a row of `submissions` with how it ended, the marks and the source of the words, migration `0020`, and never the text, the evidence or the words. On the models both calls are admitted by the entries of the author's word in `tutor`'s admission table, at the rung `author` alone, until 2027-01-17; at `full` a task with a rubric is still "not admitted". On the stub of the dev mode it is admitted as the gate admits it, and read and assessed by the stub's ports.**
- **What was decided** (`essay.go`, `rubric.go` and `price.go` of `tutor`; `observation.Essay` of `module-kit`; the contract's `attempt.proto`; the module format's rubric):
  - **the admission**: a task with a rubric is one `tutor` reads as an essay — a handwriting sheet whose one answer slot is bound `sheet` and checked by a rubric, `essayRead` of `internal/learning` — and the rule `admitted` of the beginning, the pool and the lessons asks the gate of `Reads(CallEssay, …)` and `Reads(CallRubric, …)` both: a submission makes both calls, and a task one of them could not make would end every submission "unchecked". The start of such a task asks the quota for a typical task with a rubric, `quota.TypicalRubric`, $0.06, not one with handwriting; the kinds of call `api` makes, of which an own key is open when one is admitted, hold the two too;
  - **the form**: `crop` is a PNG of the sheet, whole and no larger than 4096 px a side, as for a sheet (`cropTaken`). An attempt of a task with a verdict, or of a task with a rubric that `tutor` does not read as an essay, is `invalid_argument` on `attempt_id`, and a crop not taken on `crop`, before the record "going"; less than 6 s to the request's deadline is `deadline_exceeded`, as for a sheet;
  - **the reserve**: one charge of the reading with the assessment — `tutor`'s expected prices, `Price(CallEssay)` and `Price(CallRubric)`, or else the values of `internal/quota`, 15,000 microdollars each —, taken whole or refused whole, in the first transaction, then kept as two parts, each settled by what its call cost. An essay that was not read — "unreadable", "unchecked" or a reading refused — gives the assessment's part back; a refusal of the quota calls nothing;
  - **the reading**: a port `essayReader`, the gate's `ReadEssay` by default, of the task's open view and the crop; the crop goes no further. Of what it gives, `observation.Essay` says how the submission goes on: the text to assess; "unreadable" with the reason `blank` or `illegible` of the reading; or "unchecked", with the outcome of a reading that gave nothing by `outcomeOf`, or `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE` for an observation that is not valid;
  - **the assessment**: a port `rubricAssessor`, the gate's `AssessEssay` by default, of the text read, the statement as the topic, the criteria of the rubric in their order, the style of the learner's category and the module's language of explanations — and nothing of the learner. A criterion with no mark of `tutor`'s, or past the end of what it gave, is "not assessed"; an assessment that assessed none is "unchecked" with no outcome, and one that gave nothing is "unchecked" with its outcome. The assessment goes only while at least 3 s are left before the end of the calls' time — the request's deadline minus 3 s for the write —; with less it is not called, its part is given back, and the essay is "unchecked" as a failure of AI;
  - **the words**: the feedback goes to the device once it is not empty, holds none of the task's leak phrases (`leak.For`) and passes `words.Feedback` against the text read, in the module's language of explanations; otherwise the answer has none, the source is "no feedback", and `app` shows its phrase. The request's line of the log names the check that turned the words down, by the closed set the log of the hints uses — `empty`, `leak` or `form` —, not the part of the rule;
  - **the storage** — migration `0020`, three columns of `submissions`: `essay_end` — `feedback`, `unreadable` or `unchecked` —, `marks`, the mark of each criterion by its number — `met`, `not_met` or `not_assessed` —, and `words_source` — `model` or `no_feedback` —, the last two beside feedback alone, and no observation beside an essay, by checks of the table. `answer` is the answer without the text read and the feedback, which is what a repeat under the key reads, with `repeated`. The reading of the last submission, of the answers of the hints and of the evidence owed skip a row of an essay. The open attempt gives `essay_submissions`, the time and the end of each; progress gives them on `AttemptRecord` with the marks once the attempt is closed; the export carries them as `RubricSubmission`, marks and source; the deletion of the profile takes them with its attempts;
  - **two devices**: an attempt closed during the calls is no refusal: the answer comes from the request's memory and names the close in `closed`, nothing is recorded, and the key's record holds that answer without the text and the words;
  - **the stub**: `learning.WithTutor` gives the stub as `essayReader` and `rubricAssessor`, and its admitter answers as the gate does.

— *Why:*
- both calls at the admission: a task begun where only the reading is admitted would spend a reading on every submission and end it "unchecked";
- one charge for the two calls: the reserve of the reading is taken together with the assessment, so that an essay is not read and then left unassessed by a quota that ran out between the calls;
- no assessment too late: a call cut short by the deadline comes back with no record of usage, and `cost` would spend its whole reserve for nothing;
- the stored answer without the text and the words: neither is kept on the server, in the cache of idempotency too, and a repeat reads what the server kept;
- the closed set of the log: the part of the rule `words.Feedback` broke is a breach of `tutor`'s, which the log of the hints does not name either, and a new set of values for one call would make the log's words differ by the call.

— *Rejected:*
- two reserves, one for each call: the assessment could be refused after the reading was spent — above;
- the part of the rule in the log — above;
- a submission recorded on an attempt closed during the calls: nothing is recorded then;
- a table of its own for the submissions of an essay: the attempt's submissions are numbered, held and deleted together, and the key's record names one by `submission_id`.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/essay.go`; `internal/learning/begin.go`; `internal/learning/quota.go`; `internal/learning/progress.go`; `internal/admission/admission.go`; `internal/store/attempts.go`; `internal/store/export.go`; `internal/store/migrations/0020_essays.sql`; `internal/tutorstub/tutorstub.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## "Done" and the engine's tasks with a rubric in code

**R83. "Done" closes an attempt with a rubric once it has a submission, however that ended, in one transaction with no evidence of its own, and answers with the task solved — the criteria of its rubric, without the marks, and its solution, if it has one — and with what comes next: in a lesson the segment after its own, which "done" ends, and outside a lesson the engine's choice. A repeat on an attempt closed "done" gets the same answer by the state of the attempt. The engine's choice counts an essay's two calls admitted together as AI that reads the work, and asks the quota for the larger of the typical task with handwriting and the typical task with a rubric.**
- **What was decided** (`Done`, `SolvedTask` and `Next` of the contract's `attempt.proto`):
  - **"Done"** (`internal/learning/attempt.go`): the profile and `own_key_provider` are checked as at every request whose answer may give an engine's choice, and the attempt is held as every request holds it. A task with a verdict is `invalid_argument` on `attempt_id`; an attempt with no row in `submissions`, of any end, is `CAUSE_NO_SUBMISSION` (`store.Submitted`). Otherwise the attempt is closed "done" by the path every close takes, which settles the evidence the profile's closed attempts owe: an attempt with a rubric owes none (О-17). The answer is `SolvedTask` — the texts of the criteria in their order, no keys, and the solution with the format's version — and `next` by the path of a correct answer, on the route of `own_key_provider`;
  - **the repeat**: the request carries no key, and an attempt closed "done" is answered again from what does not change — the task solved of the package and the instance the attempt went on, and in `next` the segment after its own or the choice the close gave, read by the attempt (`after_attempt_id`), with no call of the engine. A close that gave no choice — the engine could not make one — repeats with no `next`, as its first answer had none, and so does a repeat of `SubmitWork`. On a package the index no longer knows it is `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN`, as for a request on an open attempt; an attempt closed otherwise is `CAUSE_ATTEMPT_CLOSED` with its reason;
  - **the task solved**: `solved` gives `criteria` on a task with a rubric, by the same order in which the marks are kept (`criteriaOf`); `ShowSolution` and `SubmitWork`, which a task with a rubric does not take, give it on no task;
  - **the lesson**: the segment of a task with a rubric ends by "done", which the lesson counts among the ends of a segment, and its run is passed once every task segment has ended;
  - **the engine** (`internal/learning/choose.go`, `begin.go`): the pool and the lessons take a task with a rubric where both its calls are admitted. AI that reads the work, `readsAdmitted`, is the call of a sheet, of a workbook page, or the two of an essay together, so a pool where only an essay's calls are admitted is not limited to the tasks a tap checks; a task with a rubric that `tutor` reads as an essay and that its admission alone left out narrows the pool, so "nothing to give" says "not admitted" for it too (`forAI`). On platform AI the choice asks the family day for room for the larger of `quota.TypicalHandwriting` and `quota.TypicalRubric`, both $0.06. Attempts closed "done" go to the engine as other closed attempts do;
  - **at the rung `full`** no entry admits an essay's calls, so there a task with a rubric neither begins nor reaches the engine; at `author`, and on the stub as the gate admits it, it does.

— *Why:*
- a submission of any end: "Done" after one however it ended is the way out of an essay that cannot be read or whose quota has run out;
- the path of every close: the evidence other closed attempts owe is written in the transaction of a close, and one path cannot miss it;
- the repeat by the state: the contract gives "Done" no key, and everything of the answer is in the package, the instance and the record of the choice, which do not change;
- the larger typical task: the engine gives no task that its start refuses, and a start asks the typical task of its own kind.

— *Rejected:*
- a record of the answer of "Done": the contract carries no key, and the answer read again is the same;
- a room of the quota for each kind of task in the choice: both are $0.06, and `begins` would take two flags where one says the same;
- the room of a task with handwriting alone: a typical task with a rubric that costs more would let the engine give a task its start refuses.

— *Consequence:* `internal/learning/attempt.go`; `internal/learning/task.go`; `internal/learning/begin.go`; `internal/learning/choose.go`; `internal/learning/lesson.go`; `internal/store/attempts.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The move to spec v0.18.3

**R84. `api` moves to `spec` v0.18.3. The submodule and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are at the tag, and the export moves with them: `store.ExportProfile` names a profile's stored works without their bytes, and `app` reads each by `GetWork`, which the learner may ask, with `ListWorks` and `DeleteWork`, while the profile waits for a consent. The devcontainer and `.claude/settings.json` are the template's, with this repository's own differences.**
- **What was decided:**
  - **the export** — `store.ExportProfile` reads `works` from `attempt_id`, `kind`, `stored_at` and `size_bytes`, never from `work`, and the export's version is the integer of `export/VERSION` of the tag, `ExportFormat`, the `export_format_version` of `ExportProfile` and `ExportAccount`. The IDs of profiles and attempts are UUIDs in one case, so the export's rule `export.case`, that no two IDs of its files are one once letter case is ignored, holds with no change;
  - **the admission table** — `tutor` reads it from the Go module `spec` the build selects, so the tag sets the version of the table that `api check` and the startup line name; the facts of `scripts/dev.sh`, of the dev projects and of the tests declare what the table asks of platform AI of an adult;
  - **the stored works while a consent waits** — the learner's `ListWorks` and `GetWork`, and `DeleteWork`, go while the profile waits for its consent or its age, checked for the profile of the account alone, as the learner's `ExportProfile` goes; `PutWork` and switching the storage on are still refused;
  - **the fuzz test** of the form of a stored work is `FuzzWorkExported` of `internal/learning`, on the file of a work `app` writes: the export no longer unpacks one;
  - **the devcontainer** — the template's draft of the kind `go` at the tag, built on a clone of `spec` in a temporary folder, with the differences that follow from what `api` does: nss_wrapper in the stage `toolchain` for the tests of the database, the volume of the dev projects' configuration and the volume of the custom token `app` signs in by; `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` loads `gopls-lsp` from this container's own path. `.claude/settings.json` is the draft's, byte for byte, without the marketplace. The container is rebuilt after the merge.

— *Why:*
- the export with the move: the tag carries no package of the export's version before, and `ci-lint` holds the Go modules at the commit of the submodule, so neither moves without the other;
- the learner's works while a consent waits: `app` builds the export by them, so an export `ExportProfile` gives would otherwise name works `app` cannot read.

— *Rejected:*
- the submodule at the tag and the Go modules at the tag before — `ci-lint` refuses it.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `spec`; `internal/store/export.go`; `internal/export/export.go`; `internal/learning/works.go`; `internal/learning/works_fuzz_test.go`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `.devcontainer/`; `.claude/settings.json`; `.claude/rules/go.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The work plan's files

**R85. The work plan of `api` takes the layout and the language of the template of `spec`: RUN.md holds its rules, in English, and table 1; tables 2, 3 and 4 are `run/EPICS.md`, `run/QUESTIONS.md` and `run/DEBT.md`; the open tasks outside an epic are bodies of `run/TASKS.md`; rule 7 names the executor's contract of spec's orchestrator. The differences of `api`'s rules from the template's stay, the way of tasks outside an epic first: such a task is also a row of table 1, and between epics its body goes to the archive of the epic that comes next.**
- **RUN.md** — the template's RUN.md for `api`, the draft of `just new-repo-draft` of the kind `go` read beside it: the opening with the four files of `run/` and the link to `docs/plan/`; Rules 1–12 with their numbers; Epics; table 1. Differences from the template:
  - rule 9 — a task outside an epic's plan is a row of table 1 and a body of `run/TASKS.md`; done, it goes to the current epic's archive, and between epics to the archive of the epic that comes next, not to the last done epic's archive;
  - rule 6 — names the rules of the parts and not the list of what `docs-check-repo` checks, which CLAUDE.md holds;
  - rule 12 — without "once SonarCloud, CodeQL and Scorecard run here": they do; Epics — the row of the measurement of an epic's sessions goes to the table of the sessions' tokens (measurements 4.2);
  - citations — a decision of another repository bears that repository's name after its number, and one of this log its number alone.
- **`run/`** — the template's openings, with lines of `api`'s own: `run/QUESTIONS.md` names a run of spec's orchestrator that waits on the author as a source of a row; `run/TASKS.md` says that each of its tasks is also a row of table 1. Table 4 is terse: a row holds what it needs to act on, and a closed part leaves it.
- **Links** — CLAUDE.md names `run/` and the language, and so does `english_justification` of `.bestpractices.json`.
- **Budgets** — RUN.md, the files of `run/` and CLAUDE.md keep within the block `repository` of `spec`, so `docs-budgets.json` gives them no limit of its own.

— *Why:* every task reads RUN.md whole, and tables 2–4 are read by the planning of an epic or a task that cites a row; the orchestrator's executor needs rule 7; table 1 is the one place a task's status is, so a task between epics needs its row, and its body is archived where the plan goes on.

— *Rejected:* the template's rule 9 for tasks between epics — table 1 would no longer hold every task's status; the tasks outside an epic without rows in table 1 — their statuses would be nowhere; translating the archives — they stay in Russian, for the author.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/EPICS.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `run/TASKS.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `docs/plan/`.

## The move to spec v0.18.4

**R86. `api` moves to `spec` v0.18.4. The submodule and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are at the tag. `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` is the template's of three stages: `toolchain-core` — Node, just, Go and buf, what the release and the checks without a test need —, `toolchain` over it with nss_wrapper alone, from a source of the snapshot of the Ubuntu archive alone, and `devcontainer`. Every call of `.github` is at one release: the release builds the core, "Go / lint", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses" and "Documents" run in it, "Go / test" and "Modules" in `toolchain`, and "Image" and "Withdrawals" in the core.**
- **The devcontainer** — the template's draft of the kind `go` at the tag, built on a clone of `spec` in a temporary folder: the heading of three stages, `toolchain-core` where `toolchain` began, and `toolchain` over it. `devcontainer.json` and `host-network.sh` differ from the draft by the two volumes of the dev run alone. The container is rebuilt after the merge.
- **What each stage keeps** — everything the release and the checks of the core run is in the core — `release-files` is `go build -trimpath`, the licenses by `go list` and the module cache, and `sha256sum`; `ci-lint`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` run `gofmt`, `go vet`, `go run` of their pinned tools and `just -f spec/justfile licenses-allowed`, on Node —, and `toolchain` adds what the tests need, nss_wrapper.

— *Why:* the release of `api` built the whole `toolchain`, with the apt of nss_wrapper, for a version and files that need none of it.

— *Rejected:*
- the Dockerfile of one stage `toolchain`: `.github`'s workflows and `toolchain.sh` would build `toolchain` with a notice wherever they ask for the core, and the copy would drift from the template with no decision that holds it.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`; `.github/workflows/scorecard.yml`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `.github/workflows/withdrawals.yml`; `.github/toolchain.sh`; `.github/image.sh`; `.github/modules.sh`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `justfile`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`.

## The move to spec v0.18.6

**R87. `api` moves to `spec` v0.18.6. The submodule and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are at the tag. `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` takes the template's `"shutdownAction": "none"`, so closing the window of VS Code leaves the container running under an executor of the orchestrator, and `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` the template's comments on gopls and on what `toolchain-core` holds; its differences from the template stay — nss_wrapper in `toolchain` and the two volumes of the dev run.**
- **The devcontainer** — the template's draft of the kind `go` at the tag, built on a clone of `spec` in a temporary folder, differs from `.devcontainer/` by `shutdownAction`, the comment on gopls and that of the stage `toolchain`, and by this repository's own differences, which stay. `shutdownAction` and the comment on gopls are taken; in place of the comment of `toolchain`, `api`'s own comment there, which names nss_wrapper, takes the template's rule that what the release and the `ci-` recipes without a test call belongs in `toolchain-core`. The container is rebuilt after the merge.
- **The hook of a session**, `spec/tools/session/`, runs from the submodule by `.claude/settings.json`, so a move to a tag brings its version with no change here.

— *Why:* without `shutdownAction` a closed window stops the container under a running executor, and its attempt is lost.

— *Rejected:* the submodule at v0.18.6 without the change of `devcontainer.json` — the copy would drift from the template with no decision that holds it, and closing a window would stop an executor's run; the Dockerfile without the template's comment — the same drift, for a comment, though any change of the Dockerfile cools the caches keyed by its hash once more.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`.

## The mode of reading handwriting

**R88. The deployment chooses how `tutor` reads the handwriting of a sheet and of a workbook page by `API_READING_MODE`: `pair`, the default when unset or empty, the two readers of the entry chosen, whose observations `module-kit` joins; or `single`, Claude Haiku 5.5 at `medium` alone, whose one observation is the reading. `api` passes the mode to `tutor`'s `Config.Reading`. In `single` a reading of exactly one observation that `module-kit` takes (`observation.Validate`) gets its verdict by `observation.Verdicts` with no `Join`, and the submission keeps it as it keeps a joined one; any other answer is `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`. Either mode holds at either rung of `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`. Another value stops `serve` at its start and `api check`. The stub of the dev mode reads by as many readers as the mode has.**
- **Why** — the price of the pair's check is mostly its strong reader's (measurements 2.1), and Haiku at `medium` is the pair's second reader, whose call is a small part of it.
- **Every rung.** In `single`, `tutor` asks the admission of the pair's entry whose reader is Haiku, so at a rung where the table does not admit that entry a reading is "not admitted", `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`, by the gate before the attempt — never a refusal of the configuration.
- **The code.** `config.Admission.Reading`, `ReadingPair` or `ReadingSingle`; `admission.Load` gives it to `tutor.New`, and `Gate.Reading` to `learning`, whose `joined` takes one observation or two by it, and to `tutorstub`, which gives one observation and one row of usage at the whole expected price in `single`. The lines `startup` and `admission` carry `reading_mode`. The price and the reserve of a reading, of a sheet and of a workbook page, are `tutor`'s by the mode, `Price(CallSheet)` or `Price(CallWorkbook)` of the one reader in `single`; `quota.TypicalHandwriting` before a task stays the pair's: an upper bound for one reader, which settles at the price spent, and most of the typical task is the words.
- **`tutor`'s mode** — the one reader is found in the entry chosen by its provider, ID and effort; `ChooseCall`, `Price` and `CheckKey` go by the mode, and an entry with no such reader is `Failed`; the reading of an essay is one reader in both modes.

— *Rejected:* the one observation joined with itself by `Join` — no pair read it, and `Verdicts` takes a valid observation of one reader as it is; the mode by the rung or the stage — the rung says which models are admitted, not how many read.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `internal/config/`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/tutorstub/`; `internal/logging/`; `internal/quota/`; `cmd/api/`; `docs/build.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The adult's correction of a module's level

**R89. `api` serves the adult's correction of a module's level, `SetModuleLevel` of `ProgressService`: at every profile of the account, with `can_manage`, the profile's consent and age as at a learning request, the band named next to the band of the level by the order of the bands of the current manifest — the one before, the one after, the lowest while the level stands below it — is appended to the log of evidence as `LevelCorrection` of `learner-model`, which sets the level in that band in one step; the level's own band appends nothing and answers as the level is, and any other is `CAUSE_BAND_NOT_NEXT`. The skill map gives the band of the level and when an adult last set it, to every learner; the progress, on its first page, the last correction of each module of the log. A correction is a row of the log of no attempt under an ID of its own, folded whatever its package, which goes into the export and is deleted with the log.**
- **`learner-model`** — a module's level starts a band below the band of the learner's grade, an adult's correction is evidence of kind 3, `LevelCorrection`, and three failures in a row past the trial series lower the level by a band; `ModuleReadout.LevelBand` gives the band of the level. Its `.proto` files the export carries are laid again by `just learner-model-proto`.
- **The band of the level** is `LevelBand` of `learner-model`'s reading of the module: the band whose middle is nearest the level, the highest above it, none more than half a band below the lowest band, where a learner of its grades starts. `api` checks the band named against it, and `learner-model` checks nothing of the kind.
- **The log** (migration `0021`): `evidence.attempt_id` may be null, `correction_id` is new, and a row has exactly one of them. The package of a correction is that of the module's current version at the time; the time is the request's, in whole seconds, as `learner-model` keeps it. The filter of `wrong_key` cuts off the evidence of attempts alone: a wrong key makes no level an adult set wrong.
- **The export.** An entry of the log of a correction names it by `correction_id`, with no `attempt_id`, its package in `package_digest` — that of the module's current version when the adult made it, which no `wrong_key` version filters out, as a correction is folded whatever its package —, and the evidence tells it apart, `level_set_by_adult`.
- **The code.** `store.AppendCorrection` and `store.Corrections`, of one module or of all; `model.Correct`, which appends to the settled state and the log and writes the cache as `Settle` does, and `model.Levels` and `model.LevelsOf`, the last correction of each module that decodes, by a query of the skill map's module or of the journal the progress has read; `SetModuleLevel`, `nextBand` and `levelOf` of `internal/learning`, and `engineOf`, which the engine's choice, the skill map and the correction share; the rule `toManage` of the method in `internal/rpc`. The answer of a request that names the level's own band gives the time of the last correction, whatever band it set, as the contract has it: "by the one before it when this one named the band of the level already".

— *Rejected:* the correction as an attempt of its own, which would put a fact of an adult among the learner's attempts and their progress; a step, "easier" or "harder", in place of the band, which the contract rejected for its repeat; a correction on a version withdrawn for a wrong key left out of the fold, which would drop a level an adult set for a reason that is not the adult's; a band further than one off taken as the nearest next, which would move a level that moved meanwhile twice.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `spec`; `internal/store/`; `internal/store/migrations/0021_level_corrections.sql`; `internal/model/`; `internal/learning/`; `internal/rpc/`; `internal/export/`; `justfile`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The workbook, the essay and the rubric by the author's word

**R90. `api` takes `tutor` v0.1.46, whose list admits `CallWorkbook`, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric` by the author's word, without eval, until 2027-01-17, at the rung `author` alone: at `author` a workbook page and a task with a rubric begin, enter the engine's pool and the lessons, and are read and assessed on the models where the facts admit a sheet, with `tutor`'s expected prices; at `full` none of the three is admitted, so they stay "not admitted". The word suspends `spec`'s rule that a model is admitted only after an eval, on `novatrail` alone and only while the author alone learns there; the bound is the deployment's and the operator's, and `api` checks none of it. `api`'s code does not change but the stub's: `Stub.Reads` answers as the gate does, and admits no kind of call itself.**
- **What the release brings:** three entries of `admission.Admitted` — the workbook by the sheet's pair on its prompt of version 3, Haiku 5.5 · `medium` alone in the reading mode `single`; the essay's reading by Haiku 5.5 · `medium`; the rubric by Haiku 5.5 · `medium` —, each with `Evidence.Word` and `Until` in place of a report, served only where the least rung is `author`; and their expected tokens in `price.go`.
- **What `api` gets** — no change of code: the beginning, the pool and the lessons ask `Reads(CallWorkbook)`, and `Reads(CallEssay)` with `Reads(CallRubric)`, as for any workbook or task with a rubric, and the reserve of the quota takes `Price(…)` where `tutor` has one. At `author` with the facts of a sheet, and on an adult's own key of Anthropic, the three are admitted wherever a sheet is; a child's cells would admit them only with the facts for minors, as a sheet, which a deployment under the word does not declare, below. A workbook page is priced by the pair as a page, not a box, since `quota.Reserve` takes `tutor`'s price whole: a run of `dev-check` spends 63,267 micro-dollars, not 174,210, and `dev_check` holds some 150 a day (measurements 12). The values of `internal/quota` for the three stay, for a route `tutor` prices none on.
- **The stub.** `Stub.Reads` asks the gate for the call itself, so the stub follows `tutor` at either rung, `just dev-app`'s child's workbook page by `child_facts` too.
- **The bound of the word.** It holds only while the author alone learns: the first code of `api invite` to another adult or the first child ends it before 2027-01-17, and before either each entry of the word passes an eval on Haiku 5.5 or leaves the list, or the author gives a new word. It holds on `novatrail` alone: a self-hosted copy that keeps the rung `author` while others than its operator learn names an `api` before v0.1.60, the first with `tutor` v0.1.46, or the rung `full`; after 2027-01-17 every deployment names only an `api` whose `tutor` admits each entry by an eval, or admits none. A deployment that declares facts for any cell under 18 names the rung `full`, so the word's entries never read a child's work. All of it binds the deployment, `infra` and the operator, not `api`'s code: neither `api invite` nor `api check` checks it.
- **Not done here** — `api` holds no clock over `Evidence.Until`: `tutor`'s list test fails from 2027-01-18, and a release of `tutor` that admits by a run or drops the entries is a move of its own (Д24).

— *Why:* the author's word: on `novatrail` the workbook and the essays are read and assessed by AI while the author alone learns. The word rests on no other learner being expected, and the rung `author` says nothing of another learner's handwriting, so it ends with the first one and stays off a child's work. The stub's own admission would differ from `tutor`'s once the word lapses or an entry leaves, and admission is `tutor`'s, which the stub follows as the gate does.

— *Rejected:* `api` refusing the entries after `Evidence.Until` by its clock — a deployment would close its calls on a date with no release and no warning, which is why `tutor` keeps the lapse out of its choice too; the lapse is `tutor`'s CI's. A check in `api invite` for the entries of the word, or in `api check` refusing the rung `author` beside facts for a cell under 18 — a gate that a run of eval on `tutor`'s side could not lift without a release of `api`, for a bound the operator keeps by the order of the rollout as a condition of the deployment. The stub's own admission kept beside the gate's — two rules for one answer.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `internal/tutorstub/`; `internal/admission/`; `internal/learning/`; `scripts/dev.sh`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

## The move to spec v0.18.14

**R91. `api` moves to `spec` v0.18.14. The submodule and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are at the tag. RUN.md's rule of language takes the template's clause: commit messages and a pull request's title and description in English and terse, never a copy of the report. The Go code, `.devcontainer/`, `.claude/`, CLAUDE.md but its tag and the layout of the plan do not change.**

The refusals are `api`'s own — the rung's words for a count no longer current by `named`, the correct answer's words by `praisable`, of `internal/learning/hints.go` —, and a submission "unreadable" or "unchecked" leaves the count, so the code stays. О-26 asks whether a child who solves with the panel closed has passed the ladder; its recommended option would have `api` give the third rung's words from the fourth wrong answer, which it refuses `CAUSE_NO_MORE_WORDS` (Д46).

— *Why:* the rules and the template of the tag describe a repository built by the template, and the language of commits and pull requests is the one the shared rules say.

— *Rejected:* the submodule at v0.18.14 without the plan's clause — the plan would drift from the template with no decision that holds it, and the executor's contract already binds the language of pull requests.
